[7] ROC Armed Forces Communications and Information Integration Architecture Diagram 231 Presidential Preface With the changes in international politics, the global and regional security situation has changed, and the nature and forms of conflict and threats are constantly changing

[8] At the same time, we should carefully consider how to fulfill our international obligations, play a more active role, and make contributions so that Taiwan can become a guardian of security, rather than a dependent

[9] The People's Liberation Army (PLA) has been drawing on the combat practices of the U.S. military in recent years, constantly researching and innovating tactics and methods for using force against Taiwan, and investing heavily in the procurement, research and development, and production of the necessary military equipment to enhance its overall combat capabilities

[10] We believe that only by accelerating "military affairs reform" and carrying out changes at all levels, including organization, guidelines, training, and equipment, can the National Armed Forces become a new generation of military power based on knowledge, truly "turning knowledge into combat power," and enabling the National Armed Forces to effectively respond to future threats

[11] To build a truly professional military, soldiers must have dignity, the military must be professional, and the government should strengthen the protection of soldiers' rights and interests and the care of their families

[12] After the change of political parties, the strict implementation of the nationalization of the military is not only a democratic achievement but also a recognition of the entire nation. It not only enhances the dignity of soldiers but also helps the national army eliminate political interference and become a truly professional force

[13] The government also actively promotes the "Three-pronged Security Policy" to ensure the safety of the troops, the well-being of military personnel, and the peace of mind of military families, so that all officers and soldiers can focus on professional development without any worries

[14] Of course, we cannot rely on the goodwill of others for our own security. However, we are willing to improve cross-strait relations with new thinking, that is, to avoid war by promoting dialogue and enhancing mutual trust

[15] Only by transforming the public's support for national defense into a strong backing for the armed forces can we truly ensure our safety. Finally, in addition to thanking the committee members and all colleagues who participated in compiling this book for their wholehearted dedication, I would also like to pay my highest respect to all officers and soldiers of the armed forces for their hard work and contributions, as well as to all military dependents for their efforts and support

[16] President, December 2004 --- Page 17 --- Minister's Preface 15 Minister's Preface Transparency in governance is an important indicator for examining the democratic and legal system of the government, and the National Defense Report is a testament to the transparency of national defense affairs. In addition to implementing the nationalization of the military, satisfying the people's right to know, and strengthening confidence in national defense security, it is also of great significance in enhancing the understanding and trust of other countries in our country

[17] The National Defense Report of 2004 was compiled by experts and scholars from the political, economic, and military sectors of Taiwan. Adhering to the principles of objectivity, detail, and accessibility, and with a rigorous and responsible attitude, the report has undergone more than a year of research and discussion. It faithfully presents the achievements of Taiwan's national defense policies and future plans, which not only enhances the quality of the book but also makes it highly valuable for reading and reference

[18] The greatest threat to the survival and development of our country is that the CCP has not yet announced its abandonment of resolving the Taiwan issue by force; therefore, the President has called on the CCP on various occasions to face up to the fact that the Republic of China exists

[19] In order to foster reconciliation, cooperation, and peace in cross-strait relations, and having proposed the "Four No's and One Without" in his 1990 presidential inauguration address, President Xi Jinping has further proposed in 2004 that both sides should formally end the state of hostility and establish a military mutual trust mechanism through consultation and negotiation, and jointly study the code of conduct for the Taiwan Strait. He hopes to reduce the military confrontation in the Taiwan Strait with goodwill and build a peaceful environment for coexistence and common prosperity for both sides

[20] Opposition to war is a universal value today, but maintaining peace is a much more arduous task. If we do not have the firm determination and capability to defend ourselves, no matter how much goodwill we extend, peace in the Taiwan Strait and Taiwan's security will still lose their last reliance. Therefore, building up our military and preparing for war is a necessary investment to safeguard peace in the Taiwan Strait and national security, and it is also a major policy matter of the government

[21] Furthermore, the "9/11 terrorist attacks" in the United States revealed that the threats to our national security are no longer limited to the military threat from the Chinese Communist Party. Various natural disasters, major accidents, serious infectious diseases, and terrorist attacks can all have a significant impact on the safety of people's lives and property

[22] Adhering to its sacred duty to protect the nation and its people, the national army actively participates in various natural disasters and emergency rescue operations. It has also revised its organization and equipment, and procured disaster relief and counter-terrorism equipment to meet the needs of maintaining national security during peacetime. It has indeed become a modern national army

[23] Modern national defense is based on building a national defense that is "all-round," "participatory to all citizens," "overall defense," and "trusted by the people." Only by building a consensus on national defense and giving full play to the country's overall combat power can we strengthen national defense capabilities and ensure national security

[24] The most distinctive feature of this book is that, in addition to providing a detailed explanation of the current national defense policy and the vision of the national armed forces, it also presents a detailed discussion of past actions and experiences in cooperating with government policies and transforming them into crisis prevention and handling

[25] It is hoped that all citizens will use this National Defense Report to understand the current national security threats and national defense affairs, thereby supporting and participating in national defense construction, and working together to contribute to the survival and development of the nation

[26] In accordance with the National Defense Act, the Ministry of National Defense regularly publishes the National Defense Report to inform the public of the latest developments in national defense. The content is carefully compiled based on the current national security situation, national defense policies, and administrative actions

[27] The current report (2004) is being compiled in a more transparent manner and is the seventh edition of the report. The entire book can be divided into eight sections: "Security Environment and Military Situation", "National Defense Policy and Military Strategy", "National Defense Organization and Armed Forces", "National Defense Resources and Management" and "National Defense Important Policies" including "Legal System", "Combat Readiness", "Security Services" and "Civil-Military Relations

[28] The contents of each article are summarized below: The first article, "Security Environment and Military Situation," provides an overview of the international security situation, focusing on international strategic trends and the military situation in the Asia-Pacific region; in particular, it provides a more detailed explanation of the Chinese People's Party's national defense policy and military development

[29] Part Two, "National Defense Policy and Military Strategy": Based on current national interests, security objectives, and national defense goals, this section formulates our national defense policy and governance guidelines, and guides military strategy, military building and force planning, as well as the formulation of the national defense vision

[30] Part Three, "National Defense Organization and Armed Forces," describes the division of responsibilities within the national defense organization and the current status of the armed forces, including: Taiwan's national defense system and responsibilities, the Ministry of National Defense's military and political affairs, military equipment, military command system, and the tasks, organization, and main weapons and equipment of standing and reserve forces

[31] Part Four, "National Defense Resources and Management," describes the current status of national defense human, financial, material, and technological resources, as well as their planning, management, and utilization, and future vision, aiming to achieve the greatest benefit in building up and preparing for war with limited national defense resources

[32] Sections five through eight, "Important National Defense Policies," are divided into four categories: "Legal System," "Combat Readiness," "Security Services," and "Military Civilian Relations." They describe the Ministry of National Defense's important policies and actions in the past two years, including the formulation and revision of national defense laws and regulations, national defense mobilization, military education, combat training, logistics, communications, electronic information, and the lives of military personnel, as well as the important policies and actions taken by the National Defense Forces and the people

[33] According to the Constitution of the Republic of China, the purpose of our national defense is to safeguard national security and maintain world peace. Our current national defense concept, military strategy, military building plan and vision are all based on the prevention of war. Based on the international situation and the development of the enemy, we formulate specific national defense policies at this stage, with "prevention of war", "territorial defense" and "counter-terrorism and counter-terrorism" as the basic objectives. With the strategic concept of "effective deterrence and defensive defense", we build a high-quality defensive force with counter-response capabilities

[34] In particular, the CCP has never given up on using force to promote unification. In addition to continuously increasing the number of missiles deployed towards Taiwan, it has repeatedly used large-scale targeted exercises to threaten Taiwan's political and economic stability. In recent years, its economy has grown significantly, and it has invested heavily in national defense and military development. Therefore, in order to maintain stability in the Taiwan Strait and avoid a military imbalance between the two sides, Taiwan is working towards establishing a cross-strait military mutual trust mechanism and forming a code of conduct for the Strait. On the other hand, without engaging in an arms race with the enemy, Taiwan is building basic defense capabilities and developing countermeasures to safeguard national security

[35] In terms of military development planning, the current military development of the Republic of China Armed Forces is focused on the integration of command, control, communications, information, intelligence, reconnaissance and surveillance (C4ISR) systems, missile defense, and air and sea control capabilities

[36] Recently, through meticulous planning and a phased approach, the Legislative Yuan allocated a special budget to procure three urgently needed pieces of equipment for combat readiness: the Patriot 3 missile system, diesel-electric submarines, and long-range fixed-wing antisubmarine aircraft. The aim is to more effectively enhance active defense and anti-blockade capabilities, increase operational flexibility, and acquire strategic deterrence capabilities

[37] In terms of national defense resources, national defense manpower is planned towards a system of recruitment and conscription, with the standing army primarily relying on recruitment and the reserve army primarily relying on conscription. National defense finances adhere to the principle of "efficiency and economy," taking into account the priority of military buildup and preparedness, and allocating financial resources appropriately to effectively support military buildup and preparedness. National defense material resources are based on the policy of "integration of national defense and people's livelihood," cultivating national defense technology capabilities in the civilian sector to achieve the goal of national defense autonomy

[38] Most importantly, we must establish a national consensus on defense, leverage the overall combat power of the nation, mobilize the entire population for defense, unite the people's will to resist the enemy, and give full play to the combined strength of the military and civilians to protect our homes, our property, and our hometowns, thereby safeguarding national security, survival, and development

[39] In addition, the Ministry of National Defense has incorporated "disaster relief" into the normal missions of the armed forces, strengthened the "Army Disaster Relief Mechanism" and overall organization, so that it can be deployed to disaster relief at the first time in accordance with the requirements of "administering according to law" so that people's lives and property can be fully protected, and demonstrate the spirit of the armed forces and the people working together and the unity of the military and the people

[40] In recent years, the Ministry of National Defense, based on military expertise, has continuously weighed changes in the internal and external environment, coordinated with adjustments to the national security strategy, and actively carried out organizational restructuring of national defense while taking into account public opinion. At the same time, it has also vigorously promoted military affairs innovation and improvement under the premise of anticipating enemy threats and operational needs, combining the vision of the armed forces, considering resource conditions, and improving joint combat effectiveness

[41] What they are pursuing is nothing more than building a national defense system with commensurate authority and responsibility, professional division of labor, and high efficiency, so as to fulfill the mission of the national armed forces and live up to the expectations of the people

[42] Page 22 --- 20 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China --- Page 23 --- Introduction Introduction 1 Our Republic of China is based in East Asia as a free and democratic country, and has always strived tirelessly to achieve the goals of government of the people, by the people, and for the people

[43] Based on this premise, the Republic of China Armed Forces have long upheld the mission entrusted to them by the Constitution, continuously strengthening and expanding to ensure the survival and development of the nation, safeguard the safety and well-being of the people, and protect the democratic system and basic human rights

[44] Therefore, the entire military continues to uphold the fine traditions and spirit that have been consistent since its founding, prudently sets long-term strategic goals, is committed to force restructuring and planning, and builds a high-quality, modern national defense force through phased timelines and policy actions

[45] In recent years, facing the restructuring of the international order, changes in the global strategic environment, and the burgeoning reforms in military affairs, the Ministry of National Defense needs to concentrate its efforts on improving national defense laws and regulations, adjusting the national defense organizational structure, defining the priorities for military building, and strengthening military strategy and tactics. Furthermore, it must rely on public opinion to improve the quality of national defense governance and strive to build a high-quality, appropriately sized, and highly capable armed force to safeguard national security

[46] Furthermore, as the nation's armed forces, the national army, as a whole, obeys constitutional norms, supports government policies, and ensures national security; as individuals, it upholds the glorious tradition of loyalty to the nation, the constitution, the people, and duties, and with a high sense of responsibility and honor, expects of itself to fulfill its duties and sincerely serve the people and the nation

[47] In conclusion, national security is the foundation of all development, and national defense preparedness cannot be relaxed for a single day. The armed forces will uphold their position and mission, implement the patriotic belief of fighting for the country and the people, become an army with strong deterrent power, fearless of any external threats, fully safeguard national interests, ensure that the people live and work in peace and contentment, that society is stable and progressive, and that the country achieves sustainable development

[48] Page 24 --- 2 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China --- Page 25 --- Part 1 Security Environment and Military Situation --- Page 26 --- 4 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China ■ Summary of this Part I. Current Global Strategic Situation Since the "9/11 terrorist attacks," the United States has regarded the war on terror as its strategic focus and has actively adjusted its troop deployment, strengthened military cooperation with other countries, and maintained regional security and global military balance

[49] Second, the Asia-Pacific region is the area with the most potential conflicts globally. After overthrowing Saddam Hussein's regime in Iraq, the United States has become more actively involved in Asia-Pacific affairs and adjusted its troop deployments. In addition, factors such as the lingering shadow of North Korea's nuclear weapons program, the continued military expansion of the People's Republic of China, the ongoing disputes over sovereignty in the South China Sea, and the military movements of various countries in the region will all affect the situation in the Taiwan Strait and have a profound impact on Taiwan

[50] Third, the PRC has adjusted its military strategic indicators to respond to the Taiwan Strait and the South China Sea, and has formulated a military strategic policy of "active defense in the new era" to implement "military reform with Chinese characteristics," focusing on mechanization and informatization in order to achieve leapfrog military modernization

[51] At the same time, guided by the strategy of "swift victory in long-range battles and decisive victory in the first battle", the military buildup policy of "strengthening the military through technology" and "promoting training through technology" is emphasized in order to achieve the strategic goal of winning local wars

[52] Fourth, if the PLA were to attack Taiwan in the future, its primary objective would be a surprise attack, employing a "low-loss, high-efficiency, rapid, and decisive" operational approach. The most likely actions would be the deterrent occupation of outlying islands, a sudden high-intensity missile attack and long-range precision strike, and a long-term air and sea blockade to paralyze the island

[53] Furthermore, the CCP's recent active non-military "three-pronged warfare" against Taiwan—"legal warfare," "public opinion warfare," and "psychological warfare"—aims to prepare for a military invasion of Taiwan by manipulating our will, undermining our psychological defenses, and emphasizing the legitimacy of handling the Taiwan issue. This is also worthy of our attention

[54] In the 21st century, with the development of globalization, the international security situation has become more complex

[55] Since the 9/11 terrorist attacks in the United States, the international community has come to realize that military power is an important guarantee for national security

[56] The active development of military capabilities by various countries may pose significant challenges to the international security environment. Furthermore, regional conflicts arising from racial and religious conflicts, territorial disputes, and resource competition, as well as the rise of terrorism, the spread of dangerous military technologies, transnational economic crimes, and drug trafficking, are major concerns for current international security

[57] Section 1 Overall Strategic Environment (Figure 1-1) In the post-Cold War era, the United States, with its overwhelming national power, became the world's only superpower

[58] In addition to its commitment to combating terrorism and curbing the proliferation of weapons of mass destruction, the U.S. is actively adjusting its global troop deployments and strengthening military cooperation with countries in the region to maintain regional security and global stability. (Figure 1-1: Global Strategic Environment Map) Europe: ▶ Expanding NATO defense ▶ Strengthening EU mechanisms ▶ Leading security affairs ▶ Incorporating member participation ▶ Enhancing cooperation and development ▶ Acting as a foundation for stability. Asia-Pacific: ▶ Maintaining alliances ▶ Proliferation prevention initiatives ▶ Leading security affairs ▶ Conducting strategic negotiations ▶ Adjusting troop deployments ▶ Combating terrorism ▶ Deterring potential threats. Oceania: ▶ Supporting peace and peaceful actions. Africa: ▶ Establishing the African Union. ▶Promote development plans ▶Coordinate regional disputes ▶Establish a balancing mechanism ▶Establish a dialogue mechanism ▶Stabilize regional security ▶Promote preventive diplomacy Americas region ▶Strengthen territorial security ▶Establish regional alliances ▶Provide early warning intelligence ▶Ensure regional security --- Page 28 --- 6 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China Military balance objectives

[59] In the Asia-Pacific region, the situation is temporarily stable under the Chinese Communist Party's (CCP) strategy of "peaceful rise" and economic development. However, after the overthrow of Saddam Hussein's regime in Iraq, the United States failed to effectively control anti-American armed groups within Iraq. Coupled with the continued escalation of the Palestinian-Israeli conflict, the overall security environment in the Middle East has become increasingly complex

[60] In the European region, following the eastward expansion and strengthening of mechanisms by the North Atlantic Treaty Organization (NATO) and the European Union (EU), Europe will dominate regional security affairs and play a key role in maintaining regional stability. Furthermore, its strengthened political, economic, and military relations with Russia, and the establishment of mutual trust mechanisms, will further contribute to maintaining regional peace

[61] In Africa, following the establishment of the African Union (AU), efforts have been made to resolve instability among various factions, promote the New Partnership for Africa's Development Project, and establish checks and balances mechanisms to effectively improve the overall economic situation in Africa, thereby eliminating poverty, AIDS, racial and religious conflicts, and other issues

[62] I. Americas Region: Following the 9/11 terrorist attacks, counterterrorism has become a strategic focus for the United States

[63] In addition to establishing the Department of Homeland Security (DHS) to integrate national security, judicial, prosecutorial, investigative, intelligence, military, police, and civilian systems, it also cooperates with countries such as Canada and Mexico to provide real-time early warnings through intelligence sharing. It coordinates and controls operations such as counterterrorism, transnational economic crime, anti-drug trafficking, and combating insurgent forces in Latin America, and strengthens the overall security of the United States, neighboring North America, and Latin America through the new strategic thinking and military deployment of "preemptive strikes

[64] II. Asia Region (1) Japan, South Korea, Australia, and Southeast Asian countries maintain an alliance and cooperation relationship with the United States, and through counterterrorism cooperation with the United States and the "Proliferation Security Initiative" (PSI) mechanism, they combat terrorist organizations and prevent the transport and proliferation of weapons of mass destruction in the region

[65] The CPC has solidified its status as a major power in Asia and deepened its dominance and checks and balances over the regional strategic environment by leveraging its international role in the Iraq War, North Korea's nuclear weapons program, and the South China Sea situation, as well as its progress in space technology

[66] ii) The United States is committed to improving relations with countries such as China and Russia to gain their support for global counterterrorism and nuclear weapons prevention: In order to ensure future military superiority and flexibility in the Asia-Pacific region, it has begun military restructuring and deployment, strengthening the mobile strike capabilities and crisis early warning mechanisms of U.S. troops stationed in Japan and South Korea, in order to deter potential regional conflicts

[67] The United States has also strengthened its military cooperation with the Philippines and Thailand, establishing "major non-NATO allies

[68] Non-NATO Allies) Relationship: Negotiations on the Strategic Cooperation Relationship Framework with Singapore: Strengthening cooperation with Australia, sharing regional defense responsibilities, integrating regional national power through political, economic and military means, maintaining regional security, and supporting the implementation of its global strategy

[69] III) The CCP and India actively improved their relations, temporarily shelved the border dispute, and further expanded economic and military exchanges and cooperation

[70] iv) In 2004, Russian President Vladimir Putin was successfully re-elected, and his high level of public support has become a strong backing for his political and economic reforms

[71] On the other hand, the assassination of Chechen President Kadyrov on May 9 and the school hostage crisis in North Ossetia, Russia on September 1 have gradually shifted the international community's attitude towards the Chechen independence movement to "opposition to any form of terrorism." The road to Chechen independence will become increasingly difficult, and the turbulent situation is unlikely to ease in the near future

[72] Furthermore, due to NATO's eastward expansion, Russia urgently sought to strengthen its strategic position through the Shanghai Cooperation Organization (SCO) and the Russia-China Friendship and Cooperation Treaty. Besides expressing its views on the United States' unipolar military actions (such as withdrawing from the Anti-Ballistic Missile Treaty (ABMT), supporting NATO expansion, and maintaining a long-term military presence in Central Asia under the guise of the war on terror), Russia also established military bases in Kyrgyzstan and Tajikistan to counter US military expansion. In October 2004, Russia formally joined the Central Asian Cooperation Organization (CAC). The organization's efforts to strengthen relations with the CCP and India will help consolidate its regional power status

[73] v) After the Taliban in Afghanistan and Saddam Hussein's regime in Iraq were overthrown, the United States originally hoped to help the two countries establish democratic regimes and effectively destroy the breeding ground for terrorist organizations in the Middle East in order to consolidate regional security

[74] However, the remaining anti-American guerrilla forces in both countries, linked with Al-Qaeda, still pose a threat to regional security. In addition, the torture of prisoners by US and British coalition forces in Iraq has also increased the conflict between hostile forces and US troops. Although the United States returned power to the Iraqi interim government on June 28, 2004, the situation in Iraq remains volatile and unpredictable

[75] The US-led Middle East peace “Roadmap” project, although internationally recognized, has been repeatedly hampered by mutual retaliation between Israel and Palestine. In addition, the death of Palestinian spiritual leader Yasser Arafat in November 2004 has left the prospects for Middle East peace uncertain

[76] Page 30 --- 8 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China III. Europe The NATO organization, in coordination with the United States' global counterterrorism strategy, took over the role of the International Security Assistance Force in Afghanistan and gradually expanded its role in Afghanistan: In March 2004, Lithuania, Estonia, Latvia, Bulgaria, Romania, Slovakia and Slovenia joined NATO, bringing the number of member states to

[77] The European Union signed a new charter, establishing a "Common Defense Clause" to strengthen its independent defense mechanisms and the implementation of international security affairs such as peacekeeping, humanitarian assistance, and crisis management. In May 2004, it agreed to the accession of 10 countries, including Lithuania, Estonia, Latvia, Hungary, Czech Republic, Poland, Slovakia, Slovenia, Southern Cyprus (of Greek descent), and Malta, bringing the total number of member states to

[78] IV. Africa The African Union, established in July 2002, has become an organization dedicated to preventing and controlling wars in Africa, curbing the rise of terrorism, stabilizing society, developing regional resources, and building a political and economic community as a whole, leading Africa toward economic development

[79] In July 2003, the 2nd African Union Summit of Heads of State and Government Leaders was held in Mozambique. The key issues discussed included how to stop regional armed conflicts and implement the "New Partnership for African Development Programme," which brought new opportunities for the positive development of Africa

[80] In order to effectively resolve the problems in Africa, and in response to the humanitarian crisis arising from the unrest in Liberia, Ivory Coast and Sudan, UN Secretary-General Kofi Annan and the heads of state of 13 African countries held a summit in Ghana in July 2004 to seek a peaceful solution

[81] Through years of effort, African countries have established an effective peacekeeping mechanism for resolving internal conflicts. However, whether the African Union can become an effective arbitration and check and balance institution on the African continent, thereby eliminating regional conflicts such as ethnic, religious, and civil wars and stabilizing overall regional security, still depends on the efforts of the countries in the region

[82] Section 2 Major Potential Conflict Areas Influenced by latent factors such as sovereignty, race, religion, and international terrorism, certain regions in the world are highly susceptible to military conflict. Among these, the Korean Peninsula, the Taiwan Strait (see Chapter 4 of this book), the South China Sea, Southeast Asia, South Asia, and the Middle East are the most likely areas for unexpected conflicts

[83] I. The Korean Peninsula: The security situation on the Korean Peninsula is fraught with uncertainty, making it a high-risk area for conflict in the Asia-Pacific region

[84] The trilateral talks held in April 2003 (United States, North Korea, and China), and the subsequent three Six-Party Talks in August, February, and June 2004 (United States, Japan, South Korea, China, North Korea, and Russia), while failing to reach a concrete agreement on North Korea abandoning its nuclear weapons, all agreed to establish a working group, which became an important mechanism for consulting on resolving the North Korean nuclear issue

[85] II. Southeast Asia Due to its vast territory, frequent exchanges between countries, and the existence of numerous religious sects in some countries, Southeast Asia is a region where terrorist organizations can easily flourish, primarily including Jemaah Islamiyah (JI

[86] I.), the Moro Islamic Liberation Front (MILF), and the Abu Sayyaf Group (ASG), and most of them have connections with al-Qaeda

[87] Since the 9/11 terrorist attacks, the activities of these organizations have shifted from the Middle East to Southeast Asia, with Indonesia and the Philippines suffering the most severe terrorist attacks. For example, the bombings that occurred in Bali in October 2002, at the Marriott Hotel in August 2003, and in front of the Australian Embassy in Indonesia in September 2004 caused serious casualties. Their terrorist attack methods have also shifted from targeting military bases and diplomatic embassies to focusing on financial institutions, energy companies, and tourist areas, making security and prevention increasingly difficult

[88] Due to lax management of some international waterways and the Strait of Malacca, piracy is rampant in the region, with frequent incidents of ship hijacking, smuggling, and drug trafficking. This not only threatens the safety of maritime transport and trade but also provides economic and operational channels for terrorist organizations

[89] On July 20, 2004, Singapore, Malaysia, and Indonesia signed a defense agreement for the Strait of Malacca in Batam, Indonesia, agreeing to jointly conduct patrols in the Strait of Malacca to combat piracy and terrorism in order to safeguard the safety of maritime transport routes in the area

[90] III. South Asia In South Asia, India and Pakistan have a long history of discord over religion, territory, and counter-terrorism. However, --- Page 32 --- 10 Republic of China National Defense Report, 2004 Following the meeting between the leaders of the two countries during the 12th South Asia Summit in January 2004, bilateral relations have significantly improved

[91] However, relations between China and India have warmed rapidly since the signing of the "Declaration on Principles and Comprehensive Cooperation between China and India," and military interactions have been frequent, such as joint naval exercises between China and India and the permission granted to an Indian army delegation to visit Tibet

[92] IV. The issue of the Palestinian Authority in the Middle East is the cause of the long-standing conflict between Israel and Arab countries

[93] The US-led Middle East peace "roadmap" plan, while envisioning peaceful coexistence between Palestine and Israel, has repeatedly stalled the peace process due to ongoing armed conflicts between the radical Palestinian organization Hamas and the Israeli military

[94] The United States originally expected that the collapse of Saddam Hussein's regime in Iraq would benefit the security environment, political reforms, and human rights development in the Middle East. However, since the end of the Iraq War, the coalition forces stationed in Iraq have continued to suffer harassment from anti-American armed groups, resulting in increasing casualties. Furthermore, the abuse of prisoners by the US-British coalition forces and the frequent hostage-taking incidents in various countries have led to an increase in regional unrest

[95] Furthermore, Spain's decision to withdraw its troops from Iraq due to terrorist attacks has indirectly impacted other countries' willingness to send troops to Iraq. The United States has been forced to formulate troop increases and contingency plans to ensure the stability of the situation in Iraq

[96] V. South China Sea The islands in the South China Sea hold a strategically important position due to their location at a crucial point on the South China Sea shipping routes

[97] In addition, the region is rich in oil, natural gas, fisheries, and mineral resources. With the advancement of seabed exploration technology, its economic value and strategic position in the Nansha Islands are constantly increasing due to the covetousness of various countries in the region

[98] There are 192 islands and reefs in the South China Sea. Apart from Taiping Island and Dongsha Island, which are owned by our country, the following countries in the region have disputes over the sovereignty of islands: China (7 islands), Vietnam (27 islands), the Philippines (9 islands), Malaysia (5 islands), and Brunei (which claims sovereignty over Nantong Reef). (The situation of islands occupied by various countries in the Spratly Islands is shown in Table

[100] Section 3 Future Security Challenges The current global military situation is unpredictable due to regional conflicts, weapons proliferation and transfer, rapid technological development, changing forms of warfare, and environmental and pandemic-related factors. Potential future security challenges include: I. Political Aspects: Confrontations stemming from racial and religious issues will continue; while separatist sentiments, regional conflicts, territorial disputes, and overlapping economic and maritime territories will also pose potential regional security threats

[101] II. Military Aspects: The proliferation of weapons of mass destruction remains the most serious issue for global security in the future

[102] Furthermore, China's recent successful launch of the Shenzhou-5 spacecraft has drawn attention from various countries to the competition for military interests in space, and may constitute another flashpoint for great power rivalry

[103] Third, in terms of the economy: If the wealth gap caused by globalization continues to widen, coupled with the anti-globalization movement and transnational organized crime (especially in the financial sector), if not handled properly, they will pose a potential threat to global political, economic, and social security

[104] IV. Terrorism: International terrorist organizations (especially religiously fanatical factions) pose a greater security threat

[105] V. Environmental and Epidemic Aspects: Pandemics that spread globally and cause panic, such as Severe Acute Respiratory Syndrome (SARS) and avian influenza, as well as unpredictable disasters caused by environmental and climate pollution and ecological changes, will all affect global security and stability

[106] In summary, the aforementioned international security concerns include conflicts arising from racial, religious, and territorial disputes, as well as issues such as international terrorism, the proliferation of dangerous military technology, transnational economic crimes, and drug trafficking. Once provoked, these issues are highly likely to escalate into regional military conflicts. The regions most likely to trigger such conflicts are the Korean Peninsula, the Taiwan Strait, the South China Sea, and parts of Southeast Asia, South Asia, and the Middle East

[107] In this delicate situation, our country should participate in international affairs with a pragmatic and prudent attitude, actively address potential challenges, and safeguard national security and sustainable development

[108] Chapter Two: Military Situation in the Asia-Pacific Part One: Security Environment and Military Situation 13 The military situation in the Asia-Pacific region is such that the continuous growth of the Chinese Communist Party's military power has led to increased dependence of countries in the region on the United States

[109] In response to issues such as post-war reconstruction in Iraq, North Korea's nuclear weapons program, terrorist activities in Southeast Asia, the South China Sea sovereignty dispute, the India-Pakistan conflict, and the expansion of Chinese military power, the United States has actively adjusted its troop deployment in Northeast Asia and increased its military presence in Guam

[110] Furthermore, through security cooperation mechanisms with the United States and Japan, the United States and South Korea, and the United States and Australia, as well as the operation of the "Taiwan Relations Act," the two sides will further strengthen their relations in order to control the overall security situation in the Western Pacific and maintain regional stability

[111] The Asia-Pacific Strategic Environment The Asia-Pacific region, consisting of mainland China, Northeast Asia, Southeast Asia, and the islands around the Pacific, is a diverse community

[112] Due to the isolation caused by the Pacific Ocean and the surrounding waters, the countries in the region differ in terms of race, culture, politics, religious beliefs, and customs. To ensure their survival and security, they generally prioritize economic development, and in terms of military affairs, they tend to develop towards a diversified model

[113] Asia-Pacific Security Strategy The United States has adopted a "preemptive strike" strategy in response to the growing international terrorism and regional threats, and uses its overwhelming military advantage to ensure its national security and regional strategic interests

[114] II) Military Situation in the Asia-Pacific The military situation in the Asia-Pacific region is gradually changing the military balance in the region as the military power of the PRC increases, directly affecting the national interests of the United States in the Asia-Pacific region, making the Asia-Pacific region a focus of attention for the United States

[115] To ensure its dominance in the Asia-Pacific region, the United States has gradually shifted its strategic thinking from Europe to Asia and has adjusted its regional military deployments accordingly

[116] Based on the experience of the 9/11 terrorist attacks, the United States acknowledged terrorism as the most serious security threat and therefore launched wars in Afghanistan and Iraq in an effort to eliminate the source of the terrorist threat

[117] III) Strategic Position of Guam, Hawaii, New Zealand, and Australia Guam is located in the second island chain and, along with Hawaii, is an important US military base in the Pacific, making its strategic position important

[118] In order to counter the expansion of the Chinese military and to safeguard the strategic interests of the United States in the Asia-Pacific region, the United States has not only significantly adjusted the naval and air forces at its Guam base, but also completed the renovation of the Hawaii Naval Aircraft Carrier Base in November, making the strategic importance of these two bases increasingly important

[119] New Zealand and Australia are actively involved in international affairs and, in accordance with the requests of the United States and the United Nations, participate in peacekeeping missions in East Timor and Iraq and provide economic assistance to Pacific island nations, hoping to strengthen and lead their influence in regional affairs and enhance their international status

[120] II. Situation in Northeast Asia (I) Military Situation In order to reduce its military burden in the Asia-Pacific region, the United States supports Japan's military buildup, legislation amendments and participation in international peacekeeping missions, and strengthens the security alliance between the two countries

[121] The military standoff between North and South Korea on the Korean Peninsula continues. South Korea continues to modernize and develop its military in preparation for a potential war, while North Korea continues to test-fire missiles and does not abandon its pursuit of weapons of mass destruction. Its nuclear weapons issue remains a potential threat to regional military security

[122] II) Potential Crisis: Russia and Japan have long been embroiled in a dispute over territorial sovereignty over the Northern Territories (Southern Kuril Islands), and have yet to sign a peace treaty to end the hostile relationship that began in World War II

[123] The demarcation of the East China Sea between China and South and North Korea involves the economic and water interests of the three parties and the livelihoods of fishermen. There have been several tense standoffs caused by illegal fishing boats crossing the border. However, thanks to the restraint of the three parties, no large-scale conflict has occurred

[124] Furthermore, a dispute has emerged between the CCP and Japan over the exploration of natural gas resources in the East China Sea. The dispute between Japan and South Korea over Dokdo (Takeshima) remains unresolved. Dokdo is under South Korean jurisdiction, and in addition to building a pier and deploying police forces there, South Korea also uses international propaganda to assert its control

[125] The Diaoyu Islands are an inherent part of my country's territory. However, both China and Japan claim sovereignty over them. On the one hand, China demonstrates its sovereignty through marine surveying and continental reef layer investigations. On the other hand, Japan "leases" the islands to private entities and includes them within its 200-nautical-mile exclusive economic zone, making the sovereignty dispute over the Diaoyu Islands even more complicated

[126] III. Situation in Southeast Asia (I) Military Situation The military activities of Southeast Asian countries are now mainly focused on combating terrorist activities and separatist organizations; the Association of Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN) has established the Counter-Terror Center Southeast Asia in Malaysia to demonstrate the determination of countries in the region to fight terrorism

[127] In October 2003, ASEAN held its Ninth Summit, where China and ASEAN signed the Treaty of Friendship and Cooperation in Southeast Asia and agreed to hold a Security Policy Conference with the participation of military personnel. They also issued the Joint Declaration on the Strategic Partnership between China and ASEAN, thereby dispelling ASEAN's concerns about the "China threat theory

[128] Regarding the sovereignty dispute in the South China Sea, the relevant countries, in accordance with the spirit of the "Declaration on the Conduct of Parties in the South China Sea," are striving to establish a mechanism of mutual trust to create favorable conditions for the peaceful resolution of their differences

[129] ii) Potential Crisis Although most countries in the region are politically stable and there is no immediate threat of conflict, some disputes still exist between them (such as the sovereignty and water supply issues of Pedra Branca in Singapore and Malaysia). However, they are constrained by the ASEAN system and are able to exercise self-restraint and not resort to force

[130] In April 2004, the Vietnamese government organized a tour group to the Spratly Islands under the guise of tourism, and its military decided to repair a military airport on Nanwei Island. This move drew protests from the governments of Taiwan, China, and the Philippines, adding further uncertainty to the peaceful resolution of the South China Sea issue

[131] IV. Central Asia Situation (I) Military Situation --- Page 38 --- 16 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China Central Asia is located in the heart of the Eurasian continent and serves as a bridge connecting Eurasia. It has a great influence on the security of the PRC to the west and Russia to the south. Due to its rich strategic oil and gas resources, its geopolitical position is extremely important and has always been a strategic location fought over by military strategists

[132] II) Potential Crisis The military presence of the United States in Central Asia poses a significant threat to Russia and China

[133] Russia is redeploying its military forces in Central Asia to gain strategic space and security, in order to counter the expansion of U.S. military power in the region

[134] In order to consolidate its influence, the PRC has conducted joint counter-terrorism military exercises with member states through the Shanghai Cooperation Organisation (SCO) mechanism in the past two years to strengthen the exchange of counter-terrorism experience. In the future, it may follow this model to conduct regional joint military exercises and actively intervene in regional military affairs

[135] Religious disputes, ethnic conflicts, and territorial disputes in other regions, such as border disputes between Kazakhstan and Russia, and between Tajikistan and China, will all affect the regional security situation

[136] For a long time, India and Pakistan have been in conflict over the ownership of the border region of Kashmir Province, and have also been engaged in a nuclear arms race

[137] Stability in South Asia is of great significance to security in the Asia-Pacific region, and the CCP plays a pivotal role in this. In October 2003, the navies of China and Pakistan held their first joint military exercise in the waters off Shanghai; the following month, the Chinese navy conducted exercises with the Indian navy at the same location, clearly demonstrating China's intention to promote military cooperation between the two sides

[138] The Indian military, which was originally strongly anti-China, has now clearly changed its stance. Its previous attitude of openly regarding the CCP as a hypothetical enemy has become more conservative, and military exchanges and interactions between the two countries are frequent

[139] II) Potential Crisis: Sino-Indian Border Conflict: The border between China and India is 2,000 kilometers long. Although the two sides have established a joint working group on border issues and completed the exchange of maps of the central border area, there has been no progress in resolving the territorial disputes in the eastern and western sections

[140] India-Pakistan Conflict: Since India proposed the "normalization of relations between India and Pakistan" in April 2003, relations between the two sides have improved significantly. In October, India again proposed the "12-point peace initiative" to promote people-to-people exchanges, which also received a positive response from the Pakistani government. In November, the foreign ministries of the two countries issued a joint statement agreeing to implement a ceasefire in the Kashmir region

[141] In January 2004, former Indian Prime Minister Vajpayee and Pakistani President Musharraf held the South Asia Summit in Islamabad. The two countries had in-depth discussions on issues such as Kashmir and reached a consensus to launch a comprehensive dialogue. In May and June, India and Pakistan exchanged views on dialogue and principles on nuclear weapons confidence-building measures, which helped to resolve the final negotiations on the Kashmir issue and the arms race between them

[142] Section 2: Military Movements of Various Countries The Asia-Pacific region is the area with the most conflict points in the world. After the United States overthrew Saddam Hussein's regime in Iraq, it actively intervened in Asia-Pacific affairs and adjusted its troop deployment

[143] On August 16, 2004, U.S. President George W. Bush announced that over the next decade, the deployment and command structure of U.S. troops stationed in Europe and Asia would be adjusted, and the strategic focus would be shifted from Europe to the Asia-Pacific region, with the aim of adjusting relations with regional allies to deter regional conflicts

[144] United States Asia-Pacific Military Power Current status of U.S. troops stationed in Japan: Total strength of approximately 40,000 personnel

[145] The aircraft include approximately 150 aircraft of various types, such as the F-15, F-16, and A-10; armored vehicles include approximately 350 vehicles of various types, such as the M1A1, M-2, and M-3; and approximately 84 artillery pieces of various types

[146] In addition, the U.S. military will adjust its troops stationed in South Korea. Apart from a portion of the 2nd Infantry Division being transferred to Iraq, the rest have been gradually withdrawn from the Demilitarized Zone north of the Han River since 2004 and moved south to Osan and Pyeongtaek bases

[147] Republic of China 93 National Defense Report (II) Military Developments Northeast Asia: Japan adjusted the location of the Air Self-Defense Force Headquarters and the U.S. Forces Japan Command to be stationed at Yokota Air Base to enhance military interaction; the Pacific Command's Naval Reconnaissance Group Intelligence Center moved to Misawa Air Base in Japan in November 2003; some units of the U.S. Marine Corps formerly stationed in Okinawa, Japan will be moved to Australia

[148] South Korea is proceeding with a two-phase plan to gradually relocate U.S. troops from the Demilitarized Zone (DMZ) southwards, while actively strengthening the combat capabilities of U.S. forces stationed there. In addition to increasing the number of Apache attack helicopters, F-117 fighter jets, Patriot III missiles, constructing a runway at Osan Air Base, and deploying over 100 additional armored vehicles to its heavy brigade, South Korea will invest $11 billion over the next four years to enhance its military and intelligence capabilities

[149] Southeast Asia: Expand the Changi Naval Base in Singapore and establish floating military bases and logistics and maintenance bases in the Gulf of Siam, Thailand, and the Natuna Islands, Indonesia; aim to further acquire leases for the Uttakash Naval Base and Sattahip Naval Base in Thailand, and Morotai and Pyak Islands in Indonesia; also, contact Vietnam and India to explore the possibility of establishing military outposts

[150] To enhance its strike capabilities across the Pacific, the plan includes the deployment of an additional carrier strike group, possibly based in Guam or Hawaii

[158] Military Developments: In response to the threat of large-scale destructive military equipment from North Korea, the Republic of China has increased its defense budget, strengthened military procurement, and focused on developing and enhancing its core combat capabilities. This highlights its commitment to easing political relations with North Korea while simultaneously implementing military preparedness measures

[159] In addition, to maintain the military alliance with the United States, the government strives to avoid the influence of anti-American sentiment among the public on its decisions to ally with the United States, and to maintain its ability to coordinate responses to emergencies

[160] Current status of North Korean military strength: Total military strength is approximately

[162] Military developments: The political leadership and military command structure centered on Kim Jong-il will continue, with military priorities taking precedence over politics. The country will actively increase its missile and nuclear arsenal as an important means to enhance its national status and secure international aid

[163] iv) Current status of Russian military strength: Total military strength is approximately

The remaining number of troops; the directly subordinate units (including peacekeeping forces and overseas troops) number approximately

[165] Military developments: In order to improve its economic situation, Russia has been continuously exporting weapons and equipment to China and third-world countries. Through arms sales, Russia aims to improve its domestic economy, develop its defense industry, and strengthen its military. It is committed to military reform and strives to build a professional and rapidly mobile armed force to maintain its military superiority

[166] President Putin also proposed a medium-term development strategy to modernize the armed forces by 2010. Its security strategy aims to "use strong military power to detect threats at the first moment, consolidate traditional spheres of influence, maintain national security and stability, and ensure that the economy is not affected

[167] III. [172] II) Military movements: The Philippines, Indonesia and other countries have deployed a large number of military forces to clear and combat terrorist organizations and separatist activities within their borders

[173] Due to the shadow of the Chinese Communist Party's military power, concerns have arisen regarding the security of the ASEAN region. In response, the CCP has put forward the policy of "good neighborliness, security, and prosperity" and has taken measures such as joining the Treaty of Amity and Cooperation in Southeast Asia and establishing a strategic partnership with ASEAN in an attempt to eliminate the "China threat theory," consolidate relations with neighboring countries in the region, and expand its influence in Southeast Asia

[174] Following the 9/11 terrorist attacks, Southeast Asia became a region of active terrorist organizations. In order to carry out counterterrorism missions, the United States strengthened its military cooperation with countries in the region, successively listing the Philippines and Thailand as "non-NATO major allies" and initiating negotiations on a "strategic cooperation framework agreement" with Singapore. This not only paved the way for the adjustment of its global troop presence but also served as a check on the "Southeast Asia Friendship Treaty" signed between the PRC and ASEAN countries

[175] Central Asia The five Central Asian countries of Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, Turkmenistan and Uzbekistan have a total military strength of about 160,000 people

[176] The army has a strength of 126,000 personnel and 2,227 main battle vehicles; the navy has a strength of 1,000 personnel and 5 ships; the air force has a strength of approximately 35,000 personnel and 428 fighter jets and approximately 80 armed helicopters

[177] II) Military Developments: Central Asia faces threats such as drug trafficking, religious extremist organizations, and terrorists. Countries in the region actively promoted the integration of the Collective Security Treaty of the Commonwealth of Independent States (CSI). In October 2002, the member states of the CSI Collective Security Treaty (Russia, Belarus, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, and Armenia) adopted a military organizational charter. In late April 2003, a collective rapid response force was established, and the "Southern Shield 2003" anti-terrorism exercise was held at the end of the year

[178] The Shanghai Cooperation Organisation (SCO), comprised of China, Russia, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, and Uzbekistan, held joint anti-terrorism military exercises with its member states in October 2002 and August

[188] Section 3 Impact on Regional Security The Asia-Pacific region is the region with the most conflict points in the world. Due to the continuous expansion of the Chinese Communist Party's military power, the doubts of countries in the region about the "Chinese Communist Party threat theory" have deepened

[189] Faced with challenges such as post-war reconstruction in Iraq, the North Korean nuclear issue, the situation in the Taiwan Strait, the India-Pakistan conflict, and the expansion of Chinese military power, the United States has adopted a "preemptive strike" strategy in its military operations. It is also adjusting its troop presence in the Asia-Pacific region and strengthening its defense mechanisms with neighboring countries in order to maintain regional peace and stability

[190] As the overall national strength of the CCP grows, it gradually attracts the attention of countries around the world. In order to safeguard their strategic interests in the Asia-Pacific region, the United States and the CCP will inevitably continue to compete, which will have a significant impact on the future development of the world situation

[191] Because of its pivotal position at the crossroads of the first island chain connecting China to the western Pacific (Note 2), Taiwan has a crucial role in balancing the power dynamics in the Asia-Pacific region. If China effectively leverages this advantage, it will undoubtedly contribute to the country's security and development

[192] Based on safeguarding our own security and interests, we should strengthen our key role in the Western Pacific region, accelerate military reform and force transformation, and unite with friendly military forces in the region to establish strategic alliances. We should continue to adhere to the basic objectives of "preventing war, defending our territory, and counter-terrorism and counter-disruption" to maintain regional stability, ensure national security, and adapt to the ever-changing situation in the Western Pacific

[193] Part One: Security Environment and Military Situation 25 Chapter Three: China's National Defense Policy and Military Trends Under years of continuous high economic growth, China's national defense expenditure has been expanding and its military strength has been greatly enhanced, gradually showing signs of becoming a major power

[194] As a regional military power, the PRC seeks to expand its military, economic, and political influence from Central Asia to the South Pacific islands, strengthen its military alliances with countries such as Pakistan, Myanmar, and North Korea, and actively courts Southeast Asian countries such as Cambodia, Laos, Malaysia, and Thailand, attempting to extend its influence to the entire Asia-Pacific region

[195] In order to further enhance national defense capabilities and implement the military strategic policy of "winning local wars under high-tech conditions," the People's Liberation Army is continuing its streamlining and reorganization work in accordance with the disarmament policy

[196] In January 2004, it further proposed to focus on "reform of operations and training under information (informatization) conditions," implement research and verification, and actively develop the navy and air force and strengthen joint combat capabilities, so that its security threat to Taiwan is increasing day by day

[197] Section 1 National Defense Policy The national defense policy of the People's Republic of China is formulated in accordance with its national interests. Its "2002 National Defense White Paper" clearly defines the main objectives as follows: 1. To enhance comprehensive national strength with economic development as the center; to uphold and improve the socialist system; to maintain and promote social stability and unity; to strive for a long-term peaceful international environment and a favorable surrounding environment; and to safeguard sovereignty, unity and territorial integrity

[198] II. Consolidate national defense, guard against and resist aggression; prevent division and achieve reunification; prevent armed subversion and maintain social stability; strengthen national defense construction and realize the modernization of national defense and the armed forces; safeguard world peace and oppose aggression and expansion

[199] Section 2 Military Strategy To adapt to the demands of global military reforms and national strategic development, the CCP proposed to comprehensively promote national defense modernization on the basis of economic development; and based on this, it formulated a new era military strategic policy to achieve "military reforms with Chinese characteristics"; focusing on a combined transformation of mechanization and informatization, aiming to achieve leapfrog modernization

[200] Its military strategy is "active defense," using vague military tactics to respond to national military and security affairs, and never initiating war or aggression; however, it will only retaliate if national sovereignty, territorial integrity, or if it is attacked first

[201] Therefore, the CCP's military strategy not only adopts an immediate response to threats, but also uses high-pressure force to play a deterrent role, actively building a strategic security environment and preventing the enemy from taking any actions that affect its interests

[202] The key points are as follows: (1) Adjust the military strategic indicators to respond to the Taiwan Strait and the South China Sea, and emphasize the military strategy of "active defense": formulate the military strategic policy of "active defense in the new era", and accelerate the development of national defense scientific research and weapons and equipment under the guidance of strengthening the military through science and technology, cultivate high-quality new military talents, establish a scientific system, develop combat theories with Chinese characteristics, and enhance the ability to conduct joint operations, mobile operations and perform multiple missions

[203] II) Influenced and inspired by the Second Gulf War, the military has carried out comprehensive and active reforms, forming the strategic guidance of "swift victory in long-range warfare and decisive victory in the first battle," emphasizing the military building policies of "strengthening the military through science and technology" and "promoting training through science and technology": strengthening the quality of military building, striving to complete the dual construction of automation and informatization, and aiming to achieve the strategic goal of "winning informationized local wars

[204] Its tactics and methods have shifted from emphasizing annihilation of enemy forces and seizing space to using limited forces to achieve political objectives. This trend will make the means of combat more inclined towards "deterrence" rather than "annihilation," and the tactics will include both military and non-military methods

[205] iii) The PRC’s main deterrent force includes strategic and tactical missiles and naval and air power, which are achieved through military, non-military and non-military means

[206] At this stage of development, the focus is on the Second Artillery Corps and the Navy and Air Force, while the ground forces are focused on the mechanization of the entire army and the priority of establishing emergency mobile combat units. Weapon upgrades will be carried out through a dual-track approach of independent research and development and foreign procurement to enhance overall combat capabilities

[207] iv) The CCP drew on the war experience of the U.S. military and revised “active defense” to “enhancing defensive combat capabilities under high-tech conditions”, and developed asymmetric warfare tactics with “pressure point warfare” and “paralysis warfare” as the main methods

[208] Part One: Security Environment and Military Situation 27 Section Three: Defense Budget The CCP's defense budget has grown at a double-digit percentage rate for the past decade or so, mainly used for military personnel living expenses, military social security, and military maintenance expenditures; while in order to adapt to changes in the international situation, maintain its national security and territorial integrity, improve its defense and combat capabilities under high-tech conditions, and appropriately increase some equipment construction, etc

[209] I. Overview of Budget Preparation The Chinese Communist Party's 2004 national defense budget was RMB 210 billion (approximately 253 billion USD

[210] US$9.5 billion, an increase of 11.6 percent from US$188.1 billion in 2003; accounting for 1.68 percent of its GDP and 7.85 percent of its total government spending

[211] The funds are mainly used to increase military welfare measures, respond to changes in the international situation, increase defense and combat capabilities, and to address the resettlement or retirement benefits of 200,000 troops cut before 2005 (see Figure 1-2 for the national defense budget statistics published by the CCP over the years

[212] Figure 1-2 Statistical Chart of National Defense Budgets Published by the CCP Over the Years (RMB 100 Million, USD 100 Million, Percentage Growth

[213] II. Budget Structure Analysis The Chinese Communist Party's defense budget can be divided into three parts, including personnel living expenses (i.e., personnel maintenance expenses, mainly used for the salaries, food and clothing of officers, soldiers and staff within the establishment), activity maintenance expenses (i.e., operational maintenance expenses, mainly used for troop training, engineering facility maintenance and daily consumption, water and electricity expenses, etc.), and equipment expenses (i.e. military investment expenses, mainly used for the research, testing, procurement, maintenance, transportation and storage of weapons and equipment), each accounting for about one-third

[214] Assessment of Hidden Budget The national defense expenditure announced by the CCP is actually only a part of its overall national defense budget. According to historical records, military-related expenditures are hidden in non-military sectors, including the Commission of Science, Technology and Industry for National Defense, nuclear industry, and more than 10 other departments. Hidden non-military projects include education, culture, and scientific research. Weapon and equipment procurement is obtained through funds such as "central government appropriations," "military surplus," and "military sales revenue

[215] Therefore, it can be inferred that its actual defense spending is about 3 to 5 times the published figure (approximately 500 to 630 billion RMB, or about 65 to 76 billion USD

[216] IV. Budgetary Planning Trends According to the 2004 financial report of the State Council of the People's Republic of China, although its budget deficit and domestic and foreign debt reached RMB 702.2 billion, its foreign exchange reserves were approximately USD 460 billion, so it still invested heavily in national defense

[217] Its 2004 defense budget continued the double-digit percentage growth of previous years. There are three reasons for this: (1) Accelerated economic growth: In recent years, the average economic growth rate of the CCP has reached more than 8%, and even reached 9.1% in 2003. Therefore, under the goal of "improving the defense and combat capabilities under high-tech conditions", defense funds were allocated

[218] II) Improving the treatment of military personnel: The CCP has adjusted salaries three times in the past two years to improve the treatment of officers and soldiers in the People's Liberation Army; in January 2004, its civil servants received another pay raise, and it is expected that the PLA will follow suit. (III) Responding to the needs of troop reduction: The CCP plans to reduce its military by 200,000 personnel by 2005, at which time budgets will be required for personnel resettlement and retirement benefits

[219] As China's defense budget continues to increase and its combat capabilities are significantly enhanced, it poses a potential threat to the security of its surrounding regions and world peace. The deployment of its new generation of weapons and equipment to the southeastern coast has a serious impact on the military balance across the Taiwan Strait

[220] Since the CCP merged the 11 military regions into 7 in 1985, the total number of personnel in the People's Liberation Army has remained below 2.5 million

[221] In 2003, in accordance with the policy of reducing the military by 200,000 personnel, the military actively carried out streamlining and reorganization work, and drew lessons from the military affairs reform and troop reorganization experience of Western countries to further reduce the size of the military and realize the vision of miniaturizing the military and rationalizing the force structure

[222] The current PLA downsizing primarily involves reducing the size of government agencies, military academies, and ground forces. Ground forces are being merged into army groups. The Navy and Air Force are reducing "base" and "corps" level units and accelerating organizational "flattening" to streamline the organization, improve troop utilization and combat command efficiency, eliminate old ships and replace them with new fighter jets, and continuously optimize combat formations to meet the needs of high-tech warfare

[223] People's Liberation Army (I) Total strength of approximately 2.23 million personnel; of which the Second Artillery Corps accounts for 6%, ground forces account for 64%, naval forces account for 14%, and air force forces account for 16% (the overall force structure and deployment of the CCP are shown in Figures 1-3 and

[224] II) Deployment of the Second Artillery Corps: The total strength is more than 130,000 personnel, which are divided into missile launching units, technical equipment units, engineering units, chemical defense units, training units and service support units

[225] Several corps-level units, comprising over 20 ballistic missile brigades and more than 100 missile sites of various types, have deployed over 780 strategic and tactical ballistic missiles (610 of which are deployed in the immediate vicinity). Satellite navigation systems have been continuously added to improve accuracy. The Dongfeng-15 and its improved versions are deployed in Jiangxi Province, while the Dongfeng-11 and its improved versions are deployed in Fujian Province. Their range can cover all important military and political facilities on the entire island of Taiwan (see figure on page 49 of this book

[229] Future development: Strengthen the construction of combined arms group armies and mechanized divisions (brigades), actively replace them with third-generation armored personnel carriers, enhance emergency mobile warfare capabilities, and at the same time prioritize the establishment of information warfare capabilities and an information-based command system to improve overall combat capabilities

[230] Part One Security Environment and Military Situation 31 (IV) Naval Force Deployment: The total strength is approximately 329,000 personnel, which is divided into naval forces, shore combat forces, marines, coastal defense forces, logistics support forces, academies, and naval aviation forces

[231] In addition, it will purchase and develop new weapons to improve the quality and combat capabilities, strengthen the construction of the South China Sea battlefield, enhance control over the South China Sea and expand the threat to Taiwan, and accelerate the training of tactics against Taiwan in order to enhance landing and sea control capabilities

[232] V) Air Force Deployment: The total strength is approximately 370,000 personnel, which is divided into aviation units, radar units, air defense units, airborne units and other specialized units

[233] There are nearly 100 joint military-civilian airports within 600 nautical miles of Taiwan, of which more than 20 are equipped with approximately 730 fighter jets of various types; the airlift force has the capability to transport nearly two airborne regiments in a single airborne operation

[234] Future development: The preparation goal is to achieve rapid mobility and precision strikes in joint operations; strengthen the integration of new-generation weapon and equipment systems, enhance long-range precision strike capabilities in the air, actively conduct research and training on tactics and methods, strengthen airborne combat capabilities, and integrate and establish intelligence, surveillance, and reconnaissance systems

[235] II. People's Armed Police The People's Armed Police (abbreviated as PAP) number approximately 930,000 (if the Public Security Border Defense Force and Firefighting Force are included, the total number is over 1.5 million). While the AP is organizationally part of the public security system, its operations are under the dual leadership and command of the Central Military Commission and the Ministry of Public Security of the State Council. Basic tasks and organizational structure are led by the State Council through the Ministry of Public Security; its management and training are led by the Central Military Commission through the General Staff Department

[236] I) Mission: In peacetime, the main missions are to protect important CCP agencies and personnel, maintain social order, handle emergencies, and ensure the safety of strategic resource facilities such as forestry and mining. In wartime, the missions are under the control of the military command system, responsible for maintaining battlefield security, counter-infiltration, and sentry duties, as well as coordinating with regular troops in defensive operations

[237] II) Organization: The Armed Police General Headquarters has a Command, a Political Department, a Logistics Department, and subordinate professional staff units such as the Mobile Division, Internal Security Corps, Forest Command, Hydropower Command, Gold Command, and Transportation Command, as well as professional corps; each corps is further divided into detachments, battalions, companies, squads, and platoons

[238] III) Future Development: In addition to continuing to build up the counter-terrorism brigade and streamlining its "quantity" while improving its "quality," the brigade will continue to procure counter-terrorism equipment, increase the purchase of non-lethal weapons, strengthen riot control and counter-terrorism combat training, and actively conduct joint counter-terrorism military exercises with neighboring countries. Through international cooperation, the brigade aims to eliminate the three evil forces (terrorism, separatism, and extremist religious extremism) within its borders, effectively handling sudden major crimes or large-scale riots. Furthermore, the brigade's professional forestry, transportation, and hydropower armed police forces will primarily cooperate with national forestry conservation and the Western Development Strategy to effectively support overall economic development, and their strength should not be underestimated

[239] III. Reserve Forces and Militia (I) Reserve Forces: Total strength of approximately 600,000 (nearly 200,000 active duty

[240] Mission: Composed of retired officers and soldiers as the backbone, mobilized in wartime to supplement the PLA's shortage or directly deployed to combat missions; primarily responsible for territorial defense and air defense of key areas, while also emphasizing the supporting development of logistical support capabilities, it is the main reserve armed force of the CCP

[241] ii) Militia: Number approximately 100 million (accounting for 10% of the total population). Mission: An armed organization that combines peacetime and wartime needs, integrating labor and military service. In peacetime, they engage in production work and receive military training. In wartime, they cooperate with the People's Liberation Army to carry out combat missions primarily focused on "defence

[242] The organization and deployment of "ordinary militia" are based on townships, towns, administrative villages, and factories and mines; while "core militia" are trained separately and conduct military training at training bases within county-level administrative regions. Currently, they are organized into specialized technical units such as anti-aircraft artillery, anti-aircraft missiles, artillery, communications, chemical defense, engineering, and reconnaissance
