[243] III) Future Development: The CCP reserve forces and militia will continue to strengthen training, including deepening tactics and rapid mobilization, based on actual combat; at the same time, they will cooperate with regular forces to conduct combat exercises to adapt to the needs of high-tech local wars

[244] In response to the new situation in the Taiwan Strait, the People's Liberation Army successively promulgated new "Militia Oaths" and upgraded combat readiness training in 2004. This move implies national defense mobilization for unification by non-peaceful means

[245] Military Exercises In recent years, the PLA's military exercises have shown a trend of increasing year by year (the number of exercises decreased slightly in 2003 due to the impact of SARS). Except for the joint amphibious landing exercise conducted by the Nanjing and Guangzhou Military Regions, which was more targeted, the rest are all annual planned exercises

[246] The training focuses on implementing the requirements and standards of "advanced technology, weaponry and equipment, combat missions, and battlefield environment," strengthening tactical and operational research and exercises with the aim of "seeking depth through specificity and breakthroughs through depth," increasing the difficulty and intensity of training, and actively building a targeted, confrontational, and collaborative training system

[247] I. Strengthening Joint Military Exercises (I) Artillery Corps: Focusing on training in testing, firing, and familiarizing with the operation of various types of short-range, medium-, and intercontinental ballistic missiles. The number of short-range missile tests in 2003 increased by 15% compared to 2002, and various types of missile tests continued in 2004, demonstrating that the PLA is sparing no effort in strengthening its tactical missile attack capabilities

[248] II) Ground Forces: Coastal military region ground forces focus on nearshore training for amphibious landings and have established amphibious mechanized units tailored to the characteristics of the Taiwan region; inland forces continue to use physical models to conduct loading and unloading operations around their bases; in recent years, joint amphibious landing exercises in the southeastern coastal region have focused on strengthening command procedures, landing and land combat tactics to enhance joint amphibious landing capabilities

[249] III) Navy: Focus on joint operations exercises to shorten the time to form combat capability; the exercises will focus on joint amphibious landing operations, missile launches, and live-fire confrontation exercises; and will be combined with the testing of new weapons and equipment to improve the ability to control the sea

[250] IV) Air Force: With the primary task of strengthening participation in joint exercises of various services, each military region continues to carry out long-distance cross-regional mobile deployment, off-site training, night flights, interception by different aircraft types, and tactical and operational exercises in coordination with other services, such as air-to-ground confrontation. It also actively hones pilots' combat experience, strengthens joint training and confrontation, and tests ground logistics support capabilities, so as to have the ability to provide rapid support (reinforcement) for "all-weather combat

[251] Page 56 --- 34 Republic of China 2004 National Defense Report II. Joint Anti-Terrorism Military Exercises The People's Liberation Army (PLA) Armed Police Corps in Beijing, Shenyang, Jiangxi, Anhui, Heilongjiang, Xinjiang, Sichuan, and Shanxi recently conducted comprehensive anti-terrorism exercises based on the scenario of "international terrorist attacks." In the past two years, the Xinjiang Armed Police Force has cooperated with regular troops

[252] The PLA has participated in joint counter-terrorism military exercises conducted by the Shanghai Cooperation Organisation. In addition, the Hainan Armed Police Corps served as a counter-terrorism support unit during the Boao Forum for Asia held on Hainan Island in 2003. From the beginning of 2004 to the present, Hunan, Hubei, Gansu and Xinjiang have conducted six large-scale joint counter-terrorism military exercises, codenamed "Taishan 2004", to highlight the PLA's three basic counter-terrorism tasks of "defense, rescue and strike

[253] III. Strengthen the "New Three Strikes and Three Defenses Drills (I) Second Artillery: Focus on deploying smoke and fog to obscure the enemy, strengthen camouflage and use fake vehicles to deceive the enemy, and prevent enemy reconnaissance and surveillance; as well as drill defense against precision strikes and electronic interference to ensure the integrity of combat power

[254] II) Ground Forces: Increase the use of natural conditions for reconnaissance and surveillance, and strengthen engineering units for precision strikes, electronic jamming, and communications units for electronic jamming and counter-jamming; air defense units will focus on countering cruise missiles, strengthen ground artillery units for attacking armed helicopters, and actively conduct live-fire exercises against missiles and air raids

[255] III) Navy: Improve the three-pronged attack and three-defense capabilities through beyond-visual-range missile attacks, data link testing, and electronic warfare training

[256] iv) Air Force: Focus on joint training and confrontation capabilities, adopt the approach of "from point to area, first separate and then combine, closely follow actual combat, and synchronize day and night" to carry out joint air-ground training, live-fire confrontation and unannounced air defense exercises, so as to practice the air attack and counter-air attack capabilities of air defense forces, improve the coordination of air defense missiles and artillery and the joint air defense and strike capabilities of high, medium and low altitudes, and effectively carry out the "three strikes" operation

[257] High-tech research is the future development trend of the CCP, which can be divided into two parts: "new concept weapons" and "space development

[258] The CCP believes that new weapons developed with advanced technology will change the nature of warfare, and these are collectively referred to as "new concept weapons." In terms of aerospace technology development, the CCP launched its first manned spacecraft in October 2003, and joined the ranks of international space powers

[259] The relevant developments are summarized below: I. New Concept Weapons (I) Directed Energy Weapons: Based on the existing "Shenguang Series" laser output devices, high-energy laser weapons that can be used in air defense, anti-missile and anti-satellite operations will be developed, which will be able to effectively prevent satellite reconnaissance and carry out counterattack operations

[260] ii) Electromagnetic pulse weapons: using conventional electromagnetic pulse warheads to paralyze radar, high-power communication equipment, digital wired switches, etc., causing complete paralysis of the enemy's command, control, communication, information, intelligence, reconnaissance and surveillance (C4ISR) system

[261] iii) nanoweapons: can make electronic devices smaller, more precise and more flexible than ever before, and help to miniaturize and lighten existing weapon systems, such as micro robots, micro precision missiles or parasitic stars (Note 4), which are widely used

[262] iv) Chip-based weapons: These are artificial intelligence weapons, characterized by computer chips with large memory capacity and high computing speed, such as land-attack cruise missiles and drones, which can accurately reach targets according to preset flight paths; if further developed, special chips can be implanted into the enemy's command and communication system, which can paralyze the enemy's command, control, communication and intelligence systems in wartime

[263] Space technology is a key focus of the CPC's space exploration. Currently, there are more than 10 satellites in total, including those for communication, meteorology, positioning, navigation, remote sensing, ocean observation, and intelligence gathering

[264] In 2004, approximately 10 satellites were launched, including remote sensing, communication, and meteorological satellites, giving it global, uninterrupted surveillance capabilities

[265] I) The General Armament Department of the People's Liberation Army has three satellite launch centers under its jurisdiction: Taiyuan Base in Shanxi Kelan, Xichang Base in Sichuan, and Jiuquan Base in Gansu

[266] ii) Existing space rockets, including the Long March series with a total of 14 models, use liquid rocket propulsion and can launch up to

[267] III) In the future, multiple "Resource" series remote sensing and reconnaissance satellites, "Zhongxing" series communication satellites, "Fengyun" series meteorological satellites, electronic reconnaissance satellites, long-range infrared strategic missile early warning satellites, and "Beidou" series navigation and positioning satellites will be deployed to form a space military information system. This system can form a command, control, communication, intelligence, reconnaissance, and surveillance network covering five dimensions of space: air, land, sea, space (space), and electronic (electronic warfare

[268] iv) On October 15, 2003, the Chinese Communist Party's Shenzhou V spacecraft carried out its first manned spaceflight mission; in 2005, Shenzhou VI was to be launched to test a space mission lasting nearly a week with two or more astronauts. After the completion of various space experiments, it could lay the foundation for the Chinese Communist Party to establish a "space army" (i.e., to set up a space command or space force) and help improve the Chinese Communist Party's space surveillance capabilities

[269] Section 7 Reserve Mobilization In order to meet the requirements of future wars characterized by "high technological content, short war duration, and high combat power consumption," the CPC proposed at its 16th National Congress to promote the construction and organizational adjustment of national defense mobilization in the first two decades of this century. This would be done in conjunction with the formulation and amendment of various laws and regulations, following the policy of "military-civilian integration, peacetime-wartime integration, and integrating the military into the civilian population," with the goal of "fewer troops in peacetime and more troops in wartime," continuously strengthening the national defense mobilization system to effectively support military operations

[270] I. Mobilization Mechanism To enhance its peacetime-wartime conversion capabilities, the CCP has incorporated mobilization preparation into its overall national development plan. Its national defense mobilization is categorized into militia, air defense, economic, transportation, technological, information, reserve, and political mobilization. Simultaneously, the "National Defense Mobilization Committee" has been established as a deliberative and coordinating body responsible for national defense mobilization. The committee has a chairman and vice-chairmen, concurrently held by leaders of the State Council and the Central Military Commission, respectively. Its main functions are to implement the active defense military strategy, carry out national defense mobilization work, and coordinate the relationships within national defense mobilization

[271] II. Human Resource Mobilization The militia and reserve forces are the main reserve combat forces of the CCP. The CCP adopts a conscription system that combines the militia and reserve forces, forming reserve forces and militia organizations that combine labor and military service. In order to improve the professional quality of the troops and build a professional and technical militia, the CCP adopts a joint training method with regular troops to improve the overall combat effectiveness of the militia. It also emphasizes that once war breaks out, the People's Liberation Army will be the backbone, and the militia and reserve forces will be the foundation for combat. The overall mobilization strength is of great importance

[272] III. Material Mobilization In order to reform past issues such as incompatibility with economic requirements and material mobilization, the CCP has incorporated the reform of the national defense mobilization system into the overall plan for national economic and administrative system reform in recent years. In October 2003, it promulgated its first administrative regulation, the "Civilian Sports National Defense Mobilization Act," which came into effect in 2004. This act stipulates that civilian resources can be requisitioned (or mobilized) according to national defense mobilization needs during wartime or special peacetime circumstances, demonstrating a significant improvement in its overall national defense mobilization capabilities

[273] Section 8 Military Exchanges Military exchanges are a key focus of the current implementation of the CCP's national defense policy

[274] In order to shake off the shadow of the "China threat theory" and establish a positive image for the People's Liberation Army, the CCP, guided by its overall foreign policy of "long-term stability, future-oriented, good neighborliness and friendship, and comprehensive cooperation," has comprehensively promoted exchanges and cooperation with various countries in politics, economy, military and people-to-people exchanges

[275] I. Exchange Strategy Actively promote relations with various countries in areas such as military diplomacy, arms sales, personnel training, and scientific research cooperation; the aim is to strengthen bilateral military-political ties, international standing, and assistance to the military development of Third World countries through military exchange and cooperation, and to absorb the modern military building concepts of the United States, Russia, and European advanced countries, in order to achieve the goal of establishing regional power. In the future, we will deepen military exchanges with various countries and expand the search for global strategic partners in order to break through the indirect containment strategy of the United States

[276] High-level overseas visits (I) High-level Party and government officials

[277] Military leaders: More than 50 military leaders, including Cao Gangchuan and Xu Caihou, led more than 40 delegations to more than 90 countries, including the United States, Russia, the United Kingdom, France, Germany, Japan, and South Korea, for military visits

[278] III. Foreign Visits (I) High-Ranking Foreign Party and Government Officials

[279] ii) Foreign military leaders: More than 70 countries and more than 110 delegations visited, with Vietnam having the most, followed by Pakistan, Thailand, and France

[280] III) Military Exercise Observation: In August 2003, the CPC opened the Inner Mongolia Combined Tactical Training Base for the first time and invited military observers from more than 10 countries, including the United States and Russia, to observe its armored forces' live-fire combat exercises

[281] Page 60 --- 38 National Defense Report of the Republic of China, 2004, Part IV. Fleet Visits (I) Fleet Visits: In addition to enhancing the Chinese Navy's long-range navigation capabilities and experience, it also provides familiarity with foreign military affairs and united front functions, which is of great benefit to the development of maritime strategy

[282] In 2002, he embarked on his first global voyage, covering three oceans and lasting more than five months, visiting over ten countries including South Korea; in 2003, he visited Guam, Brunei, and Singapore

[283] It also participated in the "2003 Asian Maritime Exhibition", the "Western Pacific Naval Symposium" (WPNS) and mine clearance exercises held in Singapore; and visited one country in

[284] II) Visits by Foreign Ships: In 2002, more than 20 ships from countries such as France and Germany visited Qingdao, Shanghai, Hong Kong, and Zhanjiang nearly 10 times; in 2003, nearly 20 ships from countries such as the United States and the West visited, including ships from Pakistan and India, which conducted joint maritime search and rescue exercises with the CCP; from 2004 to the present, more than 10 ships from countries such as the United States and the United Kingdom have visited Hong Kong and Shanghai nearly 10 times, including ships from France, the United Kingdom, and Australia, which conducted joint maritime search and rescue exercises with the CCP

[285] V. Peacekeeping Missions Due to changes in its foreign policy, the CCP has gradually increased its participation in UN affairs. Since 1992, it has officially dispatched organized troops to participate in UN peacekeeping missions. In 2001, its Ministry of National Defense formally established the "Peacekeeping Affairs Office" to coordinate and manage the PLA's participation in UN peacekeeping operations. In 2002, it joined the UN peacekeeping standby mechanism and formed one UN standard engineering battalion (approximately 500 personnel), one UN standard medical unit (approximately 30 personnel), and two UN standard transport companies (approximately 60 personnel), ready to support peacekeeping operations at any time upon UN request

[286] Currently, there are over 50 military observers, over 80 civilian police officers, over 100 engineers, and over 40 medical personnel participating in nearly 10 peacekeeping operations, with an additional 2 personnel stationed at the United Nations Headquarters' Department of Peacekeeping Operations

[287] In 2003, peacekeeping engineering units (over 100 personnel) were dispatched to participate in UN peacekeeping missions in the Congo and Cambodia. In October 2004, peacekeeping engineering units and peacekeeping police bomb disposal teams (over 120 personnel) were also dispatched to Liberia and Haiti to participate in peacekeeping missions

[288] VI. Arms Sales In recent years, the CCP has continuously sold weapons and equipment to nearly 20 countries in Southeast Asia, the Middle East, and Africa. The items include: J-8 fighter jets, Y-8 transport aircraft, Z-9 helicopters, infantry fighting vehicles, anti-tank missiles, positioning radars, bulletproof vests, various types of aircraft parts, and various types of firearms and ammunition

[289] Its export arms characteristics --- Page 61 --- Part 1 Security Environment and Military Situation 39 points are: a wide variety of export types, increasingly advanced weapon performance, and strengthening regional influence through arms sales, etc

[290] Section 9 Impact on the Asia-Pacific Region In its “2002 National Defense White Paper”, the CCP emphasized that “promoting modernization, completing the reunification of the motherland, safeguarding world peace and promoting common development” are its three major tasks in the new century. The permanent tasks of the PLA, as stipulated by its constitution and national defense law, are "strengthening national defense, resisting aggression, realizing the modernization of national defense, preventing division and reunification of the motherland

[291] In order to modernize its national defense, the People's Liberation Army has continuously invested a large amount of national defense budget, invested in strengthening the military through science and technology, and purchased various advanced weapons from advanced countries. Its intention to continuously expand its military power has led Asia-Pacific countries to regard it as the main source of military threat in the region

[292] I. The Impact of the PLA's Military Development on Neighboring Countries The PLA's modernization of national defense, in addition to comprehensively enhancing the capabilities of its navy, air force, and Second Artillery Corps, has also included the establishment of rapid response forces, giving the PLA long-range combat capabilities. In recent years, it has continuously conducted military exercises in surrounding waters, causing suspicion and unease among neighboring countries, who have increased their defense spending and purchased various weapons and equipment to enhance their combat capabilities

[293] The United States still regards the CCP as its primary potential strategic competitor; in response to the CCP's military threat, Japan has shifted its defense focus from defending the north to addressing threats from the west and is considering initiating an aircraft carrier development program; Southeast Asian countries are also strengthening multilateral talks through security mechanisms such as the ASEAN Regional Forum (ARF) to counterbalance the CCP

[294] To dispel the shadow of the "China threat theory," the CCP is strengthening economic cooperation and military exchanges with countries in the region. The situation in the Asia-Pacific region appears calm on the surface, but undercurrents are surging beneath

[295] II. The Impact of the CCP's Military Situation on Cross-Strait Security The 16th National Congress of the CCP included the "New Three Sentences on One China" (Note 5) for the first time, which is the latest definition of the "One China Principle" and emphasizes that "the mainland and Taiwan belong to one China." However, in reality, the CCP has been continuously increasing its missile deployments in the region, attempting to intimidate Taiwan with military force

[296] Furthermore, the CCP has repeatedly stated that the "Taiwan issue" cannot be prolonged indefinitely and emphasized that "resolving the Taiwan issue will inevitably lead to war," clearly revealing the CCP's "two-pronged approach of peace and war" and its ambition to "promote unification by force

[297] The CCP's current military modernization process is facing problems such as low quality of officers and soldiers, insufficient personnel training, difficulties in integrating weapon platforms, and weak foundation for joint operations among the three services. However, according to the U.S. Department of Defense's report on the military power of the People's Republic of China, the CCP's actual defense budget is more than what has been published, estimated to be 3 to 5 times the published budget. With such a huge amount of money, the CCP has continuously developed and procured new weapons and equipment, upgraded the performance of existing weapons and equipment, strengthened personnel training, and integrated the combat capabilities of its troops, resulting in a significant increase in its military strength

[298] Furthermore, the PLA's training increasingly emphasizes technology and joint operations, and its overall military buildup is moving towards the goal of "quick victory in long-range combat and decisive victory in the first battle." The threat to us has shifted from quantitative superiority to qualitative competition

[299] With the increasing military strength of the Chinese Communist Party and its growing sense of urgency in resolving the "Taiwan issue," the threats to the security of the Taiwan Strait deserve the vigilance and concern of the people of Taiwan

[300] Under the guidance of the Central Committee, the CCP conducts military exchanges and technical exchanges with various countries, actively participates in international peacekeeping operations, joint counter-terrorism and maritime search and rescue exercises, and observes joint military exercises with foreign militaries. It also assists in the military development of Southeast Asian and Third World countries, draws on the modernization concepts of the United States, Russia and European countries, and strives to achieve its goal of establishing regional power

[301] In recent years, the CCP has adopted a proactive approach to military exchanges with the United States, focusing on constructive cooperation and jointly striving for political, military, and economic interests in the Asia-Pacific region

[302] However, on the Taiwan issue, since the CCP still does not give up on resolving the issue by force, and the US has also stated that it insists on the arms sales agreement to Taiwan, coupled with the US's continued doubts about the PLA's future strategic intentions, the future military relations between the two sides remain to be seen

[303] Taiwan is located in the center of the island chain defense line in the Asia-Pacific region, forming a strategic hub for the crescent-shaped defense line of the Asian continent. It can control the southeastern coast of the Chinese mainland, serving as a key springboard for the CCP to develop its maritime strategy, break through the first island chain strategic defense line, and enter and exit the Pacific Ocean. It is also the forefront of Japan's southern edge maritime defense strategy, and has the function of protecting and relying on the maritime interests of the United States and Japan (the situation in the Taiwan Strait is shown in Figure

[304] Due to its strategic location, it offers the convenience of projecting military power into the surrounding oceans and the advantage of preventing the PRC from entering and exiting the Pacific Ocean. It also plays a balancing role in the strategic interests and interactions of the United States, Japan, and the PRC in the Western Pacific, and can serve as a lever for the stability and development of the Asia-Pacific region

[305] Figure 1-5 Taiwan Strait Situation Map Aleutian Islands Section 1 Western Pacific Situation Indian Ocean Second Island Chain 165 degrees East Longitude Alaska Peninsula Honolulu Midway Cut off Projecting military power Guam Taiwan is located in the center of the first island chain in the Western Pacific

[306] Republic of China 2004 National Defense Report The situation in the Asia-Pacific region is currently a focus of global attention and US strategic attention. Among them, the Taiwan Strait situation remains a major factor affecting the situation in the Western Pacific due to the ongoing political differences between the two sides and the unresolved military standoff

[307] Following the 16th National Congress of the Communist Party of China, which completed the generational transition, Jiang Zemin resigned as Chairman of the Central Military Commission, and Hu Jintao succeeded him. Hu Jintao has consolidated power in the party, government, and military, and is expected to fully control the CCP regime in the short term and dominate future policy direction

[308] Following China's presidential election, the CCP has recently engaged in frequent overseas visits by its party, government, and military leaders, continuously demanding that countries take a stand on the "One China" principle and creating an atmosphere of "peaceful rise" to squeeze China's diplomatic space. It has also taken retaliatory measures in response to the visit of Singaporean dignitaries to Taiwan. Furthermore, to achieve its goal of "preventing Taiwan's independence in peacetime and achieving long-term unification," in addition to ongoing military preparations, it is now actively conducting research and training in non-military "three-pronged warfare" (public opinion warfare, psychological warfare, and legal warfare) against Taiwan. Simultaneously, it deliberately obstructs Taiwanese businesspeople with differing stances, manipulating their political positions. Recently, it has further expanded its use of official, unofficial, and semi-official media to release both factual and false intelligence, attempting to influence the morale of China's military and civilians

[309] In recent years, the People's Liberation Army (PLA) has been conducting intensive joint military exercises along the southeastern coast of Taiwan. Its ships, aircraft, and fishing vessels have also been expanding their operational range, deliberately compressing the depth of our early warning system. Given the current lack of a military mutual trust mechanism between the two sides, military conflict is highly likely to occur due to miscalculations, friction, or overreactions. The people of Taiwan should be vigilant, raise their awareness, and build consensus to safeguard national security

[310] II. US-China-Taiwan Relations The trilateral relationship between the US, China, and Taiwan has undergone significant changes due to factors such as Taiwan's political pluralism, adjustments to US Taiwan military exchanges and arms sales terms, and the inclusion of Taiwan in the scope of surrounding events under the "US-Japan New Security Framework

[311] Because the CCP, in its white paper on "One China and the Taiwan Question," explicitly listed three "if" conditions for using force against Taiwan: "if Taiwan is separated from China under any name," "if foreign forces occupy Taiwan," and "if the Taiwan authorities refuse to talk indefinitely," the CCP considers these factors to be interference in China's internal affairs. Therefore, the CCP will accelerate its internal unification efforts against Taiwan, which will pose a serious challenge to our country's survival and security

[312] Furthermore, following China's presidential election, the CCP has frequently interacted with high-ranking US officials, repeatedly demanding that the US halt arms sales to China and refrain from conveying misinformation. For example, during the visits to China by US National Security Advisor Rice and Pacific Commander Fargo in July, high-ranking CCP officials expressed strong dissatisfaction regarding cross-strait issues and arms sales. In early August, Hu Jintao, during a meeting with US senators, even proposed repealing the "Taiwan Relations Act." And in late October, during US Secretary of State Powell's visit to China, his statements on the Taiwan issue... Remarks on this topic may have a certain impact on the situation across the Taiwan Strait

[313] Section 2 Development of Cross-Strait Situation Due to differences in political stances, the two sides of the Taiwan Strait have been separated and governed separately for more than 50 years. The CCP's refusal to abandon the idea of "using force to invade Taiwan" remains the main obstacle to the normalization of cross-strait relations

[314] I. Current Situation Across the Taiwan Strait On May 17, 2004, on the eve of the inauguration of the 11th President of China, the CCP issued a new statement on future cross-strait relations, emphasizing "five never" and "two paths". The two-pronged strategy and the "one China" proposition contained therein are not conducive to cross-strait exchanges and resumption of talks

[315] In order to carry out its "anti-independence and pro-unification" policy and win over the hearts and minds of the people, the CCP has actively invited Taiwanese civil society figures, groups, and business representatives to visit Taiwan, and has further expanded and intensified its exchanges with local people, hoping to improve the Taiwanese people's perception of the mainland and attempt to undermine our defenses

[316] In recent years, the influx of mainland Chinese into Taiwan through sham marriages, family reunification, illegal immigration, and work has made Taiwan's immigration control difficult and provided the CCP with opportunities to gather intelligence and carry out sabotage against Taiwan

[317] II. The CCP’s Strategy Towards Taiwan The CCP’s current strategy toward Taiwan largely follows the Deng-Jiang line, but its methods have become more flexible than before

[318] In diplomacy, it employs a strategy of "using the US to contain Taiwan" and continues to promote "one China" internationally. In politics, it continues the basic policy of "peaceful reunification and one country, two systems," but places greater emphasis on united front tactics that "place hope in the Taiwanese people." In economics, it seeks to deplete Taiwan's economy through the "three links." In cultural united front tactics, it invests heavily in "opposing independence and promoting reunification" around the world, intensifying its attacks on Taiwan's "gradual Taiwan independence" efforts. In psychological warfare, it uses conciliatory rhetoric to win over the hearts and minds of the Taiwanese people, attempting to undermine Taiwan's defenses

[319] Its strategy and measures toward Taiwan can be summarized as follows: (1) Diplomatic suppression: The CCP demands that all countries adhere to the "one China" position. All countries that establish diplomatic relations with it or visit it must first recognize it as the sole legitimate government of China and that Taiwan is part of China. The purpose is to prevent the internationalization of the Taiwan issue and to form a mainstream trend of one China in the international community

[320] In recent times, the opportunities to utilize the US in dealing with North Korea's nuclear weapons, Iraq, Afghanistan's reconstruction, international counterterrorism cooperation, and US-China (CCP) economic and trade relations have all depended on the US's assistance, with the US strategy of containing Taiwan being the exchange condition

[321] II) Political Degradation: In order to diminish our political status, the CCP has been vigorously promoting "one country, two systems" as the best model for resolving the Taiwan issue. Its officials in charge of Taiwan affairs have repeatedly stated that if Taiwan implements "one country, two systems," it can continue to use the New Taiwan Dollar, retain its military, maintain its separate customs territory, and continue to use the original government structure, etc., in order to entice us to accept the system. Its real intention is to abolish our Republic of China national title, degrade us to a local government, and contain and block our international space for survival

[322] The recent CCP Taiwan Affairs Conference proposed three priority tasks for Taiwan: "excluding US interference in cross-strait affairs, actively promoting bilateral exchanges, and strengthening military preparations against Taiwan

[323] III) Economic and Trade Strategy: Since the implementation of the reform and opening up policy, the Chinese Communist Party has developed rapidly and has become an important economy in the world. In particular, after joining the WTO, the vast business opportunities have attracted global (foreign and Taiwanese) investment, which has greatly increased its foreign exchange reserves, enabling it to contribute to military spending and strengthen national defense

[324] Recently, China has intensified its efforts to attract Taiwanese businesses through "political investment," including granting political titles to Taiwanese businesspeople, relaxing entry and exit visa restrictions, and issuing investment protection cards to Taiwanese businesses. It also plans to allow some Taiwanese banks to establish branches in mainland China and for Taiwanese businesses to engage in the insurance and logistics industries, in order to boost their economic and technological strength. In July 2004, the CCP further formulated the "Long-Term Development Strategy for Guangdong-Taiwan Economic and Trade Cooperation," gradually expanding the scope of Taiwanese investment in Guangdong. At the "2004 Cross-Strait Relations and Economic and Trade Exchange Forum" held in Hangzhou, Zhejiang, the Taiwan Affairs Office of the State Council stated that negotiations could proceed as long as Taiwan explicitly accepts the "Three Links" as a domestic matter

[325] The current economic strategy toward Taiwan, in addition to incentive investment programs, attracting our high-tech industries, and strengthening its economic and technological capabilities to deplete our economic lifeline, also uses Taiwanese businesses to pressure our government to accept comprehensive cross-strait trade, postal, and transportation links. This shows that while the CCP emphasizes "separation of politics and economics" in its tactics, its strategic goal is to achieve "unification through economics

[326] iv) Cultural United Front: The CCP actively conducts cultural united front work against Taiwan, and invites scholars, Taiwanese businesspeople and people from all walks of life to visit the mainland in order to develop a diversified exchange and expand cross-strait academic and cultural exchanges; --- Page 67 --- Part 1 Security Environment and Military Situation 45 and continues to expand its recruitment publicity for Taiwanese students, launching various preferential measures for Taiwanese students to apply for admission, expanding cultural identity, accelerating the united front work against Taiwan, with the aim of "using the people to force officials and using the people to promote unification

[327] The psychological deterrent may employ the following methods: 1. Infiltration and sabotage: Intensify espionage and intelligence gathering in Taiwan, spread rumors to disrupt finance, and use illegal means to disturb public order

[328] vi) Infiltration and sabotage: In the past two years, there have been more than 330 attacks on our information and communication websites, most of which were cyberattacks by the CCP; in addition, the CCP has used various channels to carry out infiltration and smuggling, with more than 360 cases and more than 800 people involved to date; this shows that the CCP's infiltration and sabotage against us has never stopped, and its motives deserve special vigilance

[329] III. Future Development Although the CCP incorporated the "new three-stage theory" of the "One China" principle into its political report at the 16th National Congress for the first time, it still emphasized that it "absolutely does not promise to renounce the use of force against Taiwan." It is judged that its strategy toward Taiwan will continue to be based on the "separation of politics and economy" and the "two-pronged approach of peace and war." At the same time, it reiterated its anti-Taiwan independence stance, intending to use Taiwanese businesses to influence the mainland policy of our government, attempt to divide and create internal contradictions, and expand its united front work with Taiwan

[330] At this stage, the main approach is to conduct non-military "three wars" against Taiwan, hoping to win public support by using "public opinion warfare" as the main propaganda method; and to use "psychological warfare" tactics of intimidation and persuasion to undermine our morale; at the same time, to use "legal warfare" to formulate a "unification law," turning Taiwan into an internal problem, emphasizing the legitimacy of handling the Taiwan issue, and seeking a pretext for future military action against Taiwan

[331] a) Establishment of a dedicated unit: The Central Military Commission serves as the guiding and supervising unit, with the Military Science Academy under its jurisdiction responsible for theoretical construction and strategic research; the General Political Department is responsible for overall planning and compilation

[332] Page 68 --- 46 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China (II) Testing the "Three-War" System through Annual Exercises

[333] This includes incorporating the "three wars" into the training process to verify the operation of the political warfare and support system, and to review the application of tactics and methods; and by convening the "Military Political Work Theory Seminar", to promote the reform and innovation of military political work

[334] Recently, it has incorporated the "three wars" into its exercises and compiled a "concise manual" for grassroots officers and soldiers to anticipate and respond to potential problems in actual combat, demonstrating that its development direction is becoming increasingly refined and closer to actual combat

[335] III) Integrate relevant talents: Existing political work professionals such as psychological warfare, information, law, special dialects and foreign languages are all included in the personnel management system. In peacetime, they are used for planning, utilization and talent recruitment and training. In wartime, they can be quickly mobilized and professional teams can be formed

[336] IV) Activating the use of non-military "three war" capabilities against Taiwan: Through diplomacy, intelligence networks, local media, etc., the actions of making statements, deceiving the enemy, and intelligence strategies against us will gradually become more frequent and diverse, with the aim of influencing our intelligence assessment, policy implementation, and social stability

[337] Given that the US military effectively used "three wars" (military, civilian, and military operations) to achieve its intended combat objectives in the two wars against Iraq, Kosovo, and Afghanistan, and considering that future military action against Taiwan would inevitably elicit different reactions from the international community, the CCP has accelerated its research into non-military "three wars" against Taiwan. It intends to use international law to justify its military actions in order to eliminate the negative image of its militaristic tendencies in the international community

[338] Section 3 The CCP's Military Capabilities Towards Taiwan In order to build modern military capabilities, the CCP's military construction is guided by the policy of "military affairs reform with Chinese characteristics." In addition to actively adjusting the military system and developing high-performance weapons and equipment, it has become more stringent in its requirements for military construction guidance, combat tactics development, cadre quality, and troop training, in order to achieve the national defense modernization goal of "strengthening the military through science and technology and promoting training through science and technology

[339] I. Traditional combat capabilities (I) Air strikes: The Chinese Communist Party's air force and naval aviation forces have more than 1,000 aircraft types that pose a significant air combat threat to us

[340] Among them, the "Su Kai series" fighter jets have excellent performance, with a combat radius of 600 to 810 nautical miles. They can carry missiles for visual range attacking ships, anti-radiation missiles, and precision land attack missiles. Currently, they are being added through both foreign purchases and domestic production. In future operations against Taiwan, they will be used to seize air and sea control

[341] II) Airborne Operations: In recent years, the PLA has conducted targeted training in response to the PLA's defense operations, which emphasizes "defending in designated areas, fighting decisive battles on the beachheads, and resisting the enemy in depth." The PLA has also strengthened combat exercises such as airborne seizure of airports, river crossing attacks, and mobile blocking to enhance the mobility and assault capabilities of its airborne troops

[342] III) Surface Strike: Primarily the East China Sea Fleet, with the North and South China Sea Fleets as support; currently, it is actively upgrading existing Luda-class ships and continuing to purchase Sovremenny-class destroyers, Sunburn extended-range supersonic attack ship missiles, Club-class submarine-launched attack ship missiles, SA-N-12 air defense missiles, Lightning-class missile boats from Russia, and developing phased radar, vertical launch and large-area air defense weapons, supersonic and long-range attack ship missiles, etc.; if combined with naval aviation forces, it can enhance its air, surface and underwater coordinated combat capabilities, and carry out sea control and interception against us. When fighting against a water blockade, it can rapidly increase its long-range strike capability

[343] The Chinese Communist Party has recently been actively upgrading and equipping its conventionally powered submarines by introducing advanced technologies. In the future, it will rely mainly on new types of submarines such as the improved Ming-class, Song-class, and Kilo-class, which will greatly enhance its underwater attack capabilities. If combined with nuclear-powered submarines, it will only need to use a portion of its forces in wartime to carry out a long-term maritime blockade against China

[344] Furthermore, most of its existing surface ships and submarines are capable of mine-laying; and civilian commercial and fishing vessels can also perform mine-laying tasks with slight modifications. If conventional and unconventional mine-laying methods are used in combination during wartime, it will pose a great threat to our country's maritime shipping

[345] Regarding unconventional landings, their existing merchant ships can carry multiple organized infantry divisions and suitable motorized fishing vessels from Jiangsu, Zhejiang, and Fujian provinces, totaling approximately 400,000 armed troops and modified shipborne artillery for unconventional landings

[346] vi) Joint Operations: In recent years, due to breakthroughs in satellite, communications and information technology, the PLA's command and communication capabilities have grown significantly. In accordance with the guiding principle of "no joint operations without war", the PLA has been conducting continuous exercises on amphibious landing operations, mainly with the troops of the Nanjing, Guangzhou and Jinan Military Regions responsible for joint operations. The main purpose is to strengthen the command, control, communications and intelligence mechanisms of the three services in joint operations, and to accumulate experience in the command and operation of amphibious landing operations

[347] vii) Air defense operations: The PLA has the “Hongqi series” and “S-300 series” air defense missiles and other air defense systems, which can form a dense air defense network of aircraft, missiles and artillery that complement each other in terms of high and low altitudes and long and short ranges, thereby enhancing its joint air defense and strike capabilities at high, medium and low altitudes

[348] II. Asymmetric Combat Capabilities: In pursuit of surpassing traditional combat models, the PLA emphasizes that when facing a superior enemy, it should break through the limitations of the battlefield and non-battlefield, and strive for victory through various military or non-military means. This has been discussed and demonstrated by various units, and has had a certain degree of influence on their combat concepts

[349] In the future, we may employ several "killer" weapons for precision strikes, using "pressure point warfare" in conjunction with diverse asymmetric tactics to damage our political, economic, and military facilities, undermine our morale, and achieve maximum results at minimal cost

[350] The methods include: (1) Space threat: In recent years, the PRC has been actively developing various types of reconnaissance and early warning satellites. In the future, if military operations against Taiwan are carried out by using space command, control and surveillance systems to launch long-range precision strikes to paralyze the enemy, it will break away from traditional combat thinking. By using surprise attacks and paralysis to directly target the command center and strike command, control, communication and intelligence points, it will have a great impact on our subsequent combat capabilities

[351] Currently, China has deployed approximately 610 tactical ballistic missiles, with a strike range sufficient to cover the entire island of Taiwan. Furthermore, it is planning to develop medium-range ballistic missiles with even longer ranges. If a forward deployment is implemented with the coastal area of Zhejiang to the east of Anhui as the operational theater, its range could not only cover the entire island of Taiwan but also extend to major U.S. naval and air forward operating bases in Okinawa, Japan, and South Korea. In the event of a sudden armed conflict, China could launch multi-level, multi-directional, non-linear saturation attacks against any neighboring hostile country (the area covered by China's missile threat is shown in Figures

[352] III) Cruise missiles: In recent years, the CCP has been actively developing land-attack cruise missiles. The land-based version will be deployed in 2005, and sea-based and air-launched derivatives will also be developed. Its performance is similar to that of the US Navy's Tomahawk cruise missile. In the future, it can take advantage of its low-altitude cruise characteristics that make it difficult to detect, and its accuracy that is superior to ballistic missiles, to launch multi-directional and centripetal precision strikes against important strategic targets

[353] Unmanned attack vehicle systems possess characteristics such as homing capability, bandwidth, and long-range endurance, posing a threat to our air defense systems

[354] Furthermore, this system can also attack early warning and search radars, disrupt their signal transmission, and delay the response capabilities of air defense missiles or anti-aircraft gun fire control systems, creating favorable conditions for them to seize air superiority in the early stages of combat, which will inevitably cause serious damage to our air defense system

[355] V) Computer Warfare: In the field of cyber attacks, the CCP is currently studying the implantation of viruses into enemy networks. Although the technology is not yet mature, it can accumulate experience and capabilities in infiltrating enemy information network systems over a long period of time. Based on its related tactics and strategies, it should already possess considerable technical expertise

[356] Future --- Page 71 --- Part 1 Security Environment and Military Situation 49 Figure 1-6 Schematic map of the area covered by the CCP's missile threat (map omitted) Dongfeng-11 and its improved versions are deployed in Fujian and Jiangxi Provinces Dongfeng-15 and its improved versions are deployed in Jiangxi Provinces 600 km 300 km Dongfeng-11 Dongfeng-11 improved version Dongfeng-15 Dongfeng-15 improved version Within ten years, it may have the ability to use ballistic missiles, various types of artillery shells, and rockets to launch new electromagnetic pulse warheads

[357] The CCP currently aims to build an information warfare force. If a crisis breaks out in the Taiwan Strait, it could seize electromagnetic control through "non-lethal weapons" such as electromagnetic pulse missiles, which would seriously affect our command, control, communications, and intelligence systems. (VII) Electronic Warfare: Actively develop one missile (anti-radiation missile) and three aircraft (electronic countermeasure aircraft, helicopters, and drones), and combine them with the electronic warfare units of various military branches to form a three-dimensional, flexible, and diverse electronic warfare combination, with the aim of interfering with and suppressing our command, control, communications, and intelligence systems in the early stages of combat. (viii) Special Forces: The seven military regions and the Xinjiang and Tibet military regions have special forces brigades with the capability of rapid land and air mobility and amphibious landing (cushioned ships, ground effect vehicles). They can carry out three-dimensional infiltration and raids behind enemy lines, and can also cooperate with the navy and air force to enhance the ability to conduct raids behind enemy lines

[358] In recent years, the special forces have been gradually expanding their airborne units and strengthening airborne training in various terrains and adverse weather conditions. They have also purchased various airborne equipment, airborne transport vehicles, and unmanned attack vehicles to enable special forces to quickly paralyze the enemy's command, control, communication, and intelligence capabilities and to seize the initiative in conflict zones

[359] ix) Mobility: The PLA's railway, highway, airport and port infrastructure has been renovated and can take into account the operations of the southeastern coastal battlefield. Its ground railway and highway network and sea and air transportation lines are becoming increasingly dense, which is conducive to improving the PLA’s mobility

[360] Furthermore, with the goals of "integrated operations across the three services, networked intelligence systems, automated command systems, and concealed weapon positions," and in line with the strategy of "focused investment and integration of peacetime and wartime operations," the airports along its southeast coast have all been renovated; the expansion projects of existing second-line bases and deep-water piers along the southeast coast are being implemented gradually to aid battlefield operations

[361] Railways: Recently completed 20 railways in Shanxi, Sichuan, Guangdong, Gansu, Inner Mongolia, Jiangsu, Shanghai, Hunan, Hong Kong, Shaanxi, and Heilongjiang; currently under construction are more than 20 railways in South China, North China, Central China, Northwest China, and Southwest China, which are key areas of railway construction for the CCP. At the same time, the technical transformation and speed-up of existing lines and the separation of passenger and freight lines on busy lines are being accelerated, inter-provincial corridors are being improved and the railway network structure is being strengthened, the western railway network is being expanded, and the main trunk line network is being strengthened based on the "eight vertical and eight horizontal" (Note 8) to gradually build a rapid intercity system to enhance its military transport capacity

[362] It is projected that by 2010, all cities with a population of over 500,000 will be connected by high-speed roads, with a total length of approximately 1.8 million kilometers, including over 30,000 kilometers of expressways. In response to the widening economic development gap between the east and west, a new pattern of coordinated development with interconnected, complementary, and mutually beneficial economies among the eastern, central, and western regions will be proposed

[363] Part 1 Security Environment and Military Situation 51 4. Airports: Completed renovation (expansion) of more than 40 airports including Longtian and Zhangzhou; and built or abandoned more than 10 airports

[364] Section 4. The CCP's Military Deterrence Against Taiwan The CCP has not yet pledged to renounce the use of force against Taiwan. Its military deterrence against Taiwan focuses on achieving a "swift victory in long-range warfare and a decisive victory in the first battle," actively developing technologies such as aerospace, lasers, information, and automation. With the assumption of preventing external intervention in the Taiwan Strait, it is accelerating the construction and deployment of long-range attack weapon systems for its navy, air force, and Second Artillery Corps, leveraging the joint combat capabilities of the three services, hoping to achieve a decisive victory in the first battle in the Taiwan Strait. Therefore, the strength of its military deterrence against us will gradually increase

[365] I. Possible Timing for an Attack on Taiwan: Considering its economic growth and political stability, the CCP is unlikely to initiate a war in the Taiwan Strait in the short term unless there are unforeseen circumstances

[366] However, the CCP's use of force against Taiwan is influenced by complex factors such as the international political environment, the domestic political and economic situation, and the interdependence between the two sides. At this stage, the CCP will still focus on observing the development of our policies

[367] II. Possible Action Plans The PLA's primary objective in any future attack on Taiwan will be a surprise attack, employing a strategy of "minimum damage, high efficiency, rapid strike, and swift victory

[368] If we compare the potential growth of military power on both sides of the Taiwan Strait around 2006, in terms of the new generation of main combat forces, our navy, air force, and ground forces, although slightly inferior in number, still have a slight advantage in manpower and the quality of weapons and equipment. However, the ROC Armed Forces will also be at an absolute disadvantage in space, missiles, and submarines

[369] As the PLA's combat capabilities continue to improve rapidly, if the ROC military fails to effectively transform its capabilities, by 2006, the military advantage in the Taiwan Strait will gradually shift towards the PRC. If the situation continues to deteriorate, after 2008, an imbalance in military power between the two sides will occur, which will be extremely detrimental to our national security

[370] Based on the current situation of the CCP's military expansion and reference to US research data, if the CCP takes military action against Taiwan in the future, its possible options include: (1) Military deterrence: such as testing and displaying 'killer weapons', test-firing missiles in the waters surrounding Taiwan, directly assembling a large number of troops and conducting joint amphibious landing exercises to seize outlying islands, with the aim of increasing military pressure on Taiwan and threatening to achieve the goal of unification

[371] II) Non-contact warfare: such as non-military "three wars", cyberattacks, financial sabotage, decapitation strikes and electronic warfare, long-range precision strikes and special operations to attack and control important military and political targets, etc., with the aim of economic blockade, financial sabotage, division of public opinion and creating internal turmoil, paralyzing the command, control, communication and intelligence system of the national army, thereby achieving the goal of "containing independence and promoting unification

[372] III) Blockade Warfare: Blockade warfare will be carried out against our exports, procurement of important consumer goods, the Taiwan Strait, important ports, external shipping lanes or outlying islands, etc., with the aim of weakening our people's morale, cutting off our economic lifeline, and deteriorating our living environment, so as to achieve the goal of "stopping independence and promoting reunification

[373] iv) High-intensity strikes: Concentrate a large number of elite troops and weapons from the three armed forces to suppress, destroy, paralyze and devastate important military and political targets on the island with devastating firepower, in order to achieve the goal of quick victory and first-strike advantage, and to successfully occupy the entire island

[374] Considering the aforementioned possible options for the PLA to invade Taiwan, and taking into account the CCP's intentions, desired effects, and international situation, the most likely actions are nothing more than three options: a deterrent occupation of outlying islands, a high-intensity missile surprise attack and long-range precision strike, and a long term air and sea blockade to paralyze the region

[375] It is characterized by escalating pressure, displaying force, and flexible application of tactics. It primarily focuses on political, economic, and psychological attacks, emphasizing the combined use of conventional and unconventional methods, as well as military and civilian means. Its distinctive feature is achieving maximum political objectives with minimal casualties

[376] Military exercises to exert pressure: Using psychological warfare tactics, a large number of troops are concentrated along the coast to conduct drills and displays of firepower, or even seize an outlying island. The media is used to stir up tensions in the Taiwan Strait, induce psychological panic, and force us to submit to their will

[377] Complete blockade and suffocation: Designate the island's sea and airspace as no-navigation zones or declare a blockade; directly lay mines in our ports, waterways and sea areas with aircraft and submarines; and carry out a continuous and comprehensive blockade to suffocate us and force us to seek peace

[378] It possesses the characteristics of surprise attack, long range, precision, non-contact and nonlinearity, and mainly focuses on the paralysis and destruction of surveillance and command systems, political and economic command centers and combat power nodes, emphasizing the disintegration of combat power rather than annihilation

[379] Joint Precision Strike: Using tactical missiles and cruise missiles, unmanned vehicles and joint strike forces, single, combined or alternating joint precision strikes will be carried out against our operational sequence or key combat points of the three armed forces to destroy our counterattack capabilities and annihilate our manpower, so as to facilitate our three-pronged invasion

[380] III) Strategic Offensive: At present, the PLA is not strong enough to launch a three-pronged attack on Taiwan. However, with the upgrading of equipment, joint combat exercises and the improvement of overall combat power, future operations may use a large number of advanced weapons to launch surprise attacks, quickly paralyze our combat power, and accelerate the pace of operations. It will also deploy its forces in a full-scale offensive posture and then carry out strategic offensives on the outlying islands and the main island according to its combat objectives

[381] Considering factors such as the military buildup for Taiwan over the past three years (some lethal weapons and related combat capabilities cannot be completed in the short term), and taking into account the direction of Taiwan's independence, they have realized that they cannot launch a full-scale military invasion of Taiwan in the short term

[382] Taking advantage of meetings with foreign guests, they demanded that all countries express their support for the One China principle and refrain from official exchanges with Taiwan; at the same time, they strongly criticized the US arms sales policy to Taiwan, urged it to strictly abide by the three communiqués, sever military exchanges between Taiwan and the US, and reviewed the strategy of "containing Taiwan through the US

[383] In the future, the CCP's military strategy toward Taiwan will not only possess the military capabilities for sustained deterrence, partial blockade, and limited joint strikes, but will also intensify its psychological warfare against Taiwan, including "three wars," amplifying internal antagonism, and blurring the lines between friend and foe. All of these are worthy of our attention and early response

[384] Since 1999, it has been changed to "New Three Strikes and Three Defenses", as stated in the 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China: "Strike stealth aircraft, strike armed helicopters, strike cruise missiles"; "Defend against precision strikes, defend against electronic interference, defend against reconnaissance and surveillance

[385] Note 4: “Parasitic satellite” is a type of small killer satellite. After entering orbit, it can automatically track enemy satellites (usually reconnaissance satellites) operating in low Earth orbit. Using its built-in propulsion system, it attaches to the surface of the enemy satellite by tailing or head-on docking, completing the process of parasitizing the enemy satellite. Afterward, it can destroy its software and hardware functions, causing the parasitized host to become ineffective due to functional paralysis

[386] On November 8, the CCP held its 16th National Congress. Its political report continued the Taiwan policy since the 14th National Congress and included for the first time the "Three New Sentences on One China": "There is only one China in the world. The mainland and Taiwan belong to one China. China's sovereignty and territorial integrity are indivisible

[387] Among them, the so-called "five never" are: "We (the CCP) will never compromise on our position of adhering to the one-China principle, we will never give up our efforts to strive for peaceful negotiations, we will never change our sincerity in working with our compatriots in Taiwan to seek peaceful development across the Taiwan Strait, we will never waver in our will to resolutely safeguard national sovereignty and territorial integrity, and we will never tolerate 'Taiwan independence

[388] It also emphasized that “two paths lie before the Taiwanese authorities: one is to pull back from the brink, stop ‘Taiwan independence’ separatist activities, acknowledge that both sides of the Taiwan Strait belong to one China, and promote the development of cross-strait relations; the other is to go its own way, attempt to separate Taiwan from China, and ultimately play with fire and get burned.”

[389] Note 8: "Eight Vertical" refers to the Beijing-Shanghai, Beijing-Guangzhou, Beijing-Kowloon, Beijing-Harbin, Datong-Zhanjiang, Baotou-Liuzhou, Lanzhou-Kunming and the Eastern Coastal Corridor; "Eight Horizontal" refers to the Longhai and Lanxin land bridges, Shanghai-Kunming, the North-South Coal Transport Corridor, Beijing-Lanzhou, the Yangtze River Corridor, Nanjing-Xi'an and the Southwest Sea Access Corridor

[390] Page 77 --- Part Two: National Defense Policy and Military Strategy --- Page 78 --- 56 National Defense Report of the Republic of China, 2004 Summary of this Part I. The current national interests of our country are: to ensure the survival and development of the country, to safeguard the safety and well-being of the people, and to protect freedom, democracy and human rights

[391] National security objectives include: safeguarding national sovereignty and integrity, ensuring the country's sustainable development, preventing military conflict in the Taiwan Strait, and assisting in regional security and stability

[392] Its specific policy guidelines are: to expand public participation in national defense affairs, strengthen the concept of overall national defense, improve crisis management and response mechanisms, build a high-quality, efficient, and modern military, promote the integration of national defense and people's livelihood, cultivate national defense science and technology and weapons research and development, expand regional military and security cooperation, implement the conscription system, strengthen joint operations command mechanisms, improve the effectiveness of joint operations training, implement the nationalization and legalization of the military, and provide good care for the families of outstanding officers and soldiers

[393] Third, Taiwan's plan for promoting military mutual trust mechanisms can be divided into three stages: short-term stage – “mutual goodwill and seeking common ground while reserving differences”, medium-term stage – “establishing norms and consolidating mutual trust”, and long-term stage – “ending hostilities and ensuring peace”

[394] Fourth, the National Armed Forces' crisis management is in accordance with government policy guidance and the concept of "comprehensive security." It focuses on handling "military crises," prioritizing counterinstance and counter-instance, and utilizing joint command mechanisms to achieve the requirements of "crisis prevention, situation monitoring, emergency response, rapid handling, and prevention of escalation

[395] Following the principles of "military affairs innovation" and "military building guidance," various military building plans are being promoted to construct an effective counterforce that is of high quality, appropriate quantity, and strong combat power, and to achieve the goals of technological leadership, information and electronic advantages, joint interception, territorial defense, complete logistics, principles development, combat training and preparation, and decisive combat will

[396] The current international situation is filled with various complex, unpredictable and volatile risk factors, which certainly pose a considerable impact and challenge to our national defense security. However, the development of globalization and multilateral economic and trade cooperation also provides us with unlimited opportunities for national defense security preparation

[397] In view of this, in order to pragmatically face future challenges and proactively seize opportunities, the Ministry of National Defense, under the guidance of current national security policies and in accordance with national security objectives, safeguards national interests and formulates national defense policies to ensure national security

[398] Section 1 Current National Security Policy In the era of globalization and information technology, national security has evolved from a narrow military security concept to a broad, multi-faceted security concept, encompassing a comprehensive range of areas including national defense, diplomacy, cross-strait relations, finance, science and technology, psychology, environment, and crisis management

[399] Specifically, China's national interests include: (1) ensuring the survival and development of the country

[400] Article 1 of the Constitution of the Republic of China states: "The Republic of China is a democratic republic of the people, by the people, and for the people, based on the Three Principles of the People

[401] National interests are the core value of safeguarding national security. II. National Security Objectives To ensure the survival and development of the nation, safeguard the safety and well-being of all citizens, and protect freedom, democracy, and human rights, the current national security objectives include (as shown in Figure

[402] Figure 2-1 Section 2 National Defense Objectives Article 137 of the Constitution of the Republic of my country clearly states: "The national defense of the Republic of China is for the purpose of safeguarding national security and maintaining world peace"; Article 2 of Chapter 1 of the National Defense Law also clarifies: "The national defense of the Republic of China is for the purpose of leveraging the overall national strength, establishing national defense forces, and achieving the goal of safeguarding national security and maintaining world peace

[403] Section 3 National Defense Security Challenges and Opportunities I. Challenges

[404] I) Military Pressure from the CCP In recent years, the CCP's comprehensive national strength has grown rapidly, and its defense budget has grown by double digits every year. It has actively engaged in military building and preparation and various military reforms, which have greatly improved its overall military capabilities in both quantity and quality

[405] Since the CCP has consistently declared that it will not renounce the use of force against Taiwan, and is currently emphasizing the need to "strengthen military combat preparedness," its combat readiness deployments and annual exercises along the southeastern coast are increasingly targeted at Taiwan. In addition, the PLA's development in space warfare, information warfare, paralysis warfare, and non-traditional warfare has greatly increased the complexity and difficulty of the ROC Armed Forces' defense and countermeasures, posing multiple and severe challenges to the ROC's national defense security

[406] II) Modernization of the National Armed Forces Under the influence of the rapid development of modern military technology, the National Armed Forces face a military field full of changes and challenges. In the future, the National Armed Forces must be able to adapt to the combat mode of the information age, fully grasp the characteristics of the digital battlefield, and try to strengthen the construction of key combat capabilities in order to achieve the effect of gaining the upper hand and defeating the enemy with relatively weak military forces

[407] However, our country is inherently lacking in resources and its technological level is not as advanced as that of European and American countries. Therefore, we cannot simply follow the European and American model for military innovation. Instead, we must combine theory and practice and adopt a path that is suitable for our national strategy

[408] The urgent task for the National Army is to seize the opportunity for reform, accelerate the transformation of key combat capabilities, strive for new relative qualitative advantages, prevent the imbalance of military power in the Taiwan Strait, and ensure national security. Limited National Defense Resources Faced with the increasingly urgent military pressure from the CCP on our country, our national defense preparations must respond more actively

[409] In view of this, in order to effectively carry out the tasks of building up and preparing for war and maintain combat superiority, the National Armed Forces must not only continue to strive for a reasonable national defense budget, but also appropriately adjust resource allocation and prioritize the investment of major resources in key combat capabilities, so as to maximize the effectiveness of limited national defense resources

[410] iv) Security Threats from Terrorist Activities Since the "9/11 terrorist attacks" in the United States, which severely impacted the international peace environment since the post-Cold War era, the United States has immediately joined forces with international forces to continue to launch a global counter-terrorism operation, making "countering the spread of terrorism" a major issue in current international security affairs

[411] Therefore, based on the position of maintaining domestic counter-terrorism security and cooperating with the government in supporting international counter-terrorism efforts, the National Armed Forces must continue to build professional counter-terrorism capabilities to cope with possible future terrorist threats and challenges

[412] II. Opportunities Despite facing numerous threats and challenges, the National Armed Forces still have several opportunities for national defense and security in different aspects

[413] a) In terms of the international situation, Taiwan is located at the central hub of the first island chain in the western Pacific Ocean, controlling the two major strategic shipping routes in East Asia (the Taiwan Strait and the Bashi Channel), and serving as a lever fulcrum for the security order and stable development of the Asia-Pacific region

[414] my country's joint efforts with neighboring countries in the Asia-Pacific region to maintain peace and stability in the Western Pacific are not only in my country's national interest but also what countries in the region commonly expect

[415] ii) Regarding the situation in the Asia-Pacific region: After the collapse of the bipolar confrontation system of the Cold War, the overall military tension in the Asia-Pacific region has decreased, and the pursuit of economic development has become the main goal of Asia-Pacific countries

[416] III) Regarding the situation across the Taiwan Strait, although the current active economic, trade and social exchanges between the people on both sides are conducive to enhancing mutual understanding and easing hostility between the two sides, the military situation remains one of confrontation

[417] If our country can gradually expand the scope of substantive exchanges across the Taiwan Strait, effectively manage related measures, restore normalized dialogue and communication channels across the Strait, and seek to establish a "peaceful and stable interaction framework" and a "military mutual trust mechanism," we can avoid unnecessary misunderstandings and conflicts between the two sides, thereby maintaining stability in the Taiwan Strait region and benefiting the peaceful development of both sides

[418] iv) In terms of national defense and security, since many domestic private defense industry manufacturers have world-class technological research and development and manufacturing capabilities, this technological advantage is a valuable asset that can be utilized in the development of our national defense

[419] In recent years, China has made rapid progress in high-tech industries such as information and electronics, possessing not only top-notch R&D talent but also achieving world-leading status in many areas. Faced with constraints such as tight defense budgets and long acquisition timelines for advanced weapons and equipment, effectively utilizing its technological advantages in the development of the ROC Armed Forces' C4ISR and traditional weapon systems can enhance the ROC Armed Forces' combat effectiveness and significantly improve its combat power

[420] Based on comprehensive considerations of safeguarding national interests, achieving national security objectives, and responding to the international strategic situation, the basic objectives of Taiwan's current national defense policy are "prevention of war," "territorial defense," and "counter-terrorism and counter-terrorism

[421] On the one hand, we promote understanding through exchanges, resolve hostile intentions through mutual benefit, and gradually establish a military mutual trust mechanism; on the other hand, we continue to build a strong national defense consciousness, establish national defense capabilities, and strive for security cooperation in the Asia-Pacific region and international support, so that the enemy dares not easily start a war. Figure 2-2 Basic Objectives of National Defense Policy (Figure omitted: Basic objectives of national defense policy: counter-terrorism and change, territorial defense, and war prevention) --- Page 84 --- 62 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China

[422] a) Engagement: Support the government in promoting the “Cross-Strait Peace and Stability Interaction Framework”, and gradually build a cross-strait military mutual trust mechanism through the establishment of multiple channels for security dialogue and exchange, so as to promote the security and stability of the Taiwan Strait and achieve the goal of preventing war

[423] Establish security dialogue and exchange. In line with the government's overall foreign policy, expand institutionalized strategic dialogue and security cooperation platforms with Asia-Pacific countries, and combine regional security mechanisms to work together to maintain security and stability in the Taiwan Strait through regional and international cooperation

[424] Establish a cross-strait military mutual trust mechanism. Under the guidance of the government's overall cross-strait policy, and based on the past experience and tacit understanding of military self-restraint between the two sides, we should initially promote security cooperation in areas such as humanitarian affairs, maritime affairs, and counter-terrorism, gradually reduce mutual hostility, cultivate mutual goodwill and shared experience in cooperation, and, in order to avoid the emergence of conflict crises, jointly negotiate a "Code of Conduct for the Taiwan Strait" and pragmatically promote a "cross-strait military mutual trust mechanism" to promote stability and security in the Taiwan Strait

[425] II) Deterrence In order to be proactive and positive in the defense of Taiwan and Penghu, the military's combat capability preparation and building objectives are to construct basic defense capabilities of "effective deterrence and defensive defense" and develop defensive countermeasures, with the aim of preventing war and avoiding conflict

[426] The following actions have been taken: 1. Building basic defense capabilities. Enhancing Taiwan's self-defense capabilities is the primary condition for ensuring the peaceful status quo across the Taiwan Strait and a strong backing for equal dialogue between the two sides
