[765] II. Fund Operation Management (I) Based on the needs of business development, the following regulations have been added to and revised: “Accounting System for Production and Service Operation Fund of the Armed Forces”, “Accounting System for Housing Purchase Loan Fund of Armed Forces Officers and Soldiers”, “Regulations for Budget Preparation of Subsidiary Units of the Special Fund of the Ministry of National Defense”, “Key Points for the Preparation of Semi-annual Budget Settlement Reports of Subsidiary Units of the Special Fund of the Ministry of National Defense”, and “Key Points for the Preparation of Final Accounts of Subsidiary Units of the Special Fund of the Ministry of National Defense”, in order to improve the fund accounting system and strengthen the internal management of the fund

[766] Section 5 Policy Direction I. National Defense Budget A sound national defense organizational structure and operation model is an important link in ensuring national security. However, building a complete national defense system requires long-term planning and huge resource investment

[767] Our country has long been under a high level of threat from the enemy. All national defense policies are based on the premise of achieving the strategic goal of "effective deterrence and defensive defense." This means that improving the force structure, eliminating outdated weapons and equipment, increasing equipment readiness rate, and building a modern national defense force that is "high-quality, appropriately sized, and highly capable" to enhance overall national defense capabilities. This relies on the comprehensive development of "national defense planning and forecasting" to seek a reasonable force structure and understand the gaps and deficiencies in combat capabilities. It also requires providing concrete and compelling data for future national defense policy planning in order to secure an adequate national defense budget

[768] Part Four: National Defense Resources and Management Chapter Twelve: National Defense Material Resources National defense construction requires a wide range of material resources, covering weapons, equipment, supplies, land, and facilities, which are indispensable elements for forming tangible combat power. Under limited national defense resources, how to conduct appropriate and effective needs assessment, acquisition, allocation, and utilization depends on sound national defense material policies, planning, and management

[769] Section 1 National Defense Material Resources Planning National defense material resources planning takes into account "national defense security," "local development," and "people's livelihood needs." It utilizes policies such as "integrating civilian resources to develop national defense science and technology industries," "prioritizing domestic production of weapons and equipment," "domestic procurement as the primary method and foreign procurement as a supplement," "ensuring technology transfer in foreign procurement," and "utilizing cooperation, mutual entrustment, and entrusted operation when integrating civilian resources." It also expands cooperation with industry, government, academia, and research institutions to cultivate national defense science and technology industrial capabilities within the civilian sector, gradually to achieve the goal of national defense autonomy; and in response to the needs of national economic development, in conjunction with national land development and to maximize the effectiveness of land, without affecting the principle of building up and preparing for war, a comprehensive review of national defense land will be conducted. Based on the policy of "merging small camps into large camps" and "relocating non-essential camps and training grounds located in urban areas to foothills or suburbs", the consolidation of camps will be proactively reviewed, and vacant and unsuitable camps of the national army will be reviewed and released in a timely manner, so as to closely integrate people's livelihood with national defense construction, and to economically and effectively support combat needs

[770] Section 2. National Defense Industry. In line with the government's efforts to promote industrial and economic development, the Ministry of National Defense, in accordance with Article 22 of the "National Defense Act," which states, "Combining civilian resources to develop national defense science and technology industries, prioritizing the domestic production of weapons and equipment," integrates the development of related industries such as aviation, shipbuilding, vehicles, electronics, electrical machinery, information technology, mechanical engineering, chemicals, materials science, and environmental science. It effectively utilizes the inter-ministerial mechanism of the "National Defense Science and Technology Development Promotion Committee" to integrate the strengths of industry, government, academia, and research institutions to conduct research on cutting-edge national defense technologies and the development of advanced weapon systems. It also provides the industry with early-stage R&D budgets and industrial cooperation technology transfer quotas. Simultaneously, it combines national defense resource release policies to enhance... --- Page 178 --- 156 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China Strengthen national defense technology research and development and maintenance capabilities, cultivate national defense technology industry capabilities in the civilian sector, and achieve the goal of national defense independence and self-reliance

[771] I. Implementation Results (I) National Science and Technology Development Plan 1. Improve the coordination mechanism for the integration of national defense science and technology industry development In May 2003, the cross-ministerial integration and coordination mechanism of the "National Defense Science and Technology Development Promotion Committee" was reorganized and improved. The secretariat's business was upgraded from the Chung-Shan Institute of Science and Technology to the Ministry of National Defense. Through the "Industry" and "Academic" departments under it, the "National Defense Science and Technology Industry Development Plan" was formulated to integrate opinions from all parties on how to combine the needs of military building, improve the level of national defense science and technology, and promote the participation of domestic industries and academia in the independent research, development, production and maintenance of national armed forces' weapons and equipment. The plan was fully implemented and promoted to cultivate the national defense industry's capabilities in the civilian sector

[772] III) Aviation Industry Development Plan In October 2001, the Executive Yuan established the "Aviation Industry Development Steering Group" to coordinate the development of the military and civilian aircraft research and development, manufacturing, and maintenance industries. In July 2003, the "Aviation Industry Development Plan" was promulgated, with the Ministry of National Defense responsible for planning. The plan aims to drive the development of the civilian aviation industry based on the needs of military aviation, such as the domestic research and development and production of advanced training aircraft, the state-owned and private operation of military factories, and the strategic commercial maintenance of military aircraft. The plan has been actively implemented in accordance with the action plan

[773] VI) National Defense Industry Satellite System To build a domestic supply system for the national armed forces' military factories, the Ministry of Economic Affairs and the Ministry of National Defense jointly established the "Military-Private Industry Co-development Conference". Through open channels such as military product certification (repair) and research and trial production, nearly 230 qualified national defense industry manufacturers have been established as satellite factories of the national armed forces, capable of producing or repairing approximately 4,854 military product components and subsystems

[774] II. Policy Directions (I) Promoting Domestic Procurement and R&D of Military Products In order to encourage domestic high-tech companies to expand their participation in national defense technology R&D, in conjunction with the "National Defense Resources Release Policy", the Ministry of National Defense and the Ministry of Economic Affairs have completed the "Key Points for the Assessment of the Capabilities of the National Armed Forces to Obtain Domestically Produced Weapons and Equipment". In the future, all new weapons and equipment purchased by the National Armed Forces must be subject to industry integration by the Ministry of Economic Affairs, with domestic production as the preferred option

[775] II) Continue to promote the "release of national defense resources to foreign companies". Adhering to the policy principle that "domestic manufacturers are capable of producing and supplying, the national military will not build up its capabilities, nor will it purchase from abroad. When it is necessary to purchase from abroad, technology transfer will be implemented to enhance the self-sufficiency of the national defense technology industry", and taking into account the national defense budget that may be obtained in the future, the target amount of the release of national defense resources for 2003-2011 has been formulated (as shown in Figure 4-22), and the various promotion measures are planned as follows: 1. General military supplies (such as facility engineering, transportation, staple and non-staple food, fuel supplies, medical equipment and medical supplies, cleaning and other services, etc.) shall all be procured or leased domestically in accordance with the relevant provisions of the "Government Procurement Act"; for new weapons and equipment, all new military equipment requirements shall be planned and coordinated by the Ministry of Economic Affairs in advance, and the research and development and production of civilian enterprises shall be coordinated. When it is necessary to procure from abroad, technology transfer shall be implemented to enhance the self-sufficiency of the national defense technology industry; for existing weapons and equipment, non-core capabilities with lower alertness and combat readiness timeliness of the national armed forces shall be released, and measures such as "strategic commercial maintenance" and "national-private partnership" shall be used to fully promote various contracting work

[776] Future weapon and equipment maintenance will be carried out through "strategic commercial maintenance" via long-term contracts, releasing the work to qualified domestic manufacturers. The military procurement industrial cooperation mechanism will be utilized to prioritize the introduction of maintenance expertise. --- Page 181 --- 159 Part Four: National Defense Resources and Management Figure 4-22 Projected Growth Trend of Target Amount of Commercial Maintenance Released by the Republic of China and the Armed Forces from 2003 to 2011 NT$140 billion 1,220 1,218 NT$120 billion 1,187 1,211 1,157 1,106 1,117 1,128 1,046 NT$100 billion NT$80 billion 864 906 785 721 673 NT$60 billion 558 582 599 629 NT$40 billion NT$20 billion NT$0 billion 2003 2004 2005 2006 2007 2008 2009 2010 2011 Scale of Business Release Amount of Business Release Trend of Business Release Ratio Data Time: November 2004 (Republic of China Year

[777] iv) Developing military-civilian technologies. The Ministry of Economic Affairs will continue to implement the "Military-Civilian Technology Development Project," entrusting the National Chung-Shan Institute of Science and Technology to select technologies or products suitable for both military and civilian use based on national defense technology research and development needs. This will facilitate collaborative research and development with domestic manufacturers, accelerate the transfer of national defense technology research and development results to the civilian sector, and lead to the establishment of spinoff companies. The budget of the project will be expanded annually based on the results, in order to promote the deep integration of national defense technology into civilian industries and realize two-way exchanges between the military and the civilian sector

[778] Section 3 Defense Procurement The procurement of weapons and equipment by the National Armed Forces is based on "domestic purchases as the main method and foreign purchases as a supplementary method". For military products that can be produced, sold, supplied, researched and developed domestically, domestic products will be given priority in order to expand domestic demand and promote domestic economic development

[779] I. Implementation Results (I) Procurement was completed in accordance with the law and timely support was provided for war preparedness. Domestic procurement cases: 26,913 cases, with a procurement amount of NT$139.61133 billion

This allows for direct procurement of small amounts under NT$100,000 from private logistics operators, establishing a cooperative model that combines civilian and military capabilities

[782] Section 4 National Defense Land Use In recent years, with the rapid development of the domestic political and economic situation, the use of some military bases, military zone control, and the establishment of training grounds have impacted local development. Therefore, in order to balance "national defense security," "local development," and "people's livelihood needs," and in conjunction with the "Streamlining Project" for combat capability building and deployment, the Ministry of National Defense, without affecting the principles of combat readiness and the minimum national defense requirements, comprehensively reviewed the overall utilization efficiency of military land. Following the principles of "merging small camps into larger camps" and "relocating urban areas to suburban areas," the Ministry reviewed and released unsuitable or unplanned bases, ensuring a close integration of national defense and people's livelihood development to support the overall economic development of the country

[783] II. General Military Use Areas: Military land that does not directly affect military operations but is indispensable for military buildup and preparation, including camps, logistics facilities, military agencies and academies, and small training grounds that are not strategic or tactical key areas

[784] I. Implementation Results (I) Strengthening the handling of idle, underutilized and occupied public real estate In accordance with the resolution of the National Assets Management Committee: "urging all agencies to actively review idle, underutilized and occupied real estate and improve utilization efficiency", the National Army adheres to the principle of "balancing the situation and administering according to law", and combines troop deployment and streamlining policies to continuously investigate and handle the use of public and private land and occupied camps in the barracks

[787] Section 5: National Defense Acquisition Management. The scope of national defense acquisition management is aimed at the main weapon systems and equipment of the armed forces. Adhering to the spirit and logic of "system integration" and "professional division of labor", it aims to create a high-quality weapon system acquisition management environment for the armed forces through improvements in organization, operating regulations, and processes, as well as various operations such as project management, systems engineering, testing and evaluation, and overall logistical support. This will enable the acquisition of various weapon systems and equipment in accordance with legal procedures, within a set time, and with a reasonable budget, in accordance with operational requirements

[788] I. Implementation Results (I) Constructing a Logistics Strategy and Developing a Lifetime Management Mechanism Looking ahead to the future logistics development direction of the armed forces, we formulated a "Logistics Strategy Plan" that conforms to the national defense military strategy and the ten-year military building concept. We then guided the services to formulate logistics policies and plans, improved logistics organization and operational processes to enhance operational efficiency and meet the needs of future combat types and troops. We developed a "Full Lifetime Project Management Operation Manual" to establish the operational capabilities of the armed forces in the full lifetime management of acquisition projects. The goal is to meet combat needs on time, in quality, and within budget in the arms acquisition operations, and to continuously maintain combat capability until equipment is phased out

[789] Part Four: National Defense Resources and Management Chapter Thirteen: National Defense Technology Under the national defense technology planning that prioritizes technology, the National Armed Forces, in conjunction with the needs of future warfare, focus on the research and development of key weapons and cutting-edge technologies, and use the national defense technology industry to guide and assist the upgrading and transformation of civilian industries in order to enhance national competitiveness

[790] Section 1 National Defense Science and Technology Policy The National Defense Science and Technology Policy guides the research and development of various advanced weapons and equipment of the armed forces, and combines the strength of private enterprises, organizations and professionals to cultivate national defense science and technology capabilities in the private sector in order to achieve the goal of national defense self-reliance. The policy is as follows: I. Combining with the needs of future combat: In response to the requirements of future combat, actively enhance high-tech military capabilities of "early warning, rapid response and precise counterattack" in order to strive for and create favorable combat conditions

[791] Section 2 Research and Development Strategy To strengthen national defense science and technology research and development, establish an independent national defense collaborative research and development and production system, and implement the goal of national defense construction rooted in the people, so as to achieve the national defense objective of all citizens, the Ministry of National Defense has formulated a national defense science and technology research and development strategy, which is described below: 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China I. Strengthening Technological Leadership and Accelerating Forward-Looking Research Guided by the national defense science and technology policy, and in line with the future operational requirements of "actively enhancing high-tech weaponry with early warning, rapid response, and precise countermeasures based on the concept of technology leadership," we focus on the development and research of key technologies in information and electronic technology, electronic warfare, information warfare, and C4ISR systems. Through the "Academic Cooperation Project," we commission domestic research institutions to conduct research and development on forward-looking weapons and key technologies in aviation, missiles, electronics, chemistry, and materials. We also strengthen the research and development of cutting-edge technologies such as aerospace, advanced information electronics, stealth and smuggling, and nanotechnology to meet national defense needs

[792] Part Four National Defense Resources and Management Section Three Current Status of Implementation In the face of the challenges posed by the development of high-tech weapons by the PRC, the ROC Armed Forces are planning the main focus of its national defense technology research and development in four directions: "enhancing electronic and information warfare capabilities", "establishing a low-altitude missile defense network and anti-missile capabilities", "strengthening C4ISR capabilities", and "strengthening the security protection of important facilities". The main focus is on missile and electronic warfare weapon systems, in order to build an effective defensive and strategic deterrent force

[793] Currently, in terms of information warfare, the ROC Armed Forces have completed the development of systems such as "protection of external communication channels of information networks," "email security," "firewalls," "internal credential trust management," and "security protection function testing" to maintain the security of the ROC Armed Forces' information networks. Furthermore, it has begun phased development of communication security technologies and information protection work, including "technical development and equipment manufacturing of ROC Armed Forces communication security equipment," "establishment of preliminary technologies for the protection of national defense information systems," "establishment of preliminary technologies for real-time monitoring mechanisms and intelligent data flow controllers for national defense information networks," and "research and development of related technologies for ROC Armed Forces computer emergency response systems

[794] III. Major Weapon Systems (I) Air Superiority Weapons Given the increasing threat to our national security posed by the deployment of missiles by the PRC, the ROC Armed Forces must actively build a missile defense system

[795] Including: "Sonar buoys", "shipborne sonar systems", "underwater monitoring and verification systems" and "anti-torpedo decoy systems", etc., to enhance anti-submarine warfare capabilities. (III) Ground defense weapons The functional verification of the new generation Thunder 2000 artillery multiple rocket launcher system has been completed. Equipped with rockets of different ranges, it has the effect of long range and wide impact point dispersion

[796] IV. Key Technologies for National Defense Science and Technology: Hsiung Feng II Ship Attack Missile Launch Status (I) Several key technologies required for missile systems have been completed, such as: "Small liquid rocket and propellant storage and control system", which can provide attitude control for various types of missiles; and "Advanced warhead key technology", which has improved the shortcomings of insufficient energy and power of traditional warhead fragments

[797] V. Military-Civilian Integrated Technology (I) Science and Technology Projects In line with the government's industrial development strategy and industry needs, the National Chung-Shan Institute of Science and Technology has invested in the Ministry of Economic Affairs' science and technology research and development projects in the fields of "communication and optoelectronics", "mechanical and aerospace", "materials and chemical engineering" and "biotechnology and pharmaceuticals", with remarkable results

[798] In line with the "National Key Development Plan", the Council for Economic Planning and Development of the Executive Yuan approved the "Taoyuan Longyuan Mobile Communication Engineering Center" promotion plan in July 2002. The plan involves the construction of a third-generation wireless communication research and development center, which will be completed in 2005 and will begin operation and promotion in 2006. The center will assist the private sector in developing commercial wireless communication products and technology transfer, and will bring significant benefits to the development of wireless networks and communication electronics in Taiwan

[799] Section 4. Policy Direction In response to the threat from the CCP, the Ministry of National Defense will accelerate the development of precision-guided missiles to effectively deter enemy attempts to invade Taiwan; develop information and electronic warfare equipment that combines reconnaissance and attack capabilities to gain electromagnetic superiority; accelerate the development of anti-ballistic missile weapon systems based on existing missile development capabilities; focus on long-range engagement and countering overreaching targets, and prepare for the development of new multiple rocket launchers and mobile antiship missiles; and support the domestic defense technology industry to build capacity and achieve the goal of national defense for all citizens

[800] I. In terms of electronic warfare systems (I) Strengthen the electronic detection capabilities of the National Army, focusing on improving frequency coverage, expanding detection and identification capabilities, and building an integrated electronic detection network, especially emphasizing the detection capability of low-altitude stealth targets (cruise missiles

[801] III. In terms of major weapons (I) Air superiority weapons 1. Introduce weapon development technology with active radar guidance, detection and tracking, and calculation capabilities: Improve the existing Tien Kung II surface-to-air missile into a low-level anti-tactical ballistic missile system (including early warning system) with anti-electronic interference, active calculation of incoming missile flight trajectory and tracking capabilities; develop a new generation of fighter jet air-to-air (surface) missiles, precision strike weapon systems, electronic reconnaissance pods, enhance airborne mobile reconnaissance capabilities, develop fighter jet stealth technology, unmanned vehicles, infrared reconnaissance and tracking systems, etc

[802] Improve the electronic warfare system of the Ching-kuo fighter jet, enhance the composite radar to take into account both air-to-air and air-to-ground combat capabilities, improve the sliding rail launch of the Tien Chien II medium-range missile, enhance air superiority and possess complete ground attack capabilities to achieve long-range combat objectives

[803] In the future, the Ministry of National Defense will coordinate and integrate the resources of various ministries, utilize the advantages of domestic electronics and information industries, leverage the technological integration capabilities of the National Chung-Shan Institute of Science and Technology, actively promote large-scale collaborative research and development projects, and lead domestic manufacturers to compete for research and development and manufacturing opportunities. 174 National Defense Report of the Republic of China, 2004, Part 5: Important National Defense Policies (I) Legal System 176 National Defense Report of the Republic of China, 2004 [Summary of this article] I. [834] Modern warfare and modern national defense are no longer simply military acts, but comprehensive acts that involve the entire national strength, military strength, people's strength and spirit; and the security threats to the country are not limited to external military threats, but should also include natural and man-made threats from within. Therefore, establishing a comprehensive security concept is a basic concept and consensus that the entire nation must have after the country enters the 21st century

It fully utilizes the overall strength of the government, economy, military, psychology, and technology to strengthen national security

[838] III. Annual Training Based on the needs of national defense mobilization preparation, the Ministry of National Defense effectively integrated the two major mobilization mechanisms of "administrative mobilization" and "military mobilization" to conduct the "National Defense Mobilization Executive Supervisor Training Course" in 2004. The training was conducted in two sessions, with 40 participants in each session (20 military personnel and 20 administrative personnel). The first session was held in early August 2004; the second session was held in mid-August 2004, both at the Reserve Mobilization Management Office of the Ministry of National Defense Reserve Command. The school conducted a three-and-a-half-day training program, targeting mobilization staff officers from central ministries and various military branches (general) headquarters, as well as relevant business managers from various combat zones and county (city) governments. The training was conducted through academic lectures, professional course instruction, and war game simulations. By adopting a unified and centralized educational approach, the program aimed to strengthen mobilization reporting functions and related laws and procedures, lay a solid foundation for the knowledge and skills of mobilization officers at all levels, enhance the comprehensiveness of operational mobilization drills, and improve defense capabilities and coordination, thereby implementing national defense mobilization and combat readiness preparation

[839] IV. Mobilization Drills (I) Mobilization Support for Defense Operations Exercises To verify the appropriateness of the annual mobilization preparation plan and to enhance mobilization emergency response capabilities, each year, in conjunction with the "Han Kuang Exercise," relevant central ministries and county (city) governments and important public enterprises are invited to conduct war games or live-fire exercises: In 2002, 10 mobilization preparation test exercises were conducted, including oil, electricity, tap water, and reservoirs, which effectively enhanced emergency response capabilities

[842] Section 3 Promotion of National Defense Concepts To strengthen national beliefs and awareness of potential threats, and to establish the understanding that "national security is everyone's concern and national defense is everyone's responsibility," so as to build a consensus on the common life of all citizens, our government combines social, school, military education and mass media to promote the national defense concept, aiming to make all citizens pay attention to, support and participate in national defense

[843] The number of visitors increased from approximately 130,000 in 2002 to over 258,000 in 2003, demonstrating that the concept of national defense has gained enthusiastic response and support from the public through an entertaining and educational approach

Military Education Reform of the National Armed Forces: By concentrating limited educational resources, appropriately planning educational courses, establishing an evaluation system, and promoting lifelong learning, the aim is to cultivate officers with both literary and military skills and a global, scientific, and social perspective, thereby comprehensively improving the quality of officers at all levels of the National Armed Forces

Based on the principle of "fewer soldiers in peacetime and more soldiers in wartime," and in accordance with the military building guidelines of "streamlining standing forces and expanding reserves," the National Army has established a suitable and effective military mobilization system to make full use of the nation's human and material resources and strengthen mobilization and combat readiness. Chapter Sixteen Military Education Part Six Important National Defense Policies (II) Combat Readiness 193 In order to implement the policy of building a lean and efficient army and to establish a modern national defense force that is of high quality, appropriate quantity, and strong combat power, the National Army is actively promoting adjustments to its organization, manpower, and operational processes

[846] Section 1 Military Education Policy The military education improvement plan is based on the concept of "teaching for war and cultivating for use," and follows the strategic thinking of "fighting, talent, selection, and training" (i.e., what kind of war to fight, what kind of talent is needed, how to select and use talent, and how to cultivate talent). It combines the advantages of information and electronic systems, technological leadership, and the direction of building the military by improving the integration of the three services' weapon systems and enhancing the effectiveness of joint operations. Through professional training streams, it refines and adjusts the military education system at the basic, advanced, and higher education levels to achieve the goals of command and control and professional streams, thereby cultivating the national defense personnel needed for military building and war preparedness

Establishing a Military Education Consultation Mechanism In order to improve the military education decision-making mechanism, develop the vision of military academies, and combine the actual situation of the troops with the future needs of military building and combat readiness, the "National Armed Forces Military Education Committee" was specially invited to form a consultation and advisory mechanism for military education policies and development directions, so as to strengthen the military education system

[854] III. Selection and Examination for Master's and Doctoral Degrees: To cultivate high-quality officers and improve the overall quality of the armed forces, master's degree programs adopt a selection and training system, focusing on improving professional skills. Doctoral programs are based on the needs of the unit, determining the areas of expertise and application plans, with the goal of cultivating teachers, technology researchers, and senior professional staff, in order to implement the policy of "education for use." In addition, to encourage students' balanced development, graduating students with excellent comprehensive performance in four areas—academics, character, military affairs, and physical fitness—are allowed to apply for master's programs. Through planned training and pre-assignment mechanisms, officers with more diverse abilities are cultivated to effectively improve the quality of the armed forces' personnel

[855] Chapter 17 Combat Training In order to respond to the evolution of the strategic environment, the National Armed Forces, in accordance with the spirit of the National Defense Law and the "Refinement Plan", conduct all combat training with the goal of "implementing joint operations

[856] Section 1 Joint Operations Joint operations refer to operations carried out by two or more branches of the armed forces to perform a common mission and achieve the same operational objective. This type of operation has long been the mainstream of modern warfare

[857] In terms of the joint command system, it is divided into two levels: strategic decision-making and command, and strategic execution. In terms of the operational support system, it is divided into two levels: the political support coordination center and the war support coordination center (integrated coordination center for each service). The two systems achieve networked operational interaction through multiple communication networks and enhance the effectiveness of the joint operational command mechanism. In order to build on the results of the "Han Kuang 18 and 19 Exercises", to study and improve existing combat readiness issues, the focus was on verifying the organization and operation process and command and control capabilities of the improved and adjusted "Joint Operations Command Mechanism of the National Army", and testing the appropriateness of defense combat readiness preparation and the construction of national army combat power: "Han Kuang 20 Exercise" and related verifications were carried out from April to August

[858] The "Han Kuang 19 Exercise" validated the effectiveness of the ROC Armed Forces' training. The entire plan was implemented in three phases: Phase One "Joint..." The "Operational Plan Action" was validated on April 30, 2004, focusing on the implementation of various actions within the joint operational plan to support the step-by-step execution of the second phase, the "Joint Computer Wargame Simulation." This second phase, the "Joint Computer Wargame Simulation," marked the first time the ROC Armed Forces used a joint theater simulation system for the exercise. The scenario framework was based on the relative combat capabilities of both sides in 2006: the attacking force adopted a surprise attack mode, utilizing special operations, infiltration and sabotage, cyber warfare, missiles, air, submarine attacks, and the fifth column, combined with airborne (airborne) landings. The exercise aimed to estimate the cost of an invasion of Taiwan using the enemy's maximum deployable combat power, employing a broad and complex assessment of the enemy's intentions. The defending force, on the other hand, used the "ROC Armed Forces Joint..." Based on the overall framework of the "Operational Command Mechanism," it integrates the overall defense structure of Taiwan, Penghu, Kinmen, and Matsu with the comprehensive combat capabilities of the entire population. Focusing on maximizing operational effectiveness, it implements integrated joint operations, fully utilizing logical thinking models such as the "Key Elements of Joint Operational Missions" and the "Key Elements of Cooperative Support Operations." It seeks near-realistic verification data through computer-simulated battlefield scenarios, focusing on strategic guidance and joint operational actions related to joint force preservation, joint interception operations, and territorial defense. The third phase, "Joint Force Assessment," uses annual routine major combat readiness training to verify combat capabilities line by line with live troops and equipment, thereby reviewing various joint operations. [863] Section 2 Joint Exercises Training is the source of combat power and the basis for victory. Therefore, the military must use exercises to verify the appropriateness and feasibility of combat plans

[864] The joint combat exercises of the National Armed Forces, guided by the policy of "simplifying exercise missions and deepening joint training," adopt a "joint operation" model under the principle of normal training without increasing troop strength. Scenario designs are conducted within a unified hypothetical framework, simplifying and integrating similar exercises from various branches of the armed forces. Following the procedures of basic, combined, coordinated, and joint training, the exercises clearly define training objectives and responsibilities, and strictly control missions to achieve the requirement of "integration of combat and training

[882] Section 5. Strategy Direction As technology advances, the nature of warfare is constantly evolving. To adapt to future forms of warfare, the National Armed Forces' strategic thinking and concepts must not only continuously absorb the ideas and concepts of advanced countries, but also develop strategic thinking and concepts suitable for future Taiwan and Penghu defense operations

[883] Based on this, the National Armed Forces' various military building and combat readiness work will continue to be guided by the concept of "military affairs reform," and through the "Streamlining Project" organization and force structure adjustment, it will develop towards building a "high-quality, appropriately sized, and highly combat-effective" "joint force," focusing on "strengthening the National Armed Forces' joint combat effectiveness" and "improving counter-terrorism and counter-terrorism capabilities." Furthermore, it will fully integrate and utilize limited national defense resources and training capabilities to implement basic troop training, combined training, and collaborative training, and deepen joint training. Adhering to a pragmatic attitude of "seeking excellence, practicality, rigor, and difficulty," it will comprehensively refine troop training, enhance the National Armed Forces' combat capabilities, achieve the goal of "winning with quality," and ensure national security

[884] Continue to build up the military and prepare for war, and build a national army that is "high-quality, of appropriate quantity and strong in combat". Logistics is the source of combat power. The support system for the national army's defense operations must utilize logistics organization to effectively control military and civilian resources, quickly allocate and focus them, and maximize the logistics capacity in order to achieve the goal of supporting combat operations

[885] Section 1 Logistics Policy In coordination with force structure adjustments and to meet the needs of Taiwan and Penghu defense operations, the National Armed Forces, guided by the principle of "centralized preparation and mobile support," have formulated the following objectives and implementation strategies for the joint operations logistics support system: I. Coordinating with Force Structure Adjustments and Planning the Logistics Organization System The Ministry of National Defense, referencing the logistics organization and military affairs innovation achievements of advanced countries, compared the logistical actions of the US military in the two Gulf Wars

[886] IV. Accelerate the disposal of obsolete ordnance and eliminate the safety hazards of ammunition depots. To actively dispose of obsolete ordnance of the armed forces, the Ministry of National Defense will have the Joint Logistics Command coordinate the planning and, based on the armed forces' current skills and equipment, categorize it into two groups: "those that can be disposed of independently" and "those that cannot be disposed of independently." For general obsolete ordnance that can be disposed of independently, a five-year disposal schedule will be established to control the progress of disposal. Special and dedicated waste munitions (such as white phosphorus bombs, chemical bombs, torpedoes, mines, and missiles) that are "handled by the authorities" will be handled through "outsourcing to overseas disposal." In order to strengthen the safety of storage and use, in addition to storing them in accordance with the requirements of the Ammunition Service Manual for safety distances and temperature and humidity control, inventory will be carried out in accordance with the "Army Ammunition Management Information System" and the "Mechanical, Ammunition and Explosives Management Operations" regulations. The management personnel will be strengthened to check and audit the responsibilities of management at all levels, so as to prevent the improper outflow of weapons and ammunition

[887] Section 2 Current Status of Preparations In line with the adjustment of the National Army's force structure, the logistics professional units of the three services are integrated: the supply of various food, fuel, clothing and spare parts continues, and proactive supply is implemented through a supply chain model; the preparation of various equipment, weapons and ammunition is strengthened to meet the needs of troops in peacetime and wartime; the transportation operations of the three services are coordinated, and civilian transportation capacity is effectively utilized; the National Army's operational logistics management system is actively developed to improve the effectiveness of support operations and achieve the goal of "timely, appropriate quality and appropriate quantity" support operations

[888] The current status of implementation is as follows: I. Organizational Adjustment In order to promote logistics reform, the Ministry of National Defense established the "Logistics Organizational Adjustment Project Working Group" at the end of 2003. Based on the joint logistics system, the division of logistics authority was adjusted, work objectives were defined, management mechanisms were unified, organizational levels were streamlined, and work processes were simplified

[889] National Defense Report of the Republic of China, Year 93 (VI) Using ammunition depots at various bases and light weapons destruction furnaces and demining facilities in various defense zones on outlying islands, a total of 6,690 tons of waste ammunition were disposed of in 2002 and 2003. In addition, 8,321 tons of waste ammunition (such as large missiles, bombs, torpedoes, mines, landmines and white phosphorus bombs) that the National Army was not capable of handling were entrusted to the German company ISL for disposal from December 2001 onwards, and the disposal was completed in July

[890] II) Considering the different characteristics of the related industries involved in the contracted maintenance of military equipment, the contracted maintenance of military equipment is divided into three major categories: "military aircraft", "ships" and "land equipment" and promoted separately

[891] III) In accordance with the Executive Yuan's policy, the National Armed Forces will no longer purchase civilian-use vehicles. Starting in 2003, the administrative vehicles of government agencies will be gradually replaced by leased vehicles. At the same time, the allocation standards for wheeled vehicles will be reviewed and revised to take into account the needs of national defense and preparedness

[892] Section 3 Policy Direction The reform of the National Army's logistics will be based on improving the logistics management system, clearly defining the logistics responsibilities for "army building" and "military deployment", and combining business management theories and concepts to establish a modern National Army logistics system

[893] IV. [894] The overall planning and development of the Republic of China Armed Forces' communications, electronic and information systems is based on the strategic concept of "effective deterrence and defensive defense" and the needs of joint operations, with the aim of gaining information and electronic advantages, consolidating national defense, and seizing the initiative from the enemy

[895] Section 1 Communications, Information and Electronics Policy This military development plan, which is based on "technology leadership, information and electronic advantages, joint interception, and national defense," integrates national communications and electronic infrastructure construction, effectively utilizes public and private communications and electronic resources, establishes the national armed forces' C4ISR system, the Taiwan Strait electromagnetic barrier, and information and electronic offensive and defensive capabilities, and combines joint operations forces to achieve the goal of "information and electronic advantages

[896] Its development policy is as follows: 1. Infrastructure construction must be advanced. Cybersecurity infrastructure construction is the foundation for creating "cybersecurity advantage". The military, in conjunction with the national communications infrastructure construction, and in response to the trend of cybersecurity technology integration, will establish an automated and secure switching communications network with multiple paths and multiple backups, improve the operational efficiency and security protection measures of various communications platforms, meet the needs of real-time intelligence and precise engagement, and lay the foundation for cybersecurity advantage

[897] II. Information and Command Management Must Be Advanced. By combining advanced technologies from industry, government, and academia, an informationbased and automated command and control system for the armed forces should be established. Based on the advanced network warfare concept, the "Po Sheng Project" (the communication and information system concept is shown in Figure 6-6) should be planned to link the armed forces' main sensing systems, combat weapons, and command and control centers. The "Common Tactical Picture" (CTP) should be transmitted to the desired combat units in real time and synchronously, so that the armed forces' joint command and control can have battlefield management capabilities such as "common battlefield awareness", "increased command speed", and "accelerated combat pace

[911] Section 1 Military Mobilization Military mobilization is the process of transitioning the military from a peacetime state to a wartime state. It includes ensuring sufficient military personnel and expanding the mobilization forces, as well as ensuring adequate equipment and supplies, so that the military can carry out combat missions

[912] National Defense Report of the Republic of China, 2004, Material Mobilization (I) Military Transportation Mobilization Military transportation mobilization refers to the use of various vehicles, machines, and tools that are currently in use by the civilian population to replenish the shortage of vehicles in the regular and reserve forces of the National Army, or to support tactical maneuvers and battlefield operations

[913] System Part Six Important National Defense Policies (II) Combat Readiness The regional and county (city) reserve commands are the coordinating agencies for requisition and requisition, coordinating the supply and demand of important materials and fixed facilities; the local governments are the executive agencies for requisition and requisition, responsible for the execution of the requisition and requisition of military supplies; and the various branches of the armed forces are the requisition and requisition agencies, responsible for the receipt and use of military supplies

[915] Section 2 Military Supplies Industry Mobilization Military supplies industry mobilization involves utilizing qualified civilian factories to establish a military supplies industry mobilization production and maintenance system, complete mobilization preparation work, and expand production and maintenance capabilities during wartime to support military needs
