[921] Summary of this Part I. Military discipline and security: Maintaining military discipline to build high-quality combat capabilities; establishing a military aircraft protection mechanism through confidentiality and security; and maintaining the physical and mental health of officers and soldiers through psychological counseling, with the aim of ensuring the safety of the troops and maximizing overall combat effectiveness

[922] Chapter 21 Military Discipline and Security Section 1 Military Discipline Maintenance Military discipline maintenance aims to uphold the national armed forces' policies and systems, supervise the implementation of orders, strictly enforce military discipline, prevent accidental casualties, improve leadership and control, cultivate a culture of integrity and competence, implement full-process supervision, protect the legitimate rights and interests of officers and soldiers, and promote the unity and progress of the national armed forces

[923] I. Preventive Measures (I) Improve the Guidelines and Norms for Surveillance Work 1. Revise the "Regulations for the Implementation of Military Discipline Maintenance": This regulation clearly defines the main tasks of the political warfare management unit in maintaining military discipline, adds a special chapter on "Preventive Measures", lists the "National Army Anti-Drug Work Implementation Plan" and the "Regulations for Case Review and Punishment of Major Violations and Security Dangers in the National Army", and compiles the existing laws and regulations that need to be paid attention to and cooperated with in maintaining military discipline into the relevant content, so as to facilitate the implementation of each unit and achieve the goals of standardization, specification and consistency

[924] II) Enhancing the Professional Competence of Supervisory Cadres Review the educational content of the "Special Training Program for Supervisory Cadres" and the "Supervision Group of the Political Warfare Academy Research Class", and invited experts and scholars from the Public Construction Commission of the Executive Yuan, the Fair Trade Commission, the Audit Department, the Investigation Bureau of the Ministry of Justice, the Criminal Investigation Bureau of the National Police Agency of the Ministry of the Interior, the Construction and Planning Agency of the Ministry of the Interior, and the State-owned Assets Supervision and Administration Commission of the Ministry of Finance to serve as instructors; in addition, actively participate in relevant training courses held by the Public Construction Commission of the Executive Yuan and its entrusted schools and legal entities to improve the overall competence of supervisory cadres, so as to facilitate the enforcement of discipline and the performance of duties, and achieve the goal of prevention

[925] II. Implementation Status (I) Military Discipline Education To educate officers and soldiers to abide by the law and discipline, and to ensure they understand that military discipline requirements are for the protection of their personal future and the enhancement of the overall honor of the armed forces, various types of military discipline programs have been planned and broadcast during the Chou Kuang Day school hours. The "Military Discipline Column" in the Youth Daily newspaper is also used as a key point and educational content for maintaining military discipline in each unit, requiring all levels to consistently implement this education. Units utilize new recruit training, pre-departure training, military (law) and discipline education, and opportunity training to consistently conduct military discipline education for officers and soldiers. To implement the government procurement policy and management system for professional responsibilities and professional certifications, we jointly organized training and assessment sessions on the Procurement Act with the Public Construction Commission of the Executive Yuan

[926] II) Implementing a "Questionnaire Survey of Grassroots Officers and Soldiers" In order to gain a deeper understanding of the actual situation of the troops, a "questionnaire survey of grassroots officers and soldiers" will be conducted annually in various combat zones on the main island and outlying islands. This survey will be conducted to gain a real understanding of the current situation of the discipline and management of officers and soldiers at all levels, as well as to collect opinions from officers and soldiers. Based on this, the survey will be reviewed and improved, with the aim of establishing a normal, rational, harmonious and law-abiding military atmosphere and effectively eradicating violations of laws and regulations

[927] III) Ensuring Smooth Complaint Channels The Ministry of National Defense has set up a joint service center with a 0800 complaint hotline (0800534780-5, 0800534880-5, a total of 12 lines), which greatly improves the efficiency of handling complaints and ensures "case-by-case control and effective handling". In addition, the center also provides consultation services for officers and soldiers, effectively eliminating their doubts

[928] IV) Prevention of fraud cases In view of the frequent occurrence of fraud cases, the Ministry of National Defense regularly convenes special meetings on "Preventing officers and soldiers from being improperly recommended for investment and consumption". The political warfare commanders at the brigade (colonel) level personally supervise, guide and assist the defrauded officers and soldiers, and the major general director establishes a case for management to prevent the development of sequel. In addition, it assists in requesting consumer protection agencies to fight for their rights. If it is necessary to deal with the matter through legal channels, the local military courts of the Ministry of National Defense can provide legal consultation and litigation guidance

[929] V) Military Discipline Assessment: Every six months, reviews will be conducted on "Military Discipline and Safety" and "Prevention of Casualties" to verify the effectiveness of military discipline work at all levels. A system of substantial rewards and quick incentives will be adopted to reward and punish officers at all levels, clarify the responsibilities of officers, improve leadership and command, stimulate a strong sense of honor, responsibility and command ethics among officers at all levels, rectify the atmosphere in the military, and reduce the occurrence of violations of discipline, crimes and casualties

[930] vi) Auditing Aspects: Establish a corruption prevention team: For major investment projects, a "corruption prevention task force" will be formed, which will include the supervision, security, constitutional investigation and other relevant departments, coordinate with the investigative units to participate in supervision and investigation, and select relevant scholars, experts and legal advisors to serve as consultants, providing consultation and review opinions based on their professional views and practical experience, so as to prevent corruption at the beginning

[931] III. Policy Directions (I) Implementing the Rule of Law Continue to review outdated laws and regulations, improve various laws and systems; implement the rule of law, establish the spirit of the rule of law; ensure that all officers and soldiers of the national army are loyal to their duties and courageous in taking on responsibilities, and build a professional and legalized national army

[932] II) Cultivate good conduct, improve military (law) education, implement preventive measures, eliminate dangerous factors, improve leadership and control, cultivate good conduct, eliminate violations of discipline and law, promote unity and progress in the military, and build a high-quality national army

[933] III) Upholding the dignity of the armed forces. With a proactive and service-oriented spirit, we will improve governance efficiency, protect the legitimate rights and interests of officers, soldiers and their families, establish a fair, united and harmonious working environment, and thereby gain the recognition and support of the people, so as to achieve the ultimate goal of upholding the dignity of the armed forces

[934] Section 2 Security and Secrecy In response to the current enemy threats and the rapid development of information technology, and in conjunction with the secrecy requirements for military buildup and combat readiness, a security and secrecy mechanism for the armed forces will be constructed, targeting the key security protection areas of all levels of the armed forces

[935] Revision and Promulgation of the "Guidelines for Classification of Military Secrets and National Defense Secrets" On October 27, 2003, the "Guidelines for Classification of Military Secrets and National Defense Secrets" were revised and promulgated. The revised guidelines were reviewed and approved by the National Defense Committee of the Legislative Yuan for filing. In order to smoothly align with the "National Secrets Protection Act" and clearly define the classification of military secrets and national defense secrets, the Ministry of National Defense will continue to educate and promote the guidelines so that military personnel are familiar with them and the definition of national defense secrets becomes clearer

[936] II) Improve the National Defense Classified Information Review and Approval Procedures. In order to establish the National Armed Forces' classified information maintenance procedures, implement the requirements of administration in accordance with the law, complete the promulgation of the "Key Points for the Review and Approval of National Defense Classified Information," explain the legal basis and purpose, establish the definitions and distinctions of "national secrets," "national defense secrets," and "general official secrets," and further clarify the legal authority and responsibility for approving the classification level and the classification procedures, supervise and implement them, so as to improve the classified information review and approval procedures

[937] In order to establish a system for the protection of state secrets by all levels of the armed forces, and in conjunction with the organizational adjustments of the Ministry of National Defense and the actual needs of the armed forces at all levels for military buildup and combat readiness, and in accordance with the “National Secrets Protection Act” implemented on October 1, 2003, the administrative rules of the “Regulations for the Implementation of Secrecy in the Armed Forces” will be revised to make the legal system for secrecy in the armed forces more complete

[938] iv) Promote the “National Armed Forces Personnel Confidentiality Agreement” In order to simplify the confidentiality agreement practice and make the signing of confidentiality agreements by national armed forces personnel more legally binding, the “National Armed Forces Personnel Confidentiality Agreement” was formulated with reference to the US “Confidential Information Agreement” practice. It incorporates the laws, regulations and penalties that national armed forces personnel should abide by, and replaces the various existing confidentiality agreements to clarify confidentiality responsibilities

[939] V) Laying the legal foundation for security investigations. In accordance with Article 32 of the National Defense Act, which stipulates that "personnel engaged in or participating in national defense and security affairs shall be subject to security investigations", the "Regulations for Security Investigations of Personnel Engaged in or Participating in National Defense and Security Affairs" were formulated and implemented simultaneously with the two national defense laws. This signifies that the "security investigation work" of the armed forces has officially entered the legal system. The implementation of these regulations can also effectively prevent enemy infiltration channels and ensure the purity and safety of members within the armed forces

[940] vi) Establishment of the Ministry of National Defense’s “Joint Reconnaissance and Defense Center” In view of the increasingly active infiltration of the CCP into our country, in order to implement the work priorities of “investigating enemy spies, preventing defection to the enemy, and maintaining military aircraft”, the “Joint Reconnaissance and Defense Center” was established in a task-based grouping manner. It will take proactive reconnaissance and defense measures such as “expanding the investigation of clues” and “strict case control” in order to improve the efficiency of case investigation, play the role of preventing enemy infiltration, and thus eliminate hidden enemy spies

[941] I) Operation of the “Committee for Reviewing Leaked Confidential Information of the Ministry of National Defense” In accordance with Article 20 of the “Organization Act of the Ministry of National Defense”, the “Committee for Reviewing Leaked Confidential Information of the Ministry of National Defense” was established on May 1, 2001. Members of the Legislative Yuan’s National Defense Committee and impartial persons with legal and journalistic expertise were invited to participate in the review of national defense secrets. Since its establishment, the committee has completed 8 cases involving 14 cases submitted by the military, judicial and investigative authorities for identification. The identification conclusions have been adopted by the identification authorities, effectively establishing the credibility and objectivity of the committee

[942] II) Enhancing the professional competence of security and defense cadres Since 2003, a three-stage core security and defense curriculum plan has been completed, consisting of "basic, advanced and intensive training", effectively achieving the goal of "training for use and integrating training with application

[943] Furthermore, counterintelligence education from various military academies, as outlined in the 2004 National Defense Report of the Republic of China, was incorporated into the National Armed Forces' education and training program. Supplementary teaching materials, such as "National Armed Forces Personnel's Awareness of the 'National Secrets Protection Act'" and "Enemy's Proneness to Exploitation, Being Prepared is Better Than Never," were compiled according to different stages to ensure that trainees are familiar with the laws and regulations governing defense work and the essentials of their implementation, thereby achieving the desired educational effect

[944] III) Strengthening Security Education for Officers, Soldiers, and the Public: In order to expose enemy infiltration and espionage plots and raise the security awareness of officers and soldiers, the Ministry of National Defense has adopted innovative concepts and focused on the education theme of "counter-intelligence, counter-sabotage, counter-infiltration, and counter-subversion." It has adopted lively and diverse educational formats such as drama, cartoons, and puppet shows, and utilized opportunities such as Chou Kuang-day teaching, military and non-commissioned officer training, and one-minute security education sessions. Through television, radio, print media, newspapers and magazines, and information networks, it has continuously educated officers and soldiers to build consensus and work together to raise their security awareness

[945] III. Policy Directions (I) Improve Secrecy Laws and Regulations Adhering to the policy concept of "legalization, professionalization, functionalization and informatization", and in response to the "Ten-Year Military Building Plan" and related legal reforms such as the "National Secrets Protection Act", we will continue to revise secrecy laws and administrative rules, establish comprehensive work norms, combine the legal aspect with the implementation aspect, and implement the practical work of military secrets

[946] II) Strengthening the Security Mechanism In response to the CCP's aggressive attacks on our highly sensitive units, in order to ensure the security of national defense classified information, we will continue to promote the "National Armed Forces' Enhanced Security Maintenance Mechanism Plan". With countermeasures and protection functions as the main focus, and based on the adjustment of systems and regulations, we will reconstruct and strengthen the National Armed Forces' security maintenance mechanism. Through comprehensive security investigations and various preventive measures, we will prioritize intelligence, research and development, and sensitive units, divide them into stages, and fully promote the plan according to the schedule to effectively discover and combat any possible leaks of secrets

[947] III) Lively and Diverse Security Education To enhance the security awareness of officers and soldiers, we continue to focus on "detecting enemy spies, preventing defection, and maintaining military aircraft" in our education and training. We compile teaching materials and supplementary materials (CDs) and incorporate them into the annual education and training. In addition, we combine current enemy threats, actual cases, and the policies and programs we promote with advertising and marketing methods. We produce and broadcast special programs or issue special materials in a lively and diverse manner to promote our work philosophy, deepen the implementation of policies, strengthen the security awareness of officers and soldiers, and prevent enemy infiltration and espionage plots

[948] IV) Establishing a Security Certification System. To assess the current situation regarding enemy and foreign forces' infiltration of sensitive units such as senior military commands, research institutions, and intelligence agencies, and to completely prevent military intelligence leaks and ensure the loyalty of military personnel, the Ministry of National Defense, referencing Taiwan's special audit system for civil servants and the U.S. loyalty investigation system, is proposing to establish a "security certification" mechanism for the armed forces. This mechanism aims to establish a system for key military positions and senior personnel through a process of self-reporting, in-depth investigation, and certification. The strict threshold for appointment to sensitive positions, which prohibits unqualified personnel from being assigned, has been established and piloted by designated units. The results have met expectations. Based on the protection of people's rights and in line with the administrative organs' intention to administer according to law, this case has now been handed over to the relevant departments such as the legal system and has entered the legislative amendment stage. Once the security certification system is established, the loyalty of personnel will ensure that national defense secrets can be better protected

[949] Section 3 Psychological Counseling The goal of the military's psychological counseling work is to use professional counseling to help officers and soldiers adapt to military life, solve psychological problems, improve their adaptability, prevent self-harm incidents, and at the same time strengthen officers' psychological counseling knowledge and skills, promote mental health concepts, promote the physical and mental health of officers and soldiers, and maintain the overall combat effectiveness of the military through diversified educational measures

[950] Currently, there are a total of 229 mental health centers at all levels (including 7 regional mental health centers), with 281 counselors (241 counselors and 40 recruited professional counselors from the private sector). These centers provide counseling and referral services in 12 areas, including emotional imbalance, life adjustment, interpersonal relationships, emotional problems, family and marriage, self-harm, career planning, addiction, mental illness, physical health, behavioral deviations, and other psychological issues. They also offer services such as psychological testing, mental health education, group counseling, and book (video) lending

[951] II. Implementation Results (I) Improvement of Plans To clarify the policy guidance for mental health work, five plan regulations were revised and promulgated, including the "Implementation Plan for Mental Health (Counseling) Work of the Armed Forces", the "Implementation Regulations for Self-Injury Prevention of Armed Forces Officers and Soldiers", the "Operational Regulations for the Application of Psychological Assessment Work of the Armed Forces", the "Implementation Regulations for Supporting Counseling Networks for Unstaffed Mental Health Personnel and Remote Units by Mental Health Centers at All Levels of the Armed Forces", and the "Operational Regulations for the Selection, Training, Use and Experience Management of Mental Health Counselors of the Armed Forces". The "Guidelines for Mental Health (Counseling) Work of the Armed Forces" were also compiled and distributed to units at the company level and above for use, so that mental health work at all levels has guidelines to follow

[952] II) Conducting Special Research Projects In order to understand the behavioral characteristics of officers and soldiers in military situations and strengthen the theoretical basis for psychological counseling practice, in 2002, experts and scholars were invited to conduct four special research projects: "A Study on the Prevention and Handling of Suicide Cases by Military Psychological Counselors", "An Analysis of Relevant Psychological and Social Factors of Military Criminals", "An Evaluation of the Effectiveness of Emotional Intelligence Scale and Emotional Management Courses", and "A Study on the Professional Attitudes of Volunteer Noncommissioned Officers in Taiwan - A Sketch of the Military under Social Change". The research findings and recommendations have been incorporated into important references for the formulation of military psychological counseling policies

[953] III) Enhancing Prevention Mechanisms To effectively leverage the proactive function of prevention mechanisms, the "Interpersonal, Thinking, Emotion, and Behavior Scales and Checklists" and the "Lai's Personality Test" were developed and completed for norm analysis of officers at the rank of colonel and lieutenant and military academy students, making the psychological testing tools of the armed forces more comprehensive

[954] iv) Promoting Professional Supervision In order to help cadre counselors at all levels improve their clinical sensitivity, enhance their ability to analyze and diagnose cases, and solve practical obstacles, a "professional supervision" system has been fully implemented since 2002. Twenty-six experts and scholars have been hired to serve as professional supervisors in different regions. Every month, they convene cadre counselors through case discussions and structured group supervision to provide professional guidance and two-way communication, so as to improve their skills in accurately assessing and counseling cases

[955] V) Strengthening Educational Measures To cultivate psychological counseling cadres and improve their professional competence and service quality, from July 2002 to November 2004, two training workshops for psychological counseling cadres were planned and conducted, with 20 sessions of inservice training for counselors. In addition, to ensure the continuity of psychological counseling education, a "Psychological Counseling Professional Group" was established in January 2003 in the regular and graduate classes of the Political Warfare Academy to cultivate the psychological and counseling professional competence of political war cadres. At the same time, psychological counseling-related issues were continuously incorporated into the psychological counseling training courses for new, in-service, and grassroots cadres to improve the prevention and control skills of cadres at all levels

[956] vi) Assisting in major disasters To prevent post-traumatic stress disorder among the rescue personnel of the "525 China Airlines crash", five "Spiritual Journey" activities were held in Penghu, Zuoying and Kenting from May to August 2002. 450 front-line rescue personnel were trained. The activities, including special lectures, group counseling, religious counseling and leisure travel, effectively relieved the stress of rescue

[957] National Defense Report of the Republic of China, 2004 (VII) Prevention of Self-Injury In order to strengthen self-injury prevention work and effectively prevent dangerous incidents, in addition to promulgating the "Regulations for the Implementation of Self-Injury Prevention by Military Personnel", which details the scale testing, case transfer procedures and prevention work, a new version of the "Military Self-Injury Prevention Manual" was compiled and distributed to officers at the company level and above to enhance the ability to grasp and coordinate dangerous signs

[958] viii) Implementing practical work "counseling and guidance" is the focus of psychological counseling work. In order to discover cases and understand the adaptation status of officers and soldiers, from July 2002 to November 2004, the entire army conducted psychological tests on 446,383 people, which served as an important reference for case screening at all levels

[959] III. Policy Directions (I) Improve Work Processes To improve the psychological counseling work of the armed forces, starting in July 2003, a wide-ranging survey was conducted on the work items, content, execution steps, and skills required for execution by personnel at the first, second, and third levels of the defense command. After the data collection is completed, experts and scholars will be invited to conduct a "job analysis" project to establish a "Standard Operating Manual for Practical Psychological Counseling in the Armed Forces," so as to standardize the work processes at all levels and conform to scientific and objective principles

[960] II) Establishment of a Management System To improve the counseling gap caused by the paper-based transmission of counseling data, we have begun to establish the "National Armed Forces Psychological Counseling Case Management System". Combined with the existing "National Armed Forces Psychological Counseling and Testing Computer Statistics System", the system will digitize the establishment, storage and transfer of case data, thereby enhancing work efficiency, simplifying the operation process and reducing management costs. It will also effectively grasp the status of counseling cases and reduce the occurrence of dangerous incidents

[961] III) Integrate social resources. Continue to cooperate with private scholars, experts and professional institutions, and establish strategic alliances to integrate the support network of mental health centers at all levels of the armed forces and social resources. Combine the pulse of the times and the characteristics of the armed forces to strengthen the existing "regional counseling network" so that it can have both case counseling and mental health education functions, enrich the working capacity of the three-level defense and enhance the adaptability of officers and soldiers

[962] iv) Refining the scale tool. Psychological testing is an important basis for case screening. In order to make the "National Armed Forces Mental and Physical Condition Assessment Scale" more accurate, the Ministry of National Defense has implemented refinement and revision of the scale items and validity, and conducted a "normative needs survey study" to establish norms and actual needs. After the needs survey is completed, experts and scholars will be invited to help establish norms applicable to each branch of the armed forces in order to improve the scale tool and enhance its administration function

[963] In recent years, the country's development has become more diversified, and the thoughts and values of the people have changed from the past. The officers and soldiers of the National Army all come from society. The Ministry of National Defense adheres to the guiding principles of protecting the rights and interests of military personnel, maintaining the dignity of military personnel, maintaining the combat effectiveness of the troops, and consolidating national security. In line with the reform of the national judicial system, it actively studies and revises outdated military laws and regulations

[964] Section 1 Reform of the Trial System The Military Trial Act and its Enforcement Act were amended and implemented on October 3, 1999. This is a major forward-looking reform that has indeed taken the military trial system a big step forward. In order to build a more complete military trial system, give full play to its role in safeguarding military interests, demonstrating fairness and justice, implementing the protection of military personnel's human rights, and reducing the differences between military and judicial trials, and in response to the problems that arise in the practical operation of military law enforcement agencies, the Ministry of National Defense has further proposed amendments, referring to the relevant provisions of the newly amended Criminal Procedure Act and the Judicial Police Dispatch Act, to ensure the clarity of the legal provisions

[965] In view of the fact that the Supreme Military Court is also a fact-based court and requires oral hearings, if the defendant or witness is detained by the judicial authorities in another case, in order to avoid the burden on manpower and resources and security concerns, it is stipulated that the court may also hold temporary hearings within its jurisdiction without the need for the defendant to be brought to justice

[966] National Defense Report of the Republic of China, Year 93, Section V: Military law police (officials) who are aware of a criminal suspect shall immediately begin an investigation and report the investigation results to the military prosecutor in charge

[967] Section 2 Protection of Individual Rights Soldiers are citizens in uniform, and their basic human rights should be protected by the same constitution as those of ordinary people, without any difference based on their status

[968] I. Legal Services To answer legal questions from officers, soldiers, their families, bereaved families, and employed personnel, and to protect their legitimate rights and interests, as well as to provide legal consultation services to various agencies and military units, the Ministry of National Defense, the General Headquarters of each service, the High Military Court and its branches, and the local military courts and their branches all have legal service departments that provide services such as legal consultation, contract review, and litigation representation. The Ministry of National Defense has also promulgated the "Guidelines for Legal Services in the Armed Forces" as the basis for application procedures, operational guidelines, and service scope, and has instructed the General Headquarters of each service and the military courts to widely publicize it so that everyone knows how to use it

[969] II. Litigation Assistance To ensure the legitimate rights and interests of the Ministry of National Defense, military personnel, and their families, boost morale, and stabilize the military, 32 lawyers have been appointed in various counties and cities on the main island of Taiwan, as well as Kinmen and Penghu, to assist the various units under the Ministry of National Defense in handling administrative litigation cases. They also provide services such as mediation, settlement, drafting of legal documents, and court defense for military personnel and their families in civil and criminal cases. Furthermore, the "Guidelines for Litigation Assistance by the Armed Forces" have been promulgated as a guideline for handling application and case transfer procedures. The General Headquarters of each service and military court are responsible for effectively implementing and promoting these guidelines and ensuring their proper use

[970] III. Protection of Rights and Interests Part VII. Key National Defense Policies (II) Security Services To protect the legitimate rights and interests of officers and soldiers, the Ministry of National Defense and all services (general

[971] Section 3. Policy Direction Starting on July 9, 1994, the Ministry of National Defense convened a "Military Trial Law Research and Revision Committee" with representatives from the three armed forces, experts, scholars, the Judicial Yuan, and the Ministry of Justice to conduct a comprehensive review of the "Military Trial Law." Subsequently, on October 3, 1997, the Grand Justices of the Judicial Yuan issued Interpretation No. 436, recognizing that the military courts exercised a form of state criminal punishment power and declaring that some articles of the old "Military Trial Law" were inconsistent with the constitutional intent. Based on this interpretation, the Ministry of National Defense made necessary revisions to ensure that military trials conformed to the trends of the times and achieved the dual purpose of protecting the rights and interests of military personnel

[972] In the future, the Ministry of National Defense will continue to uphold the principles of strict military discipline, consolidating leadership and control, and enhancing the combat capabilities of the armed forces. It will continue to promote the revision of military laws and regulations, and strengthen the various functions of the Committee for the Protection of the Rights and Interests of Military Personnel, in order to implement the policy of taking care of military personnel

[973] I. Military Trials: The Military Trials Act was completely amended on October 2, 1999, and partially amended twice on January 30, 2002 and June 11, 2003. Since its implementation, there have been no major obstacles. However, in order to improve the organization, the organizational structure of the Military Prosecutors' Court adopts a three-level, two-instance system, and its jurisdiction is divided by level. Further adjustments are needed

[974] II. Protection of Rights and Interests In order to ensure that the rights and interests of officers and soldiers are fully protected, in addition to continuing to provide legal services and counseling in litigation, the Ministry of National Defense has formulated the following directions for future improvement: (I) Expand the selection scope of publicly hired lawyers through fair and open procurement procedures, so as to improve their quality and service quality and safeguard the rights and interests of officers, soldiers and their families

[975] In its operations, the committee strives to avoid interference from the administrative system, ensuring that officers and soldiers firmly believe that the Committee for the Protection of the Rights and Interests of the Armed Forces is the last line of defense for protecting the rights and interests of the armed forces; it also adheres to the principle that "no case is too small, and every case has a result" in order to achieve the goal of "improving efficiency and shortening time"; and it ensures that the authority and prestige are established within the legal scope, so as to make the protection of the rights and interests of officers and soldiers more complete

[976] National Defense Report of the Republic of China, 2004, Chapter 23 Welfare Services, Part 7 Important National Defense Policies (II) Security Services Strengthening welfare services for officers and soldiers of the armed forces is a consistent belief and goal of the Ministry of National Defense. It mainly relies on military insurance, medical services, reconstruction of military dependents' villages and life care to actively create a good working environment, strengthen care for officers, soldiers and their families, and achieve the purpose of stabilizing the morale of the military and consolidating the hearts of their families

[977] Section 1 Insurance Since its inception, military insurance has not only effectively stabilized the morale of the military and protected the livelihoods of their families, but also provided a solid foundation for various social insurance programs offered by the government

[978] I. Improve the military personnel insurance system. The Ministry of National Defense provides necessary insurance services to active-duty officers and soldiers of the armed forces, students of military academies or platoons, and reservists who are conscripted for short-term service, in accordance with the "Military Personnel Insurance Act" and other relevant laws and regulations

[979] II. Providing Group Accident Insurance for Armed Forces Officers, Air Crew, Ordnance Technicians, and Unexploded ordnance Disposal Personnel: In view of the previous low payouts for military personnel insurance in the event of injury or death, the Ministry of National Defense has been providing group insurance for armed forces officers and soldiers, as well as group accident insurance for personnel performing flight and ordnance disposal missions, since July 1, 1998, in order to effectively care for armed forces officers and soldiers and their families

[980] The principle of "low premiums and high coverage" will continue to be followed, with budgets allocated and implemented annually, and the insured amount for those killed or injured on duty will be increased as appropriate to acknowledge their contributions to the country and achieve the goal of providing peace of mind in life and comfort in death

[981] Inclusion in National Health Insurance: Armed forces personnel have been enrolled in the National Health Insurance as Category 1 since February 1, 2001; dependents, destitute military dependents, conscripted military personnel, and military academy students enrolled under the fourth category are enrolled as Category 4. This allows all personnel to receive medical services provided by the Armed Forces Medical System, as well as to choose to seek medical treatment at public and private medical institutions at all levels throughout the country, and enjoy the same high-quality medical care from the government

[982] Section 2 Medical Care After military personnel were included in the National Health Insurance, the choice and convenience of medical treatment for officers and soldiers were improved. However, in order to ensure that officers and soldiers continue to enjoy care without having to pay medical expenses when they go to military hospitals, the Ministry of National Defense revised the "Regulations for the Implementation of Preferential Treatment (Reduction and Exemption) for Medical Treatment in Military Medical Institutions". After its implementation, it is shown that officers and soldiers still mainly choose to go to military hospitals to receive medical services

[983] I. Medical Care (I) For military personnel who are hospitalized in military hospitals for more than the reasonable hospitalization period covered by health insurance and whose condition has not improved, and who are confirmed by doctors to still require continued medical care, military hospitals have military convalescent beds and continuously provide proper medical care and a rest and rehabilitation environment to ensure that military personnel receive timely and appropriate medical services

[984] III) In order to implement medical services for officers and soldiers, all military hospitals have specially set up windows for military personnel to serve officers and soldiers seeking medical treatment, so that officers and soldiers can obtain fast and convenient priority medical services when seeking medical treatment

[985] II. First Aid Training Considering the reduction in the number of personnel in the armed forces and the changes in the missions and characteristics of the troops, the Ministry of National Defense has been actively promoting self-rescue and mutual aid training and first aid skills training for grassroots troops in recent years, in order to improve the medical quality of the armed forces and strengthen their emergency medical care and casualty evacuation capabilities in peacetime and wartime

[986] III. Military Medicine The structure, performance, and power of modern aircraft and submarines are constantly evolving, often exceeding the limits of human physiological adaptation. Factors such as high and low air pressure and temperature changes, acceleration, spatial illusions, noise, and vibration make aviation and submarine medicine a unique branch of military medicine within the national defense system. It is crucial for enhancing the frontline combat capabilities of the armed forces and has become a key focus of medical research units within the armed forces

[987] I) Strengthening Air Force Security and Aviation Physiological Training Equipment: On July 1, 1993, the Gangshan Hospital of the Republic of China Armed Forces completed the construction of various aviation physiological training equipment, including "ejection training chairs", "spatial disorientation training aircraft" and "night vision/night vision goggles training systems". It also merged with the existing "human centrifuge", "high-pressure cabin" and "low-pressure cabin" equipment to build a complete aviation physiological training center for the Republic of China Armed Forces, laying the foundation for pilot flight training

[988] II) Promote the Navy's "Submarine Medicine" Project Team: Incorporate personnel from the Navy Headquarters Medical Group, the Institute of Submarine Medicine of the National Defense Medical Center, Zuoying Armed Forces Hospital, Penghu Armed Forces Hospital, and related units to establish the Ministry of National Defense's "Submarine Medicine" Project Team, and define the task force and responsibilities

[989] IV. Medical Treatment of Biological Poisons The National Armed Forces, in coordination with the Executive Yuan's biological poison disaster prevention and relief measures, will conduct localized detection and prevention operations to prevent malicious or terrorist individuals from spreading biological viruses in urban schools, hospitals, water sources, military camps, or places where people gather, causing significant casualties

[990] V. Policy Direction (I) Continuously strive for budget and improve the quality of medical care for officers and soldiers The military medical system has an unshirkable responsibility for the health care of officers and soldiers of the armed forces

[991] The National Army will continue to strive for budget allocation, provide medical benefits to officers and soldiers, and improve medical software, hardware facilities and equipment to enhance medical quality and care capacity. (II) Implement the regional medical responsibility system to meet the medical needs of officers and soldiers. With the general hospitals of each theater command as the regional medical responsibility center, a regional small strategic alliance system will be constructed so that medical resources, management strategies and information networks within the system can support each other. Then, with the Tri-Service General Hospital as the center, alliances will be formed with each region (theater command) to construct a complete strategic alliance of National Army medical institutions

[992] III) Strengthen military medical education and training to ensure the combat capability of the armed forces. In order to ensure the operational capabilities of pilots and meet the needs of the new generation of fighter jets, the Ministry of National Defense will continue to review the current military aviation medical curriculum and various aviation medical policies, improve various training equipment and strengthen pilot preventive medicine, and provide appropriate flight health services to enhance flight safety and maintain combat capability

[993] iv) Cultivate officers' and soldiers' knowledge of biological protection and enhance their ability to handle biological poisons. Based on the goal of improving the military's biological poison prevention and medical treatment capabilities, we will continue to strengthen the biological protection and medical treatment capabilities of military hospitals and medical support units, continue to conduct biological protection education and training courses for military medical personnel, train seed instructors, and actively develop and raise funds for related drugs, medical supplies and vaccines to ensure the effectiveness of biological protection medical operations

[994] Section 3 Redevelopment of Military Dependents' Villages The redevelopment of old military dependents' villages mainly aims to improve the living environment and quality of the dilapidated villages, increase the economic efficiency of land use, increase tax revenue and resolve land disputes, thereby stimulating economic recovery, providing employment opportunities, assisting local governments in acquiring land for public facilities, beautifying the urban landscape, and creating a "win-win-win" situation for the government, the public, and military dependents

[995] I. Policy The reconstruction of old military dependents' villages has been carried out since 1970, and is divided into two phases: "old system" and "new system

[996] II. Implementation Results (I) Reconstruction of Old Military Dependents' Villages Before February 5, 1996, the Executive Yuan approved the reconstruction of 78 military dependents' villages, which have been completed (relocated

[997] Part 7 Important National Defense Policies (II) Security Services (II) Reconstruction of New Military Dependents' Villages Reconstruction Plan: In November 1996, the Executive Yuan approved 161 reconstruction sites, which were revised and adjusted to 88 sites in August 2004 (53 reconstruction sites and 35 relocation and purchase sites for state and military dependents' homes

[998] Fund Planning (1) Fund Income: Assets (including fund interest and disposal of land and residential properties, etc.) are estimated at over NT$415.9 billion, renovation expenditures are estimated at over NT$268.4 billion, and the estimated surplus is NT$147.5 billion. In addition, NT$73.2 billion of land revolving funds that are not suitable for operation must be returned. The actual fund surplus is estimated at approximately NT$74.3 billion

[999] III) Housing Loans and Housing Allocation for Military Personnel To better support the families of volunteer military personnel, the annual (period) housing purchase program for active-duty military personnel will be expanded based on the number of applicants with dependents but no housing and the availability of funds in each unit. In accordance with the "Standard Interest Rate for Housing Loans Subsidized by the Central Government Civil Servants," the annual interest rate for housing loans from the National Armed Forces Fund will be reduced to 2%

[1000] iv) Service measures for the reconstruction of old military dependents' villages: In 2002-2003, 453 telephone applications and 582 written applications (including email applications) were received. There were 54 information sessions and 87 coordination public hearings sessions, totaling 1,176 cases (times). All applications were registered and controlled, and responses were provided either orally or in writing

[1001] III. Policy Directions (I) Reconstruction of Military Dependents' Villages (New and Old Systems) For reconstruction sites where plans have been completed, expedite the selection of architects and the application for building permits; for sites under construction, control the progress and quality of the project, prevent corruption, and ensure timely and high-quality completion and handover of the houses

[1002] II) Expanding Housing Loans for Military Personnel After the completion of the housing allocation for remaining households in the military dependents' village redevelopment in 2004, in order to continue to take care of the housing needs of active duty military personnel, a proposal was submitted to the Executive Yuan to adopt a method that does not distinguish between official ranks, increases the number of applicants, provides a loan amount of NT$2.2 million per household, and reduces the interest rate (currently 2.125%) to process housing loans for military personnel, so as to achieve the goal of everyone having a place to live

[1003] Section 4. Daily Care I. Work Content To implement the "Three-Safety Policy," the Ministry of National Defense is constantly striving to improve the quality of life of officers and soldiers and create a good working environment, so as to strengthen the care for officers, soldiers and their families, and achieve the goal of stabilizing morale and consolidating family bonds

[1004] II. Implementation Results (I) Legalization of Welfare In order to improve the welfare of military personnel and stabilize their families, the draft of the "Military Personnel Welfare Act" was prepared in accordance with the "National Defense Act". The aim is to establish an operational mechanism for the welfare affairs of the armed forces through the legal system, so that the welfare matters that military personnel should enjoy can be legally guaranteed

[1005] II) Supply of Non-staple Foods Providing good meals is a concrete measure to meet the living needs of officers and soldiers. The National Army currently has 28 non-staple food supply stations, supplying 1,436 kinds of non-staple foods at prices that are on average 20% lower than the market price. In addition, in order to improve the cooking skills of the food service personnel, the National Army has produced a non-staple food recipe book for the troops to use, in order to improve the quality of group meals and meet the dietary needs of officers and soldiers

[1006] III) Comfort and Condolence In order to strengthen the care of officers and soldiers and proactively solve urgent and difficult problems, the requirements of "comprehensive care and key assistance" were adopted. For officers and soldiers who died in war or on duty, those involved in major accidents, and those who were hospitalized or went to the hospital for treatment due to injury or illness, emergency assistance was provided. The urgent difficulties of officers and soldiers were proactively identified, and the assistance was maximized to boost the morale of officers and soldiers and strengthen the cohesion of the troops

[1007] iv) Facility Improvement To meet the needs of the e-generation officers and soldiers, implement life care, promote proper leisure and entertainment, cultivate a reading atmosphere, improve the living facilities of the troops, boost the morale of officers and soldiers, and enhance their spiritual combat effectiveness, the Ministry of National Defense conducts an annual questionnaire survey and market survey of officers and soldiers. In line with the national defense policy plan, it moves towards digital development and purchases multi-functional propaganda audio-visual (recreation) equipment that combines audio-visual, video, and digital technology. Combined with the establishment of multi-functional recreation centers and recreation information stations in the military camps, it provides officers and soldiers with lively and diverse leisure options such as birthday banquets, military song teaching, and team building activities. In addition to effectively implementing political education, it also meets the audio-visual entertainment needs of officers and soldiers

[1008] Part 7 Important National Defense Policies (II) Security Services (V) Officers' Residences In order to implement the "Three Security Policies" and take care of the living welfare of officers and soldiers and their families, so that volunteer officers and soldiers can take care of their families nearby and concentrate on their work, thus stabilizing the morale of the military, the National Army has set up 81 designated officers' residences with 6,165 units in various counties and cities for volunteer officers and soldiers in the county or city where they are stationed to apply for accommodation if they have families but no housing. In the future, designated officers' residences will be gradually built in conjunction with the renovation of old military dependents' villages, donations from the Women's Federation, and camp renovation

[1009] vi) Supply of welfare products The welfare services of the armed forces meet the needs of officers and soldiers through measures such as "self-sufficiency", "price based on quantity", "price discounts" and "profit feedback

[1010] vii) Childcare To enhance services and care for the families and dependents of military personnel, the Ministry of National Defense issued the "Regulations for the Military to Promote the Establishment of Childcare Facilities or the Provision of Childcare Measures." Through contractual agreements, it has signed cooperation agreements with 391 legal, reputable, and registered childcare institutions in Taiwan to assist military dependents in solving childcare problems on preferential terms, so that officers and soldiers can have peace of mind

[1011] III. Policy Direction National Defense Report of the Republic of China in 2004 (I) Constructing a Welfare System Continue to promote the legislative work of the "Military Personnel Welfare Act" and, in accordance with the "Military Personnel Welfare Act" draft, gradually construct the operational mechanism for the welfare affairs of the national armed forces

[1012] II) Expand service efficiency. In response to the daily life and dietary needs of officers, soldiers and their families, we will organize the introduction of "manufacturer recommended high-quality products" to simultaneously introduce popular products from the market and improve consumer welfare

[1013] III) Continuous Barracks Renovation In order to improve the quality of life of officers and soldiers, in addition to the annual budget allocated by the National Army for the renovation of old barracks in accordance with the troop building plan and the needs of missions, the existing barracks (buildings) facilities are repaired and maintained using facility maintenance funds so that the living environment of officers and soldiers reaches the standard of safety and comfort

[1014] iv) Purchase more recreational facilities. In line with the government’s e-government policy, we look ahead to the future development trends of information and recreational equipment, and combine the wartime missions of the troops with the daily needs of officers and soldiers. We continue to purchase and distribute “cultural and recreational audio-visual equipment” and books in company bookshelves. Through the Internet and information links, we provide faster and more user-friendly services, effectively improving the living facilities of the troops and enabling officers and soldiers to serve in the camp with peace of mind

[1015] Part 8 Important National Defense Policies (IV) Military-Civilian Relations Summary of this Part 1. The protection and maintenance of people's rights and interests is a crucial key to strengthening military-civilian relations. In addition to continuously adjusting military control zones and gradually reducing the scope of prohibitions and restrictions on construction, the Ministry of National Defense also fully protects the legitimate rights and interests of the people through measures such as "appeals," "compensation for military service casualties," and handling "national compensation

[1016] Based on the purpose of safeguarding people's rights, in addition to adjusting the scope of "military control and prohibition/restriction of construction", the Ministry of National Defense also fully protects people's rights through measures such as "appeals", "compensation for military service casualties" and "national compensation" in a fair and objective manner

[1017] Section 1 Military Control and Restrictions on Construction In response to changes in the domestic political and economic situation and in line with the overall development of the country, the Ministry of National Defense has timely revised the "Fortress and Fortress Zone Act" and the "Regulations on Prohibited and Restricted Construction Operations." It has gradually reviewed sites of similar nature or those that can be coexisted, and appropriately merged or incorporated them to reduce the number of controlled zones. Furthermore, the prohibition and restriction measures for construction in military controlled zones have been incorporated into national land development plans, regional plans, or urban plans to delineate military-only areas. In addition, "prohibited construction zones" have been designated as parks, green spaces, and surface parking lots to improve land use efficiency. "Restricted construction zones" use floor area ratio to control building height, replacing the control of military controlled zones, to minimize the negative impact and take into account the development of surrounding communities and the rights and interests of the people in military controlled zones

[1018] I. Review of Fortress Control Zones In recent years, in coordination with the reduction of military facilities and the replacement of weapons and equipment, the National Armed Forces have continuously and proactively reduced the scope of fortress control zones through methods such as "removal," "re-establishment and management of important military facility control zones," and "reduction." From 2001 to 2004, a total of 3,649 hectares were removed from control

[1026] I. III. Amendment to the "Fortress and Fortification Zone Act" To ensure the full utilization of national land resources, enhance the country's overall competitiveness, and safeguard the security of military airports and ports, the Ministry of National Defense, recognizing that the revised "Fortress and Fortification Zone Act" passed in April 2002 was insufficient to effectively reduce its restrictions on national land development, drafted an amendment to the "Fortress and Fortification Zone Act" in July 2003 and submitted it to the Executive Yuan for proposal. Following guidance from the Executive Yuan in November 2003, it was scheduled to be submitted again to the Executive Yuan for review by the Legislative Yuan in July 2005. Once the Legislative Yuan completes the amendment, it will better balance local and urban development, enhancing the country's overall competitiveness

[1027] Section 2 Appeals The right to appeal is a people's right expressly protected by Article 16 of the Constitution. Its ultimate purpose is to urge administrative organs to implement the rule of law and ensure the legitimate rights and interests of the people

[1028] I. Policy and Current Situation (I) The “Committee for Review of Appeals of the Ministry of National Defense” is responsible for handling administrative relief matters in national defense administration. If the people believe that the administrative actions of the agencies under the Ministry of National Defense are illegal or improper, causing damage to their rights and interests, they may file an appeal in accordance with the law. Through the appeal procedure, the legality and appropriateness of the administrative actions are reviewed to ensure the lawful exercise of public power and safeguard the rights and interests of the people

[1029] II. Implementation Results (I) From July 2002 to November 2004, a total of 480 appeals were processed; of these, 173 were rejected, 166 were not accepted according to law, 59 had their original dispositions revoked and the disposing agency made other appropriate dispositions, and 82 were transferred or withdrawn (the classification statistics of appeals are shown in Table

[1032] Section 3 Compensation for Casualties in Military Service To demonstrate the government's sincerity in caring for the people injured or killed and suffering property damage due to military service by the National Armed Forces, and to soothe the pain of the injured and their families, this section specifically addresses the compensation for casualties and property damage caused by the National Armed Forces in the performance of military service from the government's relocation to Taiwan in 1949 until the implementation of the "National Compensation Act" on July 1, 1981 (extended to November 7, 1992, the date of termination of battlefield administration in the Kinmen and Matsu areas). The "Compensation Act for Casualties Caused by Military Duties" was promulgated and implemented on December 1, 1999, with an application period of 2 years. On December 17, 2003, the Legislative Yuan passed the third reading of the "Compensation Act for Casualties Caused by Military Duties of the National Armed Forces," extending the application period by 6 years (until November 30, 2007), and it was promulgated and implemented on January

[1033] Because this regulation is retroactive and the incidents occurred more than 20 years ago, it has made it difficult for applicants to provide evidence, affecting the progress of case review. In order to implement the government's sincerity in handling compensation, the Ministry of National Defense has not only revised the "Compensation Base and Issuance Standards" but also completed the "Specific Measures for Remedial Measures for Cases Without Evidence". All cases are handled according to the principle of "leniency in recognition", so that compensation applications can be compensated as soon as possible in accordance with the law. [1037] Chapter 25 Social Services Part 8 Important National Defense Policies (V) Military-Civilian Relations National defense is an integral part of the government's overall policy, therefore the relationship between the armed forces and society is close. In terms of national defense for all citizens, the key lies in the gathering of public sentiment and the utilization of public resources. In terms of national social development, the armed forces' human and material resources, without affecting combat readiness training, should fully provide necessary support to society to minimize the damage from disasters and epidemics

[1038] I. Policy Planning Protecting the nation and its people is the sacred duty of the armed forces. The armed forces have always regarded "disaster relief" as a form of warfare, accepting guidance from the national disaster prevention and relief system. In accordance with the "Disaster Prevention and Relief Act" and the "Regulations for Requesting Armed Forces Support for Disaster Management," when a major disaster occurs in society, the armed forces will devote all their efforts to disaster relief work without affecting combat readiness, disrupting the armed forces' command system, or exceeding the armed forces' support capabilities

[1039] II. Implementation Results (I) The "National Armed Forces Joint Operations Command Center" (also known as the "National Armed Forces Crisis Management Center") operates 24 hours a day. Under the guidance of the "Executive Yuan Central Disaster Response Center", it coordinates with the operation of various disaster response mechanisms and simultaneously establishes disaster relief (typhoon prevention) command centers at all levels. It effectively utilizes manpower, transportation (machinery) and various resources to respond to various disasters at any time in order to safeguard the lives and property of the people

[1040] Republic of China National Defense Report of 2004 (II) Based on the concept of "all-round security", the National Army and the Republic of China cooperated with various ministries and agencies in 2003 to actively participate in the revision of 29 laws and regulations, including the draft amendment to the "Disaster Prevention and Relief Act", the "Regulations for Radiation Protection and Control of Military Agencies", and the "Operational Plan for Prevention and Relief of Wind, Flood, Drought, Fire, Earthquake, Forest Fire and Debris Flow Disasters

[1041] iv) The National Army is determined to treat disaster relief as combat and is currently actively developing various measures to address the situation before the regulations are completed, the organization is revised, and the equipment is acquired. The following measures are proposed: Incorporate "disaster relief" into the mission of the National Army

[1042] In line with the Executive Yuan's planned "National Security Network" operational framework, the existing "Military Service Act," "National Defense Mobilization and Preparation Act," "Executive Yuan National Search and Rescue Command Center Operational Regulations," and "Application for Article 8 Important National Defense Policies Military-Civilian Relations National Armed Forces Supporting Disaster Management Regulations" and other relevant laws and regulations will be reviewed and revised to meet the requirements of administration according to law

[1043] The headquarters' "Disaster Relief and Response Integrated Coordination Center" was transformed into a "Supervisory Group". This group is responsible for monitoring disaster relief vehicles, equipment, and logistical support capabilities at all times, and for supervising disaster relief discipline to assist each operational area in successfully supporting disaster relief efforts in various counties and cities

[1044] The National Army supports local governments in various counties (cities) in carrying out major disaster relief and assistance. (See Table 8-5 for statistics on various resources provided

[1046] Section 2 Disease Prevention and Control. The National Army prioritizes preventive measures for the safety of its officers and soldiers and the maintenance of camp hygiene in the prevention and control of infectious disease outbreaks. It also assists local governments in maintaining regional cleanliness and plays an early preventive role

[1047] I. Policy When natural disasters (wind disasters, floods, earthquakes, etc.) occur, in order to ensure the health of disaster relief officers and soldiers and people in disaster areas, the National Army will proactively form inspection teams to go to various disaster areas to assist in maintaining environmental hygiene and preventing the outbreak of diseases

[1048] II. Implementation Results (I) Established the “New Infectious Syndrome Monitoring and Reporting” system for hospitals in Songshan, Taoyuan, Taichung, Zuoying, Kaohsiung, and Hualien of the National Armed Forces to fully support the regional epidemic monitoring work

[1049] III) Confirmed cases of HIV infection among volunteer service members were registered, tracked, and provided with counseling and treatment; those who were discharged or suspended from service were registered and sent to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, and the health authorities in the case's place of residence were notified to continue tracking and management

[1050] Specific Achievements in the Prevention and Control of SARS In the first half of 2003, after the Executive Yuan established the "Executive Yuan SARS Prevention and Relief Committee", the Ministry of National Defense took charge of the National Defense Resources Division. The tasks included: providing equipment support for epidemic prevention and isolation or medical facilities, assisting in medical epidemic prevention and manpower support, monitoring the epidemic in the military, and other support tasks related to the use of national defense resources

[1051] Homeless shelters: Provided three homeless shelters, including the Taipei City Government Wugang Camp, the Taipei County Government Kengtai Camp, and the Taoyuan County Government Zhongli Qiding Camp, with a total of 268 homeless people sheltered

[1052] vii) SARS prevention and control measures during the autumn and winter flu season: Influenza vaccination: In addition to the medical staff of the National Defense Army Hospitals under the Ministry of National Defense completing the vaccination, more than 13,600 doses of vaccine were allocated in September and October of 2003 for the National Army medical units, catering and aircrew personnel to administer

[1053] In 2004, another 6,000 doses were procured and provided to the officers and soldiers of the three armed forces operating in confined spaces

[1054] viii) In response to avian influenza, officers and soldiers and camps are required to conduct body temperature measurements in accordance with regulations and continuously monitor the fever status of all units of the armed forces

[1055] Section 3 Public Security Maintenance I. Policy Planning The military police, in accordance with the "Criminal Procedure Law," the "Judicial Police Dispatch Act," and the Military Police Support Agreement, adhere to the principles of "preventing problems before they occur, eliminating threats before they materialize, quelling disorder at its inception, and stopping disorder after it recurs," assisting judicial organs in carrying out public security maintenance work, with a focus on those related to the military; and in conjunction with the police system, building a comprehensive "crisis early warning" capability in peacetime, and strengthening the foundation for "counterterrorism and counter-terrorism," in order to maintain social stability and ensure national security

[1056] II. Current Status of Implementation From July 2002 to November 2004, a total of 616 criminal cases were investigated and prosecuted, 845 criminals were arrested, 1,656 deserters were apprehended, 137 hooligans were prosecuted, 250 cases of illegal immigrants from Mainland China were investigated, 594 illegal immigrants were apprehended, 79 firearms of various types and 2,038 bullets were seized, and 279 cases of drugs were seized (1,794 grams of heroin, 2,892 grams of amphetamines, 44 grams of marijuana, 5,298 grams of ecstasy, and 2,171 grams of ketamine). 374 criminals were arrested, which has made a certain contribution to maintaining social order

[1057] Section 4 Environmental Protection In order to fulfill its environmental protection obligations and cooperate with the promotion of national environmental protection policies such as "strengthening pollution prevention and control", "maintaining the natural environment and ecology" and "environmental sustainable development", the armed forces carry out relevant environmental protection work in accordance with the various pollution prevention and control regulations promulgated by the central competent authorities

[1058] I. Current Status of Implementation (I) Waste Disposal To strengthen the cleanup and disposal of military waste, local government environmental protection agencies or qualified agents approved by the Environmental Protection Administration are legally entrusted with the work. Various types of waste are categorized, managed, outsourced for proper disposal, tracked and verified, and proactively recorded and reported to reduce pollution from waste disposal operations and ensure environmental quality. In March and October of 2003, the military assisted southern counties and cities in dengue fever prevention and control efforts, deploying 1,934 personnel, cleaning up 176 tons of garbage, eliminating mosquito breeding grounds, and maintaining environmental cleanliness

[1059] II) Water Pollution Prevention and Control In accordance with national environmental protection policies, a total of 61 wastewater treatment facilities were completed for hospitals, military factories, military ports and logistics maintenance units at all levels of the military to treat domestic and industrial wastewater in order to maintain the water quality of natural water bodies

[1060] III) Airport Noise Control In order to reduce aviation noise interference, except for emergency combat exercises, each branch of the armed forces has issued relevant regulations to control training flights and test flights, and to adopt environmentally friendly flight routes to avoid passing through urban areas as much as possible

[1061] IV) Environmental Protection Personnel Training and Promotion From July 2002 to November 2004, 97 military and noncommissioned officers were trained to obtain various environmental protection professional certificates. In addition, the Environmental Training Institute was commissioned to conduct environmental protection personnel training, with a total of 2 classes and 306 people completing the training, which effectively improved the professional quality of environmental protection personnel and implemented the environmental protection work of the armed forces

[1062] II. Policy Direction (I) Continue to cooperate with the Environmental Protection Administration to jointly promote the implementation of various national environmental protection policies in order to facilitate the sustainable use of national resources

[1063] Chapter 26 Military-Civilian Liaison Part 8 Important National Defense Policies (V) Military-Civilian Relations 289 In order to implement the transparency of national defense policies and respect the people's right to "know," the National Armed Forces, on the one hand, utilizes governmentmilitary coordination to maintain close contact with other government ministries and public opinion agencies, and on the other hand, strengthens policy explanation and two-way communication through press liaison, military-civilian exchanges, and other means, so that the people can fully understand and support national defense construction, thereby establishing a national consensus on national defense

[1064] Section 1 Political-Military Coordination The Ministry of National Defense, in accordance with the National Defense Act, is in charge of national defense affairs, formulates military strategies, and conducts political-military coordination and interaction with the Presidential Office, the Executive Yuan, the Legislative Yuan, and the Control Yuan through various meetings (reports), inspections, visits, and special reports, in order to facilitate the smooth implementation of national defense policies

[1065] I. Coordination and Implementation Results of the Presidential Office and Executive Yuan (I) Conducting military talks and submitting reports to the President on issues such as forward-looking and sustainable national defense policies, procurement and construction of major weapons and equipment systems, combat readiness training, and the formulation and revision of important laws and regulations, so as to ensure that the various military building and combat readiness work of the National Armed Forces can be carried out smoothly in coordination with the government's administration

[1066] II) Participate in Executive Yuan meetings, cooperate with the government's policies to promote overall national development, and propose national defense-related bills; and attend various bill review and special report meetings of relevant ministries such as the Council for Economic Planning and Development, the Mainland Affairs Council, the 921 Reconstruction Committee, and the Disaster Prevention and Relief Committee, to fully implement the spirit of administrative unity. (III) Handle petitions (including emails) submitted by the public to the President, the Premier, and the Minister of National Defense. From July 2002 to November 2004, there were a total of 14,379 petitions: 10,960 of which were categorized by the protection of rights and interests, accounting for 76%
